# Jagannatha-charitamrita

*"The Nectar of the Life of Jagannatha"*

*A poem by Divakara Dasa (Odia, 17th century). Chapters 1–7 and 23*

*Translated from Odia into English*

---

## Translator's note

The *Sri Jagannatha-charitamrita* (in the running heads of the edition, *Jagannathamrita-gita*) is an Odia poem by Divakara Dasa (Divakara Mishra), a poet of the 17th century. He calls himself "the brahmana Divakara", son of Janadeva and Champa, of the Bharadvaja gotra. Divakara belongs to the Odia line of Jagannatha Dasa — author of the Odia *Bhagavata* and one of the Panchasakha — and stands fifth in the succession of disciples after him. The poem is a life of Jagannatha Dasa, "Ati Bada Gosain"; Chapters 1–7 tell of him in connection with Sri Chaitanya in Puri, and Chapter 23 speaks of the glory of Jagannatha's festivals and closes the book. Only these eight chapters are translated here; Chapters 8–22 do not speak of Sri Chaitanya.

What the chapters contain. Chapter 1: the mangalacharana, the sixteen disciples of Jagannatha Dasa, the tradition of his birth "from Radha's laughter", his childhood and studies. Chapter 2: Mahaprabhu's meeting with Jagannatha Dasa reading the *Bhagavata* under the banyan at Bata-Ganesha, the displeasure of the Bengali Vaishnavas, the teaching on diksha and the guru, and Jagannatha Dasa's initiation by Balarama Dasa. Chapter 3: the name "Atibada" and the Gaudiyas' departure from Puri — to Yajapur, Vishnupur and Vrindavana. Chapters 4–7: Mahaprabhu's teachings to Sarvabhauma, Nityananda and the Vaishnavas on maha-prasada, the Name, the murti of Jagannatha and the Harinama, the account of Prataparudra, and the Lord's departure into the body of Jagannatha. Chapter 23: the glory of beholding the Snana-yatra, the chariot and Gundicha, and the glory of Purushottama-kshetra.

**Source.** The edition: Cuttack, 1927 (corrected by Pandit Janardan Kar), from the archive.org scan; the text was read line by line from the page images. **Only Divakara's text** has been translated; the 1963 edition and modern translations were not used.

**How the translation was made.** There was no native speaker of Odia among the translators. Every couplet was read from the scan, transliterated and translated word for word (this is in the full edition, to be published separately); the literary translation rests on that work. Doubtful places are marked "(?)", unclear ones "(??)"; words added for the sense are in square brackets; Sanskrit shlokas are marked "[Skt.]". Questions for an Odia specialist are collected separately.

**Numbering.** The couplets are not numbered in the edition; the "chapter.verse" numbers are the translators', and so are the section headings.

**Divakara and the Gaudiya Vaishnavas.** The line of Jagannatha Dasa ("ativadi", "atibadi") is counted by the Gaudiya Vaishnava acharyas, Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati among them, among the apasampradayas — currents that have departed from Sri Chaitanya's teaching. Divakara consistently exalts Jagannatha Dasa: he is "born from Radha's laughter", the Lord calls him "Atibada" and holds him above His Bengali associates; the murti of Jagannatha is the source of all avatars, Krishna above Krishna; the mahamantra is in the Odia order "Hare Rama Krishna"; Mahaprabhu's departure is His entering the body of Jagannatha. Neither the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* nor the *Chaitanya-bhagavata* mentions Jagannatha Dasa. The translation renders the author exactly, softening nothing; at each such place a note briefly gives the Gaudiya Vaishnava position according to Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati: Sri Chaitanya is Krishna Himself, who has accepted Radha's mood and radiance; Jagannatha is Krishna Himself; His body is sat-cit-ananda, and His departure is a disappearance by His own will. The book is valuable as a testimony of how Sri Chaitanya lived on in the memory of the Odia Vaishnavas of the 17th century and as a source of Odia traditions about Puri — not as an authority in theology.

---

## Contents

- Chapter 1. Introduction: Obeisances to the Guru, the Vaishnavas and the Gods; Ati Bada Gosain and the Sixteen Disciples
- Chapter 2. Sri Chaitanya in Purushottama; Jagannatha Dasa Reads the *Bhagavata* under the Banyan
- Chapter 3. Jagannatha Dasa Serves Sri Chaitanya; Mahaprabhu Asks about His Former Form
- Chapter 4. Mahaprabhu Goes with the Swami to Sarvabhauma
- Chapter 5. The Author on Himself and His Book; Nityananda's Question and the Kavacha of the Mantra
- Chapter 6. The Murti of Jagannatha, the Lord above the Lord: the Lotus of the *Brahma-samhita*, Goloka in the Puri Temple, Mantra, Yantra and Tantra
- Chapter 7. Mahaprabhu with His Associates in the Jagamohana; the Gundicha Yatra and Bahuda
- Chapter 23. The Glory of Beholding the Four Deities: the Snana-yatra, the Chariot and Gundicha

---

# Divakara Dasa. *Jagannatha-charitamrita*, Chapter 1
## Introduction: Obeisances to the Guru, the Vaishnavas and the Gods; Ati Bada Gosain and the Sixteen Disciples


*Jagannathamrita-gita*[^01-1]

*Chapter One*

*Shloka*  
**1.1.** *[Skt.]* Full of all perfections, [granting] knowledge, devotion and liberation as befits each, he speaks (?) all perfection … (??). Obeisance to Sri Guru![^01-2]  
**1.2.** First of all I, the brahmana Divakara, bow to the guru.[^01-3]  
**1.3.** He delivered [me] from terrible darkness and showed [me] the lamp of bhakti.  
**1.4.** The feet of all the Vaishnavas are the refuge of the brahmana Divakara.  
**1.5.** The baseness that was in me vanished by the guru's word.  
**1.6.** Whatever doubts [I] may have will go by your command.  
**1.7.** You will show the path of bhakti and take [me] along as your companion.

*Shloka*  
**1.8.** *[Skt.]* Lakshmi, Sarasvati, and also the divine sages (?) and Gajanana, Brahma, Sri Vishnu and Rudra — these the brahmana Divakara should worship.[^01-4]  
**1.9.** That the book [be written] without obstacles, I serve your feet.  
**1.10.** You have made the whole world; [you] are perceived in countless (?) books.[^01-5]  
**1.11.** The composition I have begun — O (?) gods, make it free of obstacles.  
**1.12.** Bring the book to completion without hindrance: the brahmana Divakara [seeks your] refuge.  
**1.13.** If the book turns out to have faults, the saints will never blame it:  
**1.14.** they take its merits and cast (?) the faults far away.  
**1.15.** Even if it be utterly flawless, the wicked one blames it without restraint (?).[^01-6]

*Shloka*  
**1.16.** *[Skt.]* Slanderers and pigs Hari has made useful: slanderers purify the saint, pigs purify the village.[^01-7]  
**1.17.** There is no sinner in the universe equal to a person [given to] slander.  
**1.18.** Sinful and base people do not understand — and they blame an excellent book.  
**1.19.** If he does not slander (?), he will suffer [the torments of the hell] Raurava (?).[^01-8]  
**1.20.** Now — to Ati Bada Gosain: before him I prostrate.[^01-9]  
**1.21.** His sixteen disciples — before them I prostrate.  
**1.22.** [The disciples] of Sri Ati Bada Gosain — these are the names of the sixteen disciples:

*Shloka*  
**1.23.** *[Skt.]* Uddhava (?), Ramachandra, Gopinatha, Sri Hari, Vamana, Nandana, Gaura (?), Gopala, Khandana (?),[^01-10]  
**1.24.** *[Skt.]* Janardana Pati (?), Krishnadasa and Vanamali, and also Govardhana, Kanhu Khuntia (?) and Karmi (?) — so they should be named.[^01-11]  
**1.25.** *[Skt.]* And then the disciple of Jagannatha Dasa … (??). Thus the sixteen disciples; to them, blessed in the [age of] Kali (?), I bow.[^01-12]  
**1.26.** Kirtana in ecstatic (?) [love] for Hari, worship in the mood of a sakhi.[^01-13]  
**1.27.** Sri Ati Bada Gosain was born from a portion of Radha.[^01-14]  
**1.28.** His sixteen disciples are portions of the sixteen sakhis.  
**1.29.** For the joy of serving their Lord they left the eternal [abode] and were born together [with Him].  
**1.30.** Of the sixty-four crores of sakhis, they are the chief in eternal service.[^01-15]  
**1.31.** To perform the lila they came and gathered in the world of mortals.  
**1.32.** Whoever shows ecstatic (?) love — know that one to be an eternal sakhi.  
**1.33.** [He] whom they call Purushottama is eternal; here there is no "difference-and-non-difference" (?).[^01-16]  
**1.34.** There are proofs of this; I shall tell [them] — listen, O saints.

## Signs of the Devotees; the Succession of Gurus; Fidelity to One's Own Path


**1.35.** [Those who bear] the marks of conch and disc by nature (?) — know them as Vaishnavas of Dvaraka.[^01-17]  
**1.36.** One whose eyes do not blink — know him [as one] of the host of the gods.  
**1.37.** As a physician does not know the medicine that revives the dead (?),[^01-18]  
**1.38.** as a man does not know [how] to cross beyond dying, —  
**1.39.** [so,] as long as the body is not a woman's (?), one does not know the bhava of ecstasy (?).[^01-19]  
**1.40.** Only by staying outside ecstasy (?) does one fail to attain prema in bhakti.  
**1.41.** Now I am writing down (?) in the book the succession of gurus.[^01-20]  

*Shloka*  
**1.42.** *[Skt.]* First, the brahmana Divakara; his guru, Navinakeshara (?); his [guru], the all-merciful (?) Kola (?) Krishna;[^01-21]  
**1.43.** *[Skt.]* his disciple (?), Vanamali; and Ati Bada Jagannatha Dasa; the glorious Balarama, and then Hridayananda;  
**1.44.** *[Skt.]* Gauridasa Pandita and Chaitanya Mahaprabhu; Advaita (??), Madhavacharya (?), Nrihari, … (??);[^01-22]  
**1.45.** *[Skt.]* Padmanabha, a shadow-portion (?), and also a portion of Madhava; Vyasa and Narada, Brahma and Narayana with Vishnu (??) — [thus] the name, the bliss of Parabrahman, was proclaimed with effort.  
**1.46.** All the gurus of this succession I have heard of from the mouths of sadhus.  
**1.47.** Honour all the Vaishnavas, but do not abandon your own [object of] worship (?).[^01-23]  
**1.48.** One who serves another's [object of] worship (?) is called a ruin of the family —  
**1.49.** he is like a … (??) young woman who leaves her husband and loves another.[^01-24]  

*In the Vishnu Purana:*  
**1.50.** *[Skt.]* "Shruti and smriti are My command. Whoever, a fool (?), transgresses it is a breaker of My command, My enemy: even if he is My devotee, he is not a Vaishnava."[^01-25]  
**1.51.** Bhagavan says to Narada: "The Vedas and Puranas are My words.[^01-26]  
**1.52.** The fools who abandon them and worship Me by another path  
**1.53.** obtain prosperity and siddhis by their sadhana, yet they do not attain bhakti.  
**1.54.** Since they break (?) My command, they are My enemies.  
**1.55.** However much sadhana they perform — where will they attain Me?"  

*In the Bhagavad-gita:*  
**1.56.** "What the Veda has enjoined on each — by doing (?) that he will cross over [the ocean of existence].[^01-27]  
**1.57.** If a shudra does a brahmana's work, it is not good: he will perish in improper work.  
**1.58.** Doing the work of their own caste, let people not fear, even if it turns out poor."  
**1.59.** Whatever dharma one follows, let him not go over to another's, even if there be gain (?) [in it].  

## Ati Bada Gosain and Sri Chaitanya — from the Laughter of Radha and Krishna

**1.60.** Sri Ati Bada Gosain — who can tell his deeds?  
**1.61.** The charming laughter of Sri Radha — from that he was born.[^01-28]  
**1.62.** Sri Vaikuntha is the eternal abode; Sri Radha and Krishna [sit] on one seat.[^01-29]  
**1.63.** They abide in one bhava; [Their] mind never wavers.  
**1.64.** They were in happiness, in the bliss of rasa; with tenderness [He] looked at [Her] face.  
**1.65.** Seeing Radha's face, like nectar, Krishna obtained happiness.  
**1.66.** That laughter is sweet nectar; from it Chaitanya appeared (lit. "was born").[^01-30]  
**1.67.** Since the Two [abide] in oneness (?), Radha, looking at Krishna, laughed.[^01-31]  
**1.68.** From that laughter at once was born he who [is] Ati Bada Jagannatha.  
**1.69.** The eternal Lord Sri said: "We two shall appear (lit. 'be born') in the world of mortals."  
**1.70.** Looking into Sri Chaitanya's face, [He said]: "Taking twelve sakhis along,[^01-32]  
**1.71.** appear (lit. 'be born') in the town of Nadiya and perform kirtana of the name of Hari."  

## The Age of Kali and the Appearance of Sri Chaitanya; the Birth and Learning of Jagannatha Dasa


*Shloka*  
**1.72.** *[Skt.]* All people are stained with the filth of Kali, intent on what is most sinful (?), laden with great sins, revilers of the Vedas and the twice-born.[^01-33]  
**1.73.** *[Skt.]* In [the age of] Kali people delight in sin and are devoid of [Vedic] study and injunctions; the twice-born live as shudras, and the shudras hate the brahmanas.  
**1.74.** *[Skt.]* Having Himself (?) assumed an incarnation in Navadvipa, … (??), having taken the form of a sannyasi, [He] bears the name Sri Chaitanya.[^01-34]  
**1.75.** Then [He] said: "You (?) are a portion of Radha: be born on earth.[^01-35]  
**1.76.** The glorious, royal (?) name of Hari — cause it to be sung in kirtan in the world."[^01-36]  
**1.77.** By [that] command he came and was born in the kingdom of Odisha.  
**1.78.** [There is a village] named Kapileshvarapur: in [that] sasana is his home.[^01-37]  
**1.79.** [His father was] the Purana-panda Bhagavan; his wife's name was Padma.  
**1.80.** If one is not born on earth, there is no bhakti.[^01-38]  
**1.81.** Therefore he was born from Padma's womb in the guise of a child.  
**1.82.** On the day of the bright eighth of [the month of] Bhadra, at the hour of midday — [his] birth.[^01-39]  
**1.83.** All the [rites] of birth, of dying (?) and the rest his father performed with a pure heart.[^01-40]  
**1.84.** Playing with the boys, he would say: "Hari, Rama, Krishna."  
**1.85.** In time — the vow, learning, perfection: soon he mastered all his duties.  
**1.86.** At twelve years of age his father celebrated (?) his marriage.[^01-41]  
**1.87.** He freed his father of cares (?) and with a pure [mind] immersed himself in learning.[^01-42]  
**1.88.** The Yajur Veda and the Sama Veda — these he learned by heart.  
**1.89.** Both [grammars], Kalapa and Vardhamana, he kept on the tip of his tongue.[^01-43]  
**1.90.** He became versed in all the shastras and read the entire *Bharata*.[^01-44]  
**1.91.** Having mastered the book (?) of the *Sri Ramayana*, he set his mind on the *Sri Bhagavata* —  
**1.92.** on the *Bhagavata* in twelve cantos, with commentaries and glosses (?).[^01-45]  
**1.93.** When he reached eighteen years, he thoroughly mastered all this.  
**1.94.** Before Bata-Ganesha, continually, the *Sri Bhagavata*[^01-46]  
**1.95.** … (??) [he], gaining joy, and Ganesha would answer [him] (?).[^01-47]  
**1.96.** Says the brahmana Divakara: "Jagannatha, deliver me!"  

*Thus [ends], in the *Sri Jagannathamrita*, [the life of] Sri Ati Bada Jagannatha Dasa — the first chapter, called "Anukramani" (?).*[^01-48]

[^01-1]: The title on the first page of the edition and in its running heads; the title page calls the book *Sri Jagannatha-charitamrita*.
[^01-2]: The source of the shloka has not been identified. The reading is uncertain: printed ବଦତି (*vadati*, "speaks"); the sense would lead one to expect ଦଦାତି (*dadāti*, "grants"). The word ସ୍ୟାଭଭତ୍ (*syābhabhat?*) is not identified — perhaps a corruption of *syād bhavet* ("may it be"); the metre (anushtubh) is kept either way.
[^01-3]: "The brahmana Divakara" is the author's signature, repeated in the mangalacharana (1.4, 1.8, 1.12). The "guru" here is his own teacher in the Atibadi line.
[^01-4]: The list of deities of the mangalacharana. The reading ସୁରର୍ଷୀଂଶ୍ଚ (*surarṣīṁś ca*, "and the divine sages") is conjectural: the second sign looks like ସ. Printed ଦିବାକରେ (*divākare*); grammar would require *divākaro* (nom.). Verses 1.9–1.12 address these deities ("you").
[^01-5]: The form ଗ୍ରନ୍ଥଇଁ (*granthaï̐*) is unclear; taken as "in / through books".
[^01-6]: The print has no divider "।" between the half-lines. ଖଳତା (*khaḷatā*) is taken as "the wicked one [blames] it" (*khaḷa tā*); "wickedness" is also possible. ଅବଧା (*abadhā*) is taken as "without restraint" — a guess.
[^01-7]: The shloka seems to be a current saying: the slanderer "cleanses" the saint as a pig cleans the village of filth. The grammar of the first line is faulty (*saphalo nirmito hariḥ*, lit. "Hari was made useful"); it is translated by sense. *Śuddhyanti* ("are purified") stands for "purify".
[^01-8]: Raurava is one of the hells (*Bhagavata Purana* 5.26). The couplet is printed with a negative ("if he does not slander") and is translated literally, but so it contradicts the context (1.17–1.18). The likely sense is "if he slanders, he will suffer Raurava": the negative is either a misprint or part of an idiom not understood. The word ଭୁଞ୍ଜି (*bhuñji*, "having suffered") is read with doubt.
[^01-9]: Ati Bada (Odia *ati baṛa*, "very great") is Jagannatha Dasa, founder of the line to which the author belongs; hence the name of his followers, the Atibadi. According to Odia tradition Mahaprabhu gave him this title (Divakara narrates the episode below); it is absent from the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* and the *Chaitanya-bhagavata*. Cf. VL 5.119 and its note. *Gosāi̐* is the honorific "Gosvami".
[^01-10]: The sixteen disciples of Jagannatha Dasa (1.21–1.22). The division into names is conjectural: the print is unclear at the start of the line (ଉଦ୍ଧବୋ, *Uddhava*?), in ଗୌରେ (*gaure*, *gauro* expected), and in "Gopala-Khandana" (one name or two?). One counts 15–16 names depending on the division.
[^01-11]: "Kanhu Khuntia" (*kāhnu-khuṇṭikāś ca*) is a conjectural reading. A disciple of Jagannatha Dasa; whether he is the same as Kanai (Kanhai) Khuntia, Mahaprabhu's associate and author of the *Mahabhava-prakasha* (cf. VL 2.25–27), is not established. "Janardana Pati" and "Karmi" are likewise conjectural (Pati is an Odia family title); *vadet* — "one should name".
[^01-12]: The words ମୃତବାନଥ (*mṛtavān atha?*) are not understood (perhaps *smṛtavān atha*, "then remembered"?); କଳଧନ୍ୟା (*kaḷadhanyā*) is taken as "blessed in the [age of] Kali" — a guess. Who is called "the disciple of Jagannatha Dasa" in the first line is unclear.
[^01-13]: The word is printed as ସ/ଉ + ମା + ଦ; taken as ଉମାଦ (*umāda*) = *unmāda*, "ecstasy, madness [of love]"; likewise in 1.32. The final "॥" is damaged in the print.
[^01-14]: An Atibadi teaching: Jagannatha Dasa is a portion (*amsha*) of Sri Radha, and his sixteen disciples are portions of sixteen sakhis (1.28). Gaudiya Vaishnavas do not accept this. According to the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 4), the sakhis and gopis are expansions of Sri Radha Herself, and Sri Chaitanya is Krishna Himself in the mood and complexion of Radha (Adi 1.5). Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati counts the "ativadi" among the apasampradayas precisely for such claims. Cf. VL 5.119 (note on the identification of Jagannatha Dasa with Radha).
[^01-15]: "Sixty-four crores of sakhis" is a number from the Odia tradition; it does not occur in Gaudiya Vaishnava texts (cf. Radha's eight principal sakhis, *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 4; *Ujjvala-nilamani*).
[^01-16]: The translation of the second half-line is conjectural. *Bhinnābhinna* means "different-and-non-different"; perhaps the point is that the sakhis (the disciples) and Purushottama are eternally inseparable. Cf. Sri Chaitanya's teaching of *achintya-bhedabheda*, the inconceivable oneness and difference of the Lord and His energies (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 2; Madhya 20); Divakara shows no direct link to it.
[^01-17]: "Signs" (cf. 1.34): according to Divakara, the different classes of beings are recognised by distinguishing marks. Conch and disc are Vishnu's emblems. The reading ସ୍ୱଭାବ (*swabhāba*, "by nature, from birth") is uncertain, and the ି in ଚିହ୍ନ (*cihna*) is unclear in print. Unblinking eyes are the traditional sign of the gods (*animiṣa*).
[^01-18]: *Mrita-sanjivani* is the remedy (or knowledge) that brings the dead back to life. The print seems to have ମୃତୁ (*mṛtu*), taken as *mṛta*. Couplets 1.37–1.38 are similes for 1.39–1.40.
[^01-19]: The word is read as ଉମାଦ (*umāda* = *unmāda*, "ecstasy [of love]"), as the translator of 1.26 and 1.32 also took it (see the note to 1.26). The first sign resembles ସ୍ୱ/ଉ and the second ମା. The reading "of Radha" (ରାଧାଙ୍କ) is rejected: in Radha's name on these pages the second sign is a clear ଧା (cf. 1.61, 1.65). ସ୍ତିରୀ (*stirī*) is taken as colloquial "woman" (= *strī*); the second sign is unclear. The author seems to say that knowing the sakhi's bhava requires a female body or a female self-identification. Gaudiya Vaishnavas understand service in the mood of a sakhi (*manjari*) as internal — in the spiritual body (*siddha-deha*) contemplated within the mind (CC, Madhya 22.156–157), not as a change of outward appearance. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati's list of apasampradayas includes, besides the "ativadi", the "sakhibheki", who outwardly imitate the sakhis.
[^01-20]: *Guru-pranali* is the list of gurus in a succession. The form ଲେଖୁଣ (*lekhuṇa*) is unclear; taken as "I am writing down".
[^01-21]: The shlokas 1.42–1.45 give Divakara's guru-pranali, ascending from the author to Narayana. His guru is Navinakeshara (?); then comes Kola (?) Krishna, then Vanamali (apparently the same as in the list of the sixteen disciples, 1.24) and Ati Bada Jagannatha Dasa. This makes Divakara fifth in succession from Jagannatha Dasa (cf. the note to 1.1). Above Jagannatha Dasa stands Balarama (Dasa; Chapter 23 tells of Jagannatha Dasa's initiation by Balarama Dasa), then Hridayananda and Gauridasa Pandita, and above them Sri Chaitanya. Gauridasa Pandita is a companion of Sri Chaitanya and Nityananda from Ambika-Kalna (CC, Adi 11). Hridayananda is apparently Hridaya-Chaitanya, Gauridasa's disciple and Shyamananda's guru (according to the Gaudiya hagiographies). Balarama Dasa's initiation by Hridayananda is not mentioned in Gaudiya sources. The upper part of the list (Narayana, Brahma, Narada, Vyasa, … Padmanabha, Nrihari, Madhava) resembles the Brahma-Madhva parampara accepted by Gaudiya Vaishnavas (Kavi Karnapura's *Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika*, Baladeva Vidyabhushana's *Prameya-ratnavali*). In that parampara, however, Sri Chaitanya takes initiation from Ishvara Puri, disciple of Madhavendra Puri (CB, Adi 17; CC, Adi 9), while Divakara puts Advaita (?) and Madhavacharya (?) in that place. Gaudiya Vaishnavas do not recognise the branch "Sri Chaitanya — Gauridasa — Hridayananda — Balarama Dasa — Jagannatha Dasa". Many readings in the shlokas are uncertain (see 1.44), and the Sanskrit is irregular (e.g., *divākaro* before *tad-*).
[^01-22]: Uncertain readings in 1.44–1.45. ଅଦ୍ୱତି (*advati*) is taken as "Advaita" (printed ଅ + a sign like ଦ + ତି); ମାଧବାଚାର୍ଯ୍ୟୋ (*mādhavācāryo*) is read from context. Madhvacharya or Madhavendra Puri may be meant. ନୃହର[ନୃହରି] is Nrihari (Nrihari-tirtha of the Madhva parampara?); ଭବାନ ହି (*bhavāna hi*) is not understood. In 1.45, ଛାୟାଂଶକୋ (*chāyāṁśako*, "shadow-portion"?) and ସବିଷ୍ଣୁନା (*saviṣṇunā*, "with Vishnu"?) are tentative readings, and the syntax of the shloka is unclear. Shloka 1.45 takes three lines in print.
[^01-23]: ପୂଜ (*pūja*) is taken as "worship, object of worship" (= *pūjā* or *pūjya*). The sense of 1.47–1.49: honour all Vaishnavas, but do not betray one's own succession and one's own Deity. କୁଳବୁଡ଼ା (*kuḷabuṛā*), literally "one who drowns the family", is an abusive term: "ruin, disgrace of the family".
[^01-24]: The word ବଟପୀ (*baṭapī*?) is not identified; by context it means "unfaithful, wanton". The first sign is ବ or କ.
[^01-25]: A widely quoted verse, usually given as *śruti-smṛtī mamaivājñe yas te ullaṅghya vartate | ājñā-cchedī mama drohī mad-bhakto 'pi na vaiṣṇavaḥ* and ascribed to Vishnu. Divakara names the *Vishnu Purana* as its source; this could not be verified. The print has ଆଜ୍ଞାଚ୍ଛେଦା (*ājñācchedā*) where ଆଜ୍ଞାଚ୍ଛେଦୀ is expected. ମୂଢ଼ାସ୍ତୁ (*mūḍhās tu*, "but a fool"?) is an uncertain reading.
[^01-26]: The source of Bhagavan's words to Narada (1.51–1.55) is not established. Cf. the verse of the *Brahma-yamala* quoted in *Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu* (1.2.101): devotion to Hari without [following] shruti, smriti, the Puranas and the Pancharatra leads only to disturbance. ରୁଦ୍ଧି (*ruddhi*) is the Odia spelling of *ṛddhi*, "prosperity"; ଭାଜନ୍ତି (*bhājanti*, "break, violate"?) is understood from context.
[^01-27]: 1.56–1.58 are Odia verses (not Sanskrit), which Divakara quotes as words of the *Bhagavad-gita*. In sense they are a free rendering of BG 3.35 and 18.47 ("better one's own dharma, though imperfect…"). The Odia source is not established. କରଣ (*karaṇa*) is taken as "doing" (= *kariṇa*?). Following Sri Chaitanya's conversation with Ramananda Raya (CC, Madhya 8), Gaudiya Vaishnavas regard varnashrama duty as "external" compared with pure bhakti (cf. BG 18.66).
[^01-28]: The Atibadi teaching: Jagannatha Dasa was born from Radha's laughter (1.61, 1.67–1.68), and Sri Chaitanya from Krishna's (1.66). Cf. VL 5.116–5.117, where Jagannatha Dasa himself tells this story, and the note there. Gaudiya Vaishnavas do not accept this: Sri Chaitanya is Krishna Himself, who has taken Radha's bhava and complexion (CC, Adi 1.5; Adi 4). Jagannatha Dasa is not mentioned in the CC or the CB. See also the note to 1.27.
[^01-29]: In Divakara, Radha and Krishna abide in Vaikuntha. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas the eternal abode of Radha and Krishna is Goloka-Vrindavana, which is above Vaikuntha (CC, Adi 5).
[^01-30]: Whose laughter it is is not said directly; by the context (1.64–1.65) and by VL 5.116–5.117, it is Krishna's. Of Sri Chaitanya's appearance the text says "was born" (*jāta*), translated "appeared". On the Gaudiya Vaishnava position, see the note to 1.61.
[^01-31]: ଏକତ୍ୱ (*ekatwa*, "oneness") is an uncertain reading (the second sign looks like ତ/ଦ with a subscript).
[^01-32]: Who speaks to whom in 1.69–1.71 is not said directly. Understood thus: the eternal Lord (Krishna) addresses Sri Chaitanya (or the future Sri Chaitanya). "We two" in 1.69 apparently means Sri Chaitanya and Jagannatha Dasa (or Radha and Krishna). Nadiya is Navadvipa. Gaudiya Vaishnavas have no such list of "twelve sakhis" accompanying Sri Chaitanya; cf. the twelve gopalas, companions of Nityananda (*Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika*). The first sign of the word ସଖୀ is unclear in print.
[^01-33]: The source of shlokas 1.72–1.74 has not been identified. For the description of the age of Kali cf. *Bhagavata Purana* 12.2–12.3. The grammar is irregular in places: *śūdra-vṛtā* (printed with a single ତ) for *vṛttāḥ*; *kāraṇaḥ* for *kāriṇaḥ*. *Pāpiṣṭha-tatparāḥ* is a tentative reading: after ପାପି the print has a sign resembling ମ with a subscript.
[^01-34]: Sri Chaitanya's appearance in Navadvipa and His sannyasa: CC, Adi 13 and 17; Madhya 3. The word ଯତାଳୟେ (*yatālaye*) is not understood: "in the abode of yatis (ascetics)"? The sense would lead one to expect *dvijālaye*, "in a brahmana's house". In ସ୍ୱୟଂ (*svayam*, "Himself") the second sign is bold and smudged. Shlokas 1.72–1.74 introduce the continuation of 1.69–1.71: the Lord appeared in Navadvipa and sent Jagannatha Dasa to be born in Odisha.
[^01-35]: The reading ତୁହି (*tuhi*, "you") is tentative: the print has ତ with a subscript ୁ, and ହ with a sign above; the rhyme with ମହୀ (*mahī*) supports it (OCR reads ଗୃହ). The speaker is apparently the same Lord as in 1.69–1.71, and He addresses Jagannatha Dasa. On Jagannatha Dasa as a portion of Radha see the notes to 1.27 and 1.61: Gaudiya Vaishnavas do not accept this teaching.
[^01-36]: According to Divakara, the spreading of the kirtan of Hari's name in the world is entrusted to Jagannatha Dasa. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas the founder of *nama-sankirtana*, the yuga-dharma of the age of Kali, is Sri Chaitanya Himself (CC, Adi 3; Adi 17). Jagannatha Dasa is not mentioned in the CC or the CB. *Rāja* (ରାଜ) is taken as "royal, the king [of names]"; the line could also be divided differently.
[^01-37]: Kapileshvarapur is a *sasana* near Puri, that is, a village of brahmanas granted by the king. According to Odia tradition, Jagannatha Dasa was born here; his father was Bhagavan Dasa, a reciter of the Puranas (*purana-panda*), and his mother Padmavati (here Padma). The print has ଲ (କପିଲେଶ୍ୱର); the usual spelling has ଳ. ମଧେ (*madhe*) is a colloquial spelling of *madhye*, "in".
[^01-38]: ମହୀ (*mahī*, "earth") is read from the shape of the sign and from the rhyme with ନାହିଁ; the same word occurs in 1.75. The print has no "॥" at the end of the line.
[^01-39]: The bright eighth of Bhadra is Radhashtami, the day of Sri Radha's appearance; by tradition She appeared at midday. By naming this day and hour Divakara apparently supports the Atibadi teaching of Jagannatha Dasa as a portion of Radha (1.27, 1.75). Gaudiya Vaishnavas do not accept this identification (see the note to 1.27). The print has ଭଦ୍ରବ (*bhadraba*); ଭ and ଭା are hard to tell apart in this type (cf. ଭଗବାନ 1.79, ଭାଗବତ 1.91–1.94).
[^01-40]: The sense is unclear. Perhaps *janma maraṇa ādi* means "all the rites of the life cycle" (samskaras). Taken literally, a mention of funeral rites is out of place here.
[^01-41]: ବିସ୍ତରିଲେ (*bistarile*, lit. "spread out, extended") is a tentative reading. The print has ବ (with the inner stroke for ି) + a sign like ସ with a subscript ତ (cf. ବିସ୍ତର 2.10) + ରି + ଲେ. It is taken as "celebrated [the marriage] lavishly, arranged".
[^01-42]: ବିଘ୍ନ (*bighna*, "obstacle") is an uncertain reading; the second sign could also be ଞ୍ଚ. *Nāsti kale* means "brought to nothing". Perhaps the son freed his father of cares [about him].
[^01-43]: Kalapa is the *Katantra* grammar (ascribed to Sharvavarman), widespread in Orissa and Bengal. "Vardhamana" is apparently a commentary on it (Vardhamana's *Katantra-vistara*). Another understanding is possible: Kalapa and the [grammar] of Vardhamana.
[^01-44]: The *Bharata* is the *Mahabharata* (possibly the Odia *Mahabharata* of Sarala Dasa).
[^01-45]: Jagannatha Dasa is the author of the Odia *Bhagavata* (cf. VL 5.51, 5.101). ଟିପ୍ପଣୀ (*ṭippaṇī*, "glosses") is a tentative reading: after ଟିପ୍ପଣ the print has an unclear sign with chandrabindu. ଗ୍ରନ୍ଥ (*grantha*) in 1.91 is also uncertain: the first sign is bold and smudged.
[^01-46]: Bata-Ganesha is the Ganesha by the banyan in the precincts of the Jagannatha temple (cf. VL 2.22 and its note; VL 4.36–4.37, Jagannatha Dasa at Bata-Ganesha). According to Divakara, Jagannatha Dasa expounded the *Bhagavata* before Ganesha (cf. 2.9–2.10).
[^01-47]: The first word is ବକୁଣ (*bakuṇa*?): ବ + କୁ + ଣ; not identified. From the sense one may conjecture ବଖାଣୁ (*bakhāṇu*, "expounding"), i.e. "[he] would expound, gaining joy", but this is only a guess. Then comes ଥାନ୍ତି (*thānti*, auxiliary of the continuous aspect). *Gaṇeśa deuthānti kahi* is taken as "Ganesha would answer, speak [to him]".
[^01-48]: In the print the chapter colophon is set in two lines. "Anukramani" (*anukramaṇī*) means "list, sequence"; this is apparently the name of the introductory chapter. The printed signs are unclear (ଅନୁକ୍ମର୍ଣି: a subscript ମ under କ and a reph-like sign over ଣି); read from the sense. The address "Jagannatha" in the signature line 1.96 may refer to Lord Jagannatha or to Jagannatha Dasa.

---

# Divakara Dasa. *Jagannatha-charitamrita*, Chapter 2
## Sri Chaitanya in Purushottama; Jagannatha Dasa Reads the *Bhagavata* under the Banyan


*Chapter Two*

**2.1.** Listen attentively, good people, to the nectarean narrative [that] you have heard (?).[^02-1]  
**2.2.** When that Chaitanya Gosain had appeared (lit. "was born") from Shachi's womb,  
**2.3.** He completed His family duties in [worldly] life (?) and then took initiation into sannyasa.[^02-2]  
**2.4.** Taking a host of Vaishnavas with Him, He wandered much over the earth.  
**2.5.** He visited many holy places and wandered through Vrindavana and other [places].  
**2.6.** Not finding the rasa of loving devotion, He entered Purushottama.[^02-3]  
**2.7.** Having come, He stayed in a matha and went to see Jagannatha.[^02-4]  
**2.8.** Thus some days passed. Hear of the lila of Sri Chaitanya.  
**2.9.** Sri Ati Bada Jagannatha [Dasa] — the Tenth Canto of the *Bhagavata*  
**2.10.** at the root (?) of the sacred tree he expounds in detail before Ganesha.[^02-5]  
**2.11.** "Those who reject Krishna's shelter (?) and churn (?) the words of the Vedas[^02-6]  
**2.12.** [bear] toil only for gain: in the end they do not obtain liberation (??).  
**2.13.** As in pounding … (??) no rice grains are found in it,  
**2.14.** so one fallen (?) from Krishna, [given to] other worship, dies [and becomes] a bhuta."  
**2.15.** At this time Sri Chaitanya, taking His companions along,  
**2.16.** graciously came under the banyan; hearing the Purana, He was pleased.[^02-7]  

## Sri Chaitanya Honours Jagannatha Dasa; the Vaishnavas Murmur; a Dispute about Initiation


**2.17.** In the fullness of prema Sri Chaitanya embraced the Dasa.[^02-8]  
**2.18.** For two and a half days the two remained in the bonds (??) of prema.  
**2.19.** Concealing His prema and restraining Himself, Gosain blessed [him].  
**2.20.** Having walked around the temple, [He] paid honour to Jagannatha.  
**2.21.** A twelvefold (?) full prostration he made before the Prabhu.[^02-9]  
**2.22.** To the brahmana Sri Chaitanya said: "You are not different from the Sri Murti."[^02-10]  
**2.23.** [This is] the nectar of Chaitanya's deeds: this devotee is Chaitanya's [devotee].[^02-11]  
**2.24.** Thus Lord Chaitanya spoke in the brahmana's presence:  
**2.25.** "To call Jagannatha 'Brahman' does not come from My mouth.[^02-12]  
**2.26.** Brahman dwells in all bodies (lit. 'vessels'): [to call him] equal [to all] I fear.  
**2.27.** To say [that I wish] to become his servant — [that too] I fear to utter.  
**2.28.** He is certainly a Dasa (lit. 'servant'); what am I to do, [his] state being such (?)?  
**2.29.** His own feet (?) are a great abode of prema.  
**2.30.** Placing them in the lotus (?) of My heart, I shall meditate [on them] without selfish desire (?)."  
**2.31.** Having said this, Sri Chaitanya honours [him] again and again.  
**2.32.** The host of Vaishnavas who were [there], seeing this, lamented (?):[^02-13]  
**2.33.** "With an Odia brahmana He has unceasingly increased … (??).[^02-14]  
**2.34.** He will stay in this kingdom and observe Odia rites.[^02-15]  
**2.35.** We shall draw Him away from this association." Thus lamenting (?), they began [to speak].  
**2.36.** They said: "The Utkala brahmanas, moreover, have received no instruction (initiation).[^02-16]  
**2.37.** Do not touch (?) them: the shastras forbid it.[^02-17]  
**2.38.** Do not desire association with the worldly (?); do not worship various gods.  
**2.39.** A man who has not received initiation — do not touch his body.  
**2.40.** Like a tiger, a snake, a crocodile (?), shun (?) them, [even if they are] friendly (?).  
**2.41.** A person who has not received initiation is equal to an untouchable (?)."  
**2.42.** Hearing [this], Sri Chaitanya gave His command: "This is not a good word.  

*Shloka*  
**2.43.** *[Skt.]* 'Even now in Dvaraka, day after day, the proclamation is heard (?): whether unlearned or learned, a brahmana is My body.'[^02-18]  
**2.44.** All the gods are in the brahmana's body; in the brahmana's body Vishnu dwells.  
**2.45.** Though he has not received instruction (initiation), he is nonetheless a body of bhakti.[^02-19]  

*Shloka*  
**2.46.** *[Skt.]* 'In whose body Krishna dwells, whose body is the form of bhakti — between that Krishna and the brahmana one should make no distinction (?).'"[^02-20]  
**2.47.** Since such a command had been given, all then fell silent.  
**2.48.** The Dasa received this news and went for the Prabhu's darshan.  
**2.49.** Taking two [pots of] abhada in his hands, he came and presented himself [before Him] at the matha.[^02-21]  
**2.50.** Having washed his hands, he at once made a full prostration to the Prabhu.  
**2.51.** Sri Chaitanya commanded: "Come, O Dasa, full of bhakti!"  
**2.52.** The Dasa, with joined palms, said: "I (lit. 'we') have no initiation (lit. 'instruction')."[^02-22]  

*Shloka*  
**2.53.** *[Skt.]* 'Vows, charity, austerity, ablution, purity, shraddha and sacrifice — all dharmas perish [if they are] … (?) without instruction (initiation).'[^02-23]  
**2.54.** Thus Jagannatha Dasa spoke before the Prabhu.  

*Shloka*  
**2.55.** *[Skt.]* 'Sin committed in millions of births, knowingly and unknowingly, is put to flight (?) by merely accepting diksha — of this there is no doubt.'[^02-24]  
**2.56.** Though there be sins of millions of births, [committed] knowingly and unknowingly, they depart (?):  
**2.57.** by merely accepting diksha that [person] becomes sinless and is delivered.  

## The fruits of initiation; what a guru must be, and from whom initiation is not to be taken


*Shloka*  
**2.58.** *[Skt.]* "Then comes the fulfilment of what is desired (?): wealth, dharma and good bhakti; and at the end [the initiate] goes to the divine abode of Vishnu, where servants serve [Him].[^02-25]  

*Shloka*  
**2.59.** *[Skt.]* The killing of a brahmana, drinking liquor, theft of gold and the other (?) [great] sins, and hundreds of thousands of lesser sins, [a person] destroys by the power of diksha."  
**2.60.** The killing of a brahmana, drinking liquor, or whoever steals gold:  
**2.61.** these and other sins, hundreds of thousands, diksha manifestly takes away.  
**2.62.** A person who has received initiation (lit. 'instruction') obtains whatever he desires.  
**2.63.** [The initiated] obtain wealth, dharma and bhakti; in sadhana they gain all fruits.  
**2.64.** Listen (?), O sadhus, to the rule for examining (??) a guru.[^02-26]  

*Shloka*  
**2.65.** *[Skt.]* "Though born in a great family, versed in all the shastras, and having studied [Vedic branches] a thousand in number (?), a non-Vaishnava cannot be a guru."[^02-27]  
**2.66.** Though he was born in the best family and knows all the shastras,  
**2.67.** though he studied the Vedas in a thousand branches and became expert,  
**2.68.** if he is not (?) a Vaishnava, he is not fit for the position of guru.  

*Shloka*  
**2.69.** *[Skt.]* "Brahmanas, Vaishnavas, devotees, truthful, sattvic and steady: in taking diksha from them [comes] good understanding, [and one delivers] one's family (??)."[^02-28]  
**2.70.** Being a brahmana, [he must be] a Vaishnava: the disposition of his mind will be in bhakti;  
**2.71.** he will always speak the truth and by his sattvic nature be steady.  
**2.72.** Whoever takes initiation from such a one delivers a hundred [generations] of his family.  
**2.73.** From [a guru of] which (??) caste initiation is received, such is the dwelling place [of the initiate].  

*Shloka*  
**2.74.** *[Skt.]* "[One initiated] by a brahmana [goes] to Brahmaloka, by a kshatriya to Indraloka (?), by a vaishya to [the world] of Prajapati, and one initiated by a shudra to [the world] of the Gandharvas."[^02-29]  
**2.75.** [For one who received] initiation from a brahmana, his dwelling is in Brahmaloka.  
**2.76.** Whoever takes initiation from a kshatriya dwells together with the gods.  
**2.77.** Having received the mantra from a vaishya, one goes and dwells in Pitriloka.  
**2.78.** [For one who received] initiation from a shudra, his dwelling is in the world of the Gandharvas.  
**2.79.** Such is the rule for non-Vaishnavas; but from a Vaishnava one obtains every perfection.  

*Shloka*  
**2.80.** *[Skt.]* "One without a son (?), one who has lost his children, one with a diseased scrotum (?), a dwarf, … (??); one with bad nails, with dark teeth, a leper, and one with the look of a rogue;[^02-30]  
**2.81.** *[Skt.]* one lacking a limb, one suffering from a grievous disease (?), one without family roots, and also the unsteady (?): [from them] there is no diksha; such are to be avoided.  
**2.82.** *[Skt.]* The untouchable (?), … (??), and one who has sided with enemies; a boaster, one fallen from [his own] guru, the unrighteous: such a one is to be abandoned.[^02-31]  
**2.83.** *[Skt.]* The blind, the deaf (?), the dumb, the dull, and also the lame; one fallen from purity and without propriety: such a guru is to be avoided.  
**2.84.** *[Skt.]* The cheat, the miser, the fool, the fickle (?), one devoid of bhakti, or an adulterer, one of crooked mind: [such] are to be avoided.  
**2.85.** *[Skt.]* Horse-traders (?) and cattle-traders (?), village priests, physicians (??), weapon-makers (?), cooks, those who bow [to no one]."  
**2.86.** These [people] with bad marks (?), having become gurus, bear guilt.[^02-32]  
**2.87.** If initiation is received from them, it destroys wealth, dharma and fame.  
**2.88.** In the practice of the mantra there is no perfection; whence, then, will he find bhakti?  
**2.89.** Therefore whoever is wise will quickly abandon such a guru.  
**2.90.** A guru who has no son: from him there is no initiation;  
**2.91.** one with a swollen scrotum (??) or a dwarf: from him do not accept [initiation];  
**2.92.** one whose nails have withered, whose teeth (?) will be … (??)[^02-33]  

## From Whom Not to Accept the Name; a Guru of Any Varna; Jagannatha Dasa Asks for the Name


**2.93.** a hunchback, a rogue, an ugly person — do not accept the Name from [such].[^02-34]  
**2.94.** One-eyed, sick with phlegm, … (??), [suffering from] elephantiasis, one whose body is lame;  
**2.95.** one who has no root of lineage — [he] is not fit to be a guru.  
**2.96.** A guru who does the work of a purohita — abandon him as guru, away with him (?)![^02-35]  
**2.97.** One whom it is improper to touch; one who is younger in age;  
**2.98.** a guru who is on the enemy's side — from him there is no initiation.  
**2.99.** One who quarrels with everyone — do not touch him.  
**2.100.** One who abandons his own dharma — from him there is no initiation.  
**2.101.** A guru who is blind or deaf — abandon him from afar.  
**2.102.** A person who is a slanderer (?), palsied, or subject to phlegm disease;  
**2.103.** one who does not keep his own purity — such a guru is not fit to be touched.  
**2.104.** A rogue, a miser, a fool — they are not worthy of worship (?) as gurus.  
**2.105.** [One who] falsely calls himself a Vaishnava [but] does nothing for the sake (?) of bhakti;  
**2.106.** one who consumes intoxicating substances, one who drinks wine;  
**2.107.** [one who] casts his eye on another's wife (printed 'on another's servant') — such a guru is not fit to be touched.[^02-36]  
**2.108.** One who speaks with guile; one who is impotent (a eunuch);  
**2.109.** one who trades in horses; one who kills animals with his own hand;  
**2.110.** one whose position is that of a village priest — reject his Name from afar.  
**2.111.** One who practises medicine (?); one who, serving, carries out [others'] orders;[^02-37]  
**2.112.** or one who serves out … (??) [food] — they are not worthy of obeisance.  
**2.113.** Having understood thus the signs of a guru, receive the mantra into the ear.  
**2.114.** Perhaps [someone] will say thus: "He is surely absorbed in the Name;[^02-38]  
**2.115.** they know many things and are intent on bhakti —  
**2.116.** why (?) then are these not [counted as] Vaishnavas and not fit (?) for the position of guru?"  
**2.117.** — They had faults even before, and now they are acquiring [them] still more.  
**2.118.** Until they give up these faults, how can [they] be called gurus?  
**2.119.** As if [someone], taking a good seed (?), sows it in scorched ground —[^02-39]  
**2.120.** however much effort he makes there, a tree will never grow —  
**2.121.** so the mantra is the good seed, [and] the highest bhakti is the instrument (?) [for] that seed.  
**2.122.** If this mantra comes into the hand of [such] a guru, it will perhaps become futile.  
**2.123.** As [when] an excellent brahmana, skilled in the Vedas and Puranas,  
**2.124.** has eaten in a chandala's house — so many [of his] virtues have become futile.  
**2.125.** A Vaishnava who is such (?) — never make him your guru.  
**2.126.** Practise sadhana with the highest bhakti — then there will be the highest bhakti.  
**2.127.** But one who is free from desire (?) and detached has given up these faults.[^02-40]  
**2.128.** One who worships Hari single-mindedly is entitled [to be guru] for all varnas.  
**2.129.** Brahmana, kshatriya, shudra, vaishya, the various castes, ascetic, sannyasi;  
**2.130.** be it a shudra or a woman — whoever knows the twelve [kinds of] prema,[^02-41]  
**2.131.** the bhava of the prema of the Couple — Kishori and Kishora — together,  
**2.132.** the devotee who knows these qualities is himself the form of the body of prema.  
**2.133.** He is blessed among Vaishnavas; he is fit [to be] a guru.  
**2.134.** One who has received into his ear the Name from such a guru —  
**2.135.** the two kings of mantras beginning with [the name] Rama (?), this is the essence of all mantras —[^02-42]  
**2.136.** one who serves the lotus (?) [feet] of that guru — blessed is his discipleship.  
**2.137.** Thus spoke Jagannatha Dasa before Sri Chaitanya.[^02-43]  
**2.138.** "O Lord, ocean of devotees, give [me] the command [to chant] the Name of Sri Hari!"  
**2.139.** Hearing [this], the Prabhu was delighted and said to Matta Balarama:[^02-44]  
**2.140.** "Give him initiation; have him perform the kirtan that begins with [the name] Rama."  

*Shloka*  
**2.141.** *[Skt.]* "The Name of Hari, the Name of Hari, the Name alone is the supreme refuge. In [the age of] Kali there is no, no, no other way."[^02-45]  

## Matta Balarama Initiates Jagannatha Dasa; the End of the Chapter


**2.142.** The one who (?) [is called] Matta Balarama is the best among the Vaishnavas,[^02-46]  
**2.143.** truthful, sattvic, most steadfast, an ocean of mercy (?) in the rasa of prema.  
**2.144.** Sitting, lying, eating, and also while bathing and drinking water,  
**2.145.** they know neither night nor day: they remain intoxicated with the Name.  
**2.146.** Relishing the nectar of the Name, in bliss [they] thrill with prema (?).  
**2.147.** Seeing his prema and bhakti, the Merciful One showed mercy.[^02-47]  
**2.148.** That lord, Matta Balarama, having performed siddhi (?), sat down on the asana.  
**2.149.** Making [a mark] in the shape of Hari's foot, he applied tilaka to the forehead:  
**2.150.** it is called "Hari-mandira"; from the middle of the nose up to the hair it shines (?).[^02-48]  
**2.151.** He put malas of lotus [seeds], tulasi and dhatri (?) on Dasa's neck.  
**2.152.** On the chest, a lotus-shaped tilaka; conch and disc on both arms.  
**2.153.** Placing these mudras on the crown of the head (?), applying [them] at the sides of the … (??);  
**2.154.** the navel, the waist (?), the four sides; Srivatsa and moon, in fourteen [places].[^02-49]  
**2.155.** He had the seat purified; [he spoke] the words of the sankalpa, [naming] place and time.  
**2.156.** He began (?) [the rite with] the string and kaupina; seated, he performed [the invocation of] the rishi and chandas,[^02-50]  
**2.157.** kara-nyasa and anga-nyasa, and meditation on the form of Sri Jagannatha.  
**2.158.** In the midst of the assembly of Vaishnavas, in the presence of Sri Mahaprabhu,  
**2.159.** he gave the instruction in the Name: the sweet (?) kirtan beginning with the Name.  
**2.160.** Then he imparted the king of mantras, and also gave the sixteen [names] of prema.[^02-51]  
**2.161.** In the rasa of the prema of Radha and Krishna, he imparted the Gayatri of the Couple.  
**2.162.** He imparted the mantra of Thakura and Thakurani, which is the essence [of all].  
**2.163.** The five names of Sri Jagannatha the excellent Sri Guru imparted.  
**2.164.** The mantra called "beginning with [the name] Krishna" no one knew at that time:[^02-52]  
**2.165.** [people] do not abandon the word of the Veda; all honour Brahma's command.  
**2.166.** Thus, by the rule of initiation, Dasa obtained all perfection (?).  
**2.167.** In such [people] Divakara Dasa places his hope (?).[^02-53]  
**2.168.** Whoever accepts [initiation] thus is delivered from samsara.  
**2.169.** This is the very mantra of [our] own line. Whoever, showing the mantra of another line,[^02-54]  
**2.170.** will deceive a person and lead [him] away — do not go [to such a one], even if it costs your life.  
**2.171.** Association [with him] will be like (??) [association with] a young woman; worshipping the king of mantras, [a person] will cross over [samsara].  

*Thus [ends], in the *Sri Jagannathamrita*, [the life of] Sri Ati Bada Jagannatha Gosain — the second chapter, called "Anukramani" (?).*[^02-55]

[^02-1]: ଶୁଣିଛ (*śuṇicha*) is formally a perfect of the 2nd person ("you have heard"); perhaps "listen — [here is] the nectarean narrative" is meant.
[^02-2]: ରାଜ୍ୟେ (*rājye*, lit. "in the kingdom") is taken as "in worldly life, at home". According to the CC, Mahaprabhu lived as a householder in Navadvipa until the age of twenty-four and then took sannyasa in Katwa (CC, Adi 17; Madhya 3). The print has ସନ୍ୟାସ (*sanyāsa*).
[^02-3]: According to Divakara, Mahaprabhu wandered through holy places, Vrindavana among them, and, not finding the rasa of loving devotion [there], came to Purushottama (Puri). This prepares the account of His finding it with Jagannatha Dasa. According to the CC, after sannyasa Mahaprabhu went straight to Puri (Madhya 3–6), then travelled in the South (Madhya 7–9), and went to Vrindavana only later (Madhya 17–19). For Gaudiya Vaishnavas Sri Chaitanya is Krishna Himself in the mood of Radha, the source and giver of prema (CC, Adi 3–4). He does not seek prema from others. Cf. VL 4 (Mahaprabhu's arrival in Puri and meeting with Jagannatha Dasa at Bata-Ganesha) and the notes there. The print has ଅଦି (*adi*) for ଆଦି (2.5).
[^02-4]: A "matha" is a monastic residence. According to the CC, in Puri Mahaprabhu first stayed with Sarvabhauma and then lived in the house of Kashi Mishra (Madhya 6; Madhya 10).
[^02-5]: "Sri Ati Bada Jagannatha" in 2.9 is Jagannatha Dasa (cf. 1.20, 1.68). The "sacred tree" is the banyan in the temple precincts at whose root Bata-Ganesha stands (cf. 1.94; VL 2.22). ମୂଳର (*mūḷara*, "of the root") is an uncertain reading: the second sign is bold. The print has ଅଗର (*agara*) for ଆଗର, "before".
[^02-6]: 2.11–2.14 are apparently what Jagannatha Dasa expounds (or the author's own words). They render *Bhagavata Purana* 10.14.4: "Those who give up bhakti, the path to the highest good, and labour only for knowledge, gain nothing but trouble, like those who thresh empty husks". The unclear places are understood accordingly. ଅଶ୍ରେ (*aśre*) is taken as *āśre* / *āśraẏa*, "shelter" (possibly ଅଂଶେ). In ମନ୍ଥନ୍ତି (*manthanti*, "churn") the printed sign looks like ଣ୍ଟ ("maṇṭanti"); after the *Bhagavata* verse one would expect "only for knowledge". In 2.12 the signs are unclear: େ, a damaged sign, ା and a sign like ସ with a subscript. The word is taken as ମୋକ୍ଷ (*mokṣa*), "liberation". ରପୁ (*rapu*) in 2.13 is not identified: after the verse "husk" (*tuṣa*) would be expected. ଚ୍ୟୁତ (*cyuta*, "fallen") in 2.14 is read by the rhyme with ଭୂତ: the first sign is bold. In 2.13 the first sign of «ଯେମନ୍ତ» looks like ସ, and in 2.14 the first sign of «ସେହି» looks like ଯ (the ସ/ଯ trap); both are read from the sense (as… so…). ପ୍ରତେଜନ୍ତି (*pratejanti*, "reject") — cf. Chapter 1, 1.14.
[^02-7]: Mahaprabhu comes under the banyan to hear Jagannatha Dasa's reading. This is an episode of Odia tradition; cf. VL 4 (the meeting at Bata-Ganesha). It is not in the CC or the CB: according to the CC, in Puri Mahaprabhu hears the *Bhagavata* from Gadadhara Pandita (Antya 7). "The Purana" here is the *Bhagavata*. *Sakhā-gaṇa* means "companions, friends" (not *sakhī*).
[^02-8]: "The Dasa" is Jagannatha Dasa (cf. 2.9, 2.54). "Gosain" (2.19) is Sri Chaitanya. The last word of 2.18 is not identified: the printed sign looks like ଚ/ବ + େ + the ligature ନ୍ଧ/ନ୍ଦ (or ଛ). It is read ବନ୍ଧେ (*bandhe*, "in the bonds"); perhaps ଛନ୍ଦେ (*chande*), with the same sense. The rhyme with ପର୍ଯ୍ୟନ୍ତେ is imperfect. ଗୁଢ଼ (*guṛha*) in 2.19 = *gūṛha*, "hidden". The embrace of Mahaprabhu and Jagannatha Dasa and their two days together are Odia tradition; Jagannatha Dasa is not mentioned in the CC or the CB (cf. the note to 2.16; VL 4).
[^02-9]: ବିଧ (*bidha*, "kind, -fold") is read from the shape of the sign (ବ with the stroke ି + ଧ). Who bows is not stated: apparently Jagannatha Dasa, before the Prabhu (Sri Chaitanya), who then addresses him (2.22). Another reading is possible: Sri Chaitanya bows to Jagannatha (continuing 2.20).
[^02-10]: The "Sri Murti" is the Deity of Jagannatha in the temple. Mahaprabhu's words (2.22–2.30) express the Atibadi doctrine: Jagannatha Dasa is not different from Jagannatha, and Sri Chaitanya regards Himself as his servant. Cf. 1.61, 1.68 (Jagannatha Dasa born from Radha's laughter) and 1.27, 1.75 (he is a portion of Radha); cf. also VL 6.61–6.63, where the Prabhu calls him "Svami". For Gaudiya Vaishnavas the Deity of Jagannatha is Krishna Himself. The soul, by its nature, is Krishna's eternal servant (CC, Madhya 20.108), not identical with Him. A pure devotee is honoured as dear to the Lord, but is not identified with Him. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati lists the "ativadi" among the apasampradayas precisely for such exaltation of Jagannatha Dasa.
[^02-11]: The couplet stands outside the narrative: perhaps a refrain-signature, or Mahaprabhu's own words. The words ଚୈତନ୍ୟ ଚରିତ ଅମୃତ (*caitanya carita amṛta*) coincide with the title of Krishnadasa Kaviraja's *Chaitanya-charitamrita*. Whether this is a deliberate allusion cannot be judged from one line. Cf. the title of the poem itself, *Jagannatha-charitamrita*, and 2.1 ("the nectarean narrative").
[^02-12]: The understanding of 2.25–2.30 is tentative. "Jagannatha" in 2.25 is apparently Jagannatha Dasa (cf. 2.9, "Sri Ati Bada Jagannatha"). The train of thought: to call him "Brahman" would put him on a par with all beings (Brahman is in all), and to call Himself his servant Mahaprabhu "fears" out of humility; therefore He keeps his feet in His heart. ଡ଼ରଇ, ଡ଼ରୁଛି (*ṛaraï, ṛaruchi*) are printed with ଡ଼ (= ଡରଇ, "I fear"). In ହୁଅନ୍ତି (*huanti*, 2.28) the printed ି is not visible. In ପୟର (*paẏara*, "feet", 2.29) there is a subscript sign under ୟ. ହୃଦପଦ୍ମମଧେ (*hṛda-padma-madhe*, "within the lotus of the heart") and ନିଷ୍କାମେ (*niṣkāme*) in 2.30 are tentative readings. Gaudiya Vaishnavas know Mahaprabhu's humility before devotees: "I am the servant of the servant of the servant of the servants of Krishna, the Lord of the gopis" (CC, Madhya 13.80). Yet He remains Krishna Himself (CC, Adi 1–4); Jagannatha Dasa is not mentioned in the CC or the CB. See also the note to 2.22.
[^02-13]: The Vaishnavas who murmur against the "Odia brahmana" (2.33–2.41) are apparently Mahaprabhu's Bengali (Gaudiya) companions (cf. *sakhā-gaṇa*, 2.15). Cf. VL 6.59–6.64: there too the Gaudiyas complain that the Prabhu exalts Jaga Dasa, "who has received no instruction" (*aṇa-upadeśiā*; see the note to VL 6.63), and leave Nilachala. This episode is not in the CC or the CB. ବିସୂରିଲେ, ବିସୂରି (*bisūrile, bisūri*, "lamented, lamenting"; cf. VL 6.59): the ି signs on ବ and ର are indistinct.
[^02-14]: The first word of the second half-line is not identified. The first sign resembles ଶ/ଛ with the stroke ି, the second is ବ. It is read ଶିବ (*śiba*, "welfare, friendship"?). The sense would lead one to expect "intimacy, affection".
[^02-15]: "Odia rites" (*oṛiā karma*) are apparently the local customs and temple usages of Puri. The Vaishnavas fear that Mahaprabhu will remain in Odisha and adopt the Odia way of life.
[^02-16]: *Aṇa-upadeśiā*: "one who has not received instruction (*upadeśa*)", i.e. initiation (diksha) from a guru; cf. VL 6.63. The reproach is aimed at all Utkala (Odia) brahmanas and above all at Jagannatha Dasa. He himself confirms in 2.52 that he has no initiation. According to Ch. 23, he was later initiated by Balarama Dasa (cf. the guru-pranali, 1.42–1.45).
[^02-17]: The readings in 2.37–2.41 are uncertain in places. ପରଶ (*paraśa* = *sparśa*, "touch") in 2.37 and ଅପରଶ (*aparaśa*, "untouchable") in 2.41: in both the sign before ର is damaged. ବିଷୟ (*biṣaẏa*, "worldly things"; or ବିଷୟୀ, "worldly person") in 2.38: the first sign is damaged. In 2.40 ନାଇଁ (*nāi̐*, "like"): the first sign is damaged (ନା, or ଠା "from"). There too ବଞ୍ଚିବ (*bañciba*, "shun, escape") may be ବର୍ଜିବ (*barjiba*, "avoid"); ପ୍ରୀତି ହୋଇ (*prīti hoi*) is taken as "[even if they are] friendly". The rules themselves come from Vaishnava practice: "giving up bad association — this is the conduct of a Vaishnava" (CC, Madhya 22.87); not worshipping other gods (CC, Madhya 22). But Gaudiya Vaishnavas do not apply them to a devotee on the grounds of initiation or birth (see the note to 2.43).
[^02-18]: The shloka is known as Krishna's words about brahmanas; its source could not be established. ଶ୍ରୂୟତେ (*śrūyate*) is a tentative reading: after ଶ୍ରୂ stands a sign like ୟ/ସ୍ମ with a subscript. In Divakara, Mahaprabhu answers the reproach by appealing to Jagannatha Dasa's brahmanhood (2.43–2.46): a brahmana is the Lord's body, even without initiation. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas the criterion is not birth but knowledge of Krishna and bhakti: "Whether a brahmana, a sannyasi or a shudra — whoever knows the science of Krishna is a guru" (CC, Madhya 8.128). And initiation itself, according to the *Hari-bhakti-vilasa* (2.12), makes a person twice-born.
[^02-19]: *Bhakti aṅga*: lit. "body (limb) of bhakti"; cf. *bhakti-rūpiṇam* in the shloka 2.46. In Divakara, Mahaprabhu declares the uninitiated Jagannatha Dasa an embodiment of bhakti. According to the CB (Adi 17) and the CC (Adi 9), Mahaprabhu Himself accepted initiation from Ishvara Puri, setting the example, and Gaudiya Vaishnavas consider diksha from a genuine guru obligatory for all, whatever their birth. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati insisted on this especially.
[^02-20]: The source of the shloka has not been established. The Sanskrit is irregular: *bhakti-rūpiṇaṁ* (one would expect *bhakti-rūpakam*). *Bhinnābhinnaṁ na kārayet* is taken as "one should make no distinction" between the brahmana (Jagannatha Dasa) and Krishna; cf. the note to 2.22. Mahaprabhu's words (from 2.42) apparently end here.
[^02-21]: Abhada (*abhaṛā*, also *abaṛhā*) is Jagannatha's maha-prasad: rice and other dishes cooked in the temple kitchen and offered to the Lord; they are carried in clay pots. Jagannatha Dasa brings it to Mahaprabhu as a gift. On Mahaprabhu's and Sarvabhauma's reverence for maha-prasad see CC, Madhya 6. *Maṭhaṭhāre*: "at the matha" (*maṭha* + *ṭhāre*, "at, in"); on the matha see the note to 2.7.
[^02-22]: *Upadeśa* here means "initiation" (cf. 2.36). The shlokas 2.53 and 2.55 on the necessity of diksha are apparently quoted by Jagannatha Dasa himself (2.54, "thus spoke Jagannatha Dasa"). Who speaks 2.55–2.57 — the Dasa or the author — is not stated; the text continues on the next page.
[^02-23]: The source of the shloka has not been established. Printed ଯଥା ଚ ଉପଦେଶତଃ (*yathā ca upadeśataḥ*, "as also from instruction"). The sense (2.52) would lead one to expect *vinā copadeśataḥ*, "without instruction"; the translation follows the sense.
[^02-24]: The source of the shloka has not been established. Similar verses on the power of diksha are cited in the *Hari-bhakti-vilasa* (ch. 2). ପଳାୟନ୍ତେ (*palāyante*, "flee") is a tentative reading: in print, after ପଳା stands a sign like ସୃ/ୟ. The Sanskrit is irregular: singular *pāpaṁ* with a plural verb. 2.56–2.57 are an Odia rendering of the shloka. ବାହାରଇ (*bāhāraï*, "goes out, departs") in 2.56 is uncertain.
[^02-25]: The sources of shlokas 2.58–2.59 have not been identified. 2.59 lists the smriti "great sins" (*mahā-pātaka*): killing a brahmana, drinking liquor and theft of gold (cf. *Manu-smriti* 11.55); *upapātaka* are the lesser sins. In print ସ୍ୟାଦ୍ (2.58) and ସ୍ତେୟାଦି (2.59) are indistinct, and ସୁରପାନ (2.59) is printed without *ā*. Gaudiya Vaishnavas also accept the purifying power of diksha (*Hari-bhakti-vilasa*, ch. 2; cf. note to 2.55). But for them "wealth and dharma" (2.58, 2.63) are not the goal: pure bhakti is free from the desire for fruits (*Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu* 1.1.11; CC, Madhya 19.149).
[^02-26]: The first word is printed ଶୁଣିମା (*śuṇimā*); it is taken as ଶୁଣିବା ହେଉ (*śuṇibā heu*, "let it be heard", i.e. "listen"). ପ୍ରକ୍ଷିନର (*prakṣinara*) is taken as ପରୀକ୍ଷଣର (*parīkṣaṇara*, "of the examination, testing"; gen.), from the sense of what follows. The line ends with a single thick stroke instead of "॥". The *Hari-bhakti-vilasa* (ch. 1) also speaks of how the disciple examines the guru and the guru the disciple.
[^02-27]: Cf. *Hari-bhakti-vilasa*, ch. 1, where the verse is cited as words of the *Padma Purana*: *mahā-kula-prasūto 'pi sarva-yajñeṣu dīkṣitaḥ / sahasra-śākhādhyāyī ca na guruḥ syād avaiṣṇavaḥ*. Instead of "initiated into all sacrifices", Divakara has "versed in all the shastras". The print has ସଂଖ୍ୟାଧ୍ୟାୟୂଞ୍ଚ, taken as *saṁkhyādhyāyī ca*. Gaudiya Vaishnavas fully accept this rule: the guru is one who knows the science of Krishna, not one of high birth (CC, Madhya 8.128; cf. note to 2.43). Yet Divakara's Mahaprabhu has just placed brahmana birth above initiation (2.43–2.46), while here the guru is required to be both a brahmana and a Vaishnava (2.69–2.70).
[^02-28]: The end of the shloka is not understood. The print has ସକୁଳାନ୍ବୈ (*sakulān vai*), and there is no verb. After 2.72 ("delivers a hundred [generations] of his family") one would expect *sakulāṁs tārayet* or *uddharet*; the translation follows the sense. The print has ଧୀରତା (*dhīrāḥ* expected). The first word of 2.73 is printed as a bold କୁ + ର (*kura*). It is taken as *kāra* ("whose, which") from the sense of 2.73–2.78; it may be a misprint. The source of the shloka has not been identified.
[^02-29]: A scheme of "the guru's caste → the world the disciple reaches" (2.73–2.79). Its source has not been identified; it is not found among the Gaudiya Vaishnavas. In the shloka a vaishya leads to the world of Prajapati, in the Odia rendering to Pitriloka (2.77). The second word of the first line is printed with a sign like ଉ/ଭ + ଦ୍ର (ଉଦ୍ର / ଭଦ୍ର). It is taken as ଇନ୍ଦ୍ର, "Indra[loka]", after 2.76 ("dwells together with the gods"). The cases are irregular (*kṣatriyaḥ, vaiśyo* for "by a kshatriya, by a vaishya"). The sense of 2.79 seems to be that this whole hierarchy applies to non-Vaishnava gurus, while from a Vaishnava [of any birth?] one obtains everything. This is close to the Gaudiya Vaishnava view: "Whether brahmana, sannyasi or shudra, whoever knows the science of Krishna is a guru" (CC, Madhya 8.128). But 2.70 requires the guru to be both a brahmana and a Vaishnava.
[^02-30]: 2.80–2.92 list those from whom initiation should not be taken: for bodily defects, childlessness, birth and occupation. The source has not been identified. The list resembles that of persons not to be invited to a shraddha (*Manu-smriti* 3.150–3.166: physicians, village priests, "one with bad nails and dark teeth", etc.). The Odia couplets 2.90–2.92 follow the order of shloka 2.80: sonless, *vṛṣaṇī* / *kuruṇḍā*, dwarf, nails, teeth. The list continues on the next page. Gaudiya Vaishnavas judge a guru by bhakti and knowledge of Krishna, not by body or birth (CC, Madhya 8.128). Srila Rupa Gosvami warns against seeing a devotee as an ordinary man because of defects of his body or nature (*Upadeshamrita* 6). The demands for moral purity and bhakti (2.82–2.84) do agree with this. The readings in 2.80–2.81 are uncertain. The sign after ଅପୁ (2.80) is unclear (ତ୍ରୋ / ନ୍ତୋ). *Vṛṣaṇī* is taken as "with a diseased scrotum" (*vṛṣaṇa*); cf. *kuruṇḍā* in 2.91. The word ସ୍ତ୍ରୀଆ (*strīā*; printed ସ୍ତ + subscript + ଅ + a broken sign) is not understood: perhaps *strījit*, "ruled by his wife". In ଶ୍ୟାବଦନ୍ତଶ୍ଚ the ୍ୟା is broken. କଷ୍ଟରୋଗୀ (*kaṣṭa-rogī*) and ଅସ୍ଥୈର୍ଯ୍ୟୋଽପି (*asthairyo 'pi*, "and the unsteady") in 2.81 are conjectural readings: the second sign of the latter resembles ସ୍ୱ.
[^02-31]: ଅସ୍ପର୍ଶଶ୍ଚ (*asparśaś ca*, "the untouchable") is a conjectural reading; cf. ଅପରଶ (*aparaśa*) in 2.41. The word କନସ୍ତୁଷ୍ଟ (*kanastuṣṭa*) is not identified. In 2.83 the print has ବଧିରଶ୍ଚେ and ପଙ୍ଗୁତା (*badhiraś caiva* and *paṅgukaḥ* expected). In 2.84 it has ଚପଳେ (*capalo* expected). In 2.85 ଅଶ୍ୱକ୍ରୟ, ପଶୁକ୍ରୟ (*aśva-kraya, paśu-kraya*, "trade in horses, in cattle") are read from the sense: the last sign is printed as ସ. The word after ଗ୍ରାମଯାଜକ is printed as ା + a sign with the ି arc + a sign like ଜ + a sign like ସ୍ଥ/ତ୍ସ + କାଃ. It is taken as ଚିକିତ୍ସକାଃ (*cikitsakāḥ*, "physicians"; cf. *Manu-smriti* 3.152). ଅସ୍ତ୍ରକାରଃ (*astra-kāraḥ*, "weapon-maker") is also conjectural.
[^02-32]: କୁଳକ୍ଷଣ (*kuḷakṣaṇa* = *ku-lakṣaṇa*, "bad marks") is read from the bold sign କୁ (see the introductory note to 2.58). The gurus of the list 2.80–2.85 are probably meant; "bear guilt" means that they sin by accepting disciples. ପ୍ରତେଜିବ (*pratejiba*, "will abandon") in 2.89: cf. 1.14 and 2.11.
[^02-33]: The couplet ends on this page; its sense continues on the next. ମରଥିବ is printed without ି (= ମରିଥିବ, "have withered"). ଦନ୍ତ (*danta*, "teeth") is printed with a small ନ୍ତ in a loop. The word ବେହଡ଼ା (*behaṛā*) is not identified (the OCR reads it the same way): perhaps "crooked, misshapen"; cf. *śyāva-danta*, "with dark teeth", in shloka 2.80. କୁରୁଣ୍ଡା (*kuruṇḍā*, 2.91) is understood through Skt. *kuraṇḍa*, "swelling (hydrocele) of the scrotum", by the parallel with *vṛṣaṇī* (2.80).
[^02-34]: The third word of 2.94 is not identified: printed କେମ୍ପା (*kempā*; କେ + ମ with a subscript sign + ା). Perhaps "trembling" (cf. *kampa*). ଶ୍ଲେଷ୍ମା (*śleṣmā*) is "phlegm", i.e. a disease of phlegm (cf. 2.102). ଗୋଦର (*godara*) is elephantiasis, swelling of the legs. These diseases apparently spell out the *kaṣṭa-rogī* of shloka 2.81. The Odia କୁବ୍ଜା (*kubjā*, "hunchbacked") in 2.93 raises the question whether shloka 2.80 should read *kubjaś ca* instead of *kuṣṭhaś ca*.
[^02-35]: A purohita is a family priest who performs rites for a fee. He is not in shlokas 2.80–2.85; cf. the "village priest" (2.110). The last word is printed ଧାତ (*dhāta*). It is tentatively taken as the interjection "away!" (cf. Bengali and Hindi *dhat*). The first sign was checked against ଧ in ଧର୍ମ (2.100).
[^02-36]: Printed ପର ଦାସକୁ (*para dāsaku*, "at another's servant"). Corrected to ପରଦାରକୁ (*paradāraku*, "at another's wife") after shloka 2.84: *paradārarataḥ*, "an adulterer". The third sign is a clear ସ; the ସ/ର trap.
[^02-37]: ବୈଦ୍ୟପଣଟି (*baidya-paṇa-ṭi*, "the physician's trade" with the definite particle *‑ṭi*) corresponds to *cikitsakāḥ* in shloka 2.85. The last sign of the word is indistinct. "One who, serving, carries out orders" (*sebā kari ājñā bahi*) apparently renders the word of shloka 2.85 that was read there as ଅସ୍ତ୍ରକାରଃ (*astra-kāraḥ*). This may favour the reading *ājñā-kāraḥ*, "one who carries out orders". In 2.112 the word before ପରଷନ୍ତି (*paraṣanti*, "serve out [food]") is not identified: printed ସ + a ligature like ନ୍ନ (ସନ୍ନ). The sense would lead one to expect ଅନ୍ନ (*anna*, "cooked food"): the line renders *sūpakāra*, "cook", from shloka 2.85.
[^02-38]: 2.114–2.118 are an objection and its answer. Even if such people are absorbed in the Name, learned and intent on bhakti, their earlier faults make them unfit to be gurus until they give them up. The reading of 2.116 is uncertain. କିପାଁ (*kipā̐*) is taken as *kimpā*, "why". In ଶୋହିଲେ (*śohile*, "befitted, did honour to") the sign ହି is the same as in ନୋହିଲେ; perhaps ଖୋଜିଲେ (*khojile*), "did [not] seek the guru's feet". Gaudiya Vaishnavas also require purity of conduct from a guru. But Srila Rupa Gosvami directs that a devotee absorbed in the Name is not to be judged by his body or nature (*Upadeshamrita* 6). Cf. also *Bhagavad-gita* 9.30: "even if one commits the most abominable acts, if he worships Me exclusively he is to be considered a sadhu".
[^02-39]: The simile of the seed: the mantra is a good seed (*drabya*, "a valuable substance"). Cast into "scorched ground" (an unworthy guru), it will not sprout. The second half of 2.121 (*uttama bhakti drabya yantra*) is understood tentatively. ବୀଜ (*bīja*) in 2.119 is printed with a broken ୀ. ବଅର୍ଥ (2.122, 2.124) is the printed spelling of *byartha*, "futile". The example of the brahmana who ate at a chandala's house (2.123–2.124) expresses the caste rule of purity in food. Gaudiya Vaishnavas measure purity by bhakti, not by birth: cf. the note to 2.43 and CC, Madhya 8.128. In 2.125 there is a dot over ଯେ (ଂ/ଁ?).
[^02-40]: Here the author (through Jagannatha Dasa) says that a guru may be a person of any varna, ashrama or sex, if he worships Hari single-mindedly and knows the prema of Radha and Krishna. This is close to the Gaudiya Vaishnava rule: "Whether one is a brahmana, a sannyasi or a shudra — whoever knows the science of Krishna is a guru" (CC, Madhya 8.128). But it contradicts 2.69–2.70 (the guru must be a brahmana-Vaishnava) and the scheme of 2.73–2.79 ("the guru's caste → the disciple's world"). ନିସ୍ପୃହେ (*nispṛhe*, "free from desire") is read tentatively: the sign ୃ stands under ହ. ସ୍ତିରୀ (*stirī*, 2.130) = *strī*, "woman".
[^02-41]: "The twelve [kinds of] prema" are apparently the twelve rasas (flavours of relationship with Krishna): five primary (shanta, dasya, sakhya, vatsalya, madhurya) and seven secondary (*Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu*, parts 2–4; CC, Madhya 19). But this is only a guess: in the Odia tradition of the Panchasakha the count may differ. "Kishori and Kishora" are Radha and Krishna; "the Couple" (*yugaḷa*) is the Divine Couple.
[^02-42]: "Beginning with [the name] Rama" (*rāmādi*): apparently the mahamantra in the Odia order — "Hare Rama Hare Rama Rama Rama Hare Hare / Hare Krishna Hare Krishna Krishna Krishna Hare Hare". "The two kings of mantras" are probably its two halves (or the names of Rama and Krishna). Cf. VL 6.1–6.18: the dispute "Hare Rama" or "Hare Krishna" and Mahaprabhu's decision "Hare Rama for Nilachala" (notes to VL 6.1 and 6.15). Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, chant the mahamantra beginning with "Hare Krishna". The word ରାମାଦି is read from the sign ରା, which looks like ସ୍ୱ; likewise in 2.140.
[^02-43]: 2.137 shows that the whole discourse on diksha and the guru (from 2.52–2.53) is Jagannatha Dasa's speech before Mahaprabhu; cf. the notes to 2.52 and 2.58. In 2.138 he asks Mahaprabhu for the Name; cf. 2.52 ("I have no initiation"). ପଦ୍ମର (*padmara*, 2.136) is printed as ପ + ଦ with a subscript sign; perhaps ପଦର (*padara*), "of the feet".
[^02-44]: "Matta Balarama" is Balarama Dasa, the eldest of the Panchasakha, called Matta ("intoxicated [with prema]"), author of the Odia *Jagamohana Ramayana*; cf. VL 5.131 and its note. According to the tradition Divakara gives (the guru-pranali 1.42–1.45; Chapter 23), Jagannatha Dasa was initiated by Balarama Dasa. Here Mahaprabhu Himself entrusts this to him. Grammatically ବଳରାମେ (*baḷarāme*) could also be an agentive ("Balarama said"), but by the sense of 2.140 the speaker is the Prabhu. The episode is not in the CC or CB, and neither Balarama Dasa nor Jagannatha Dasa is mentioned there. In the Gaudiya Vaishnava view Mahaprabhu is Krishna Himself, the giver of the Name (CC, Adi 3.77–3.78; CC, Adi 7). On His own initiation from Ishvara Puri see the note to 2.45.
[^02-45]: The famous shloka of the *Brihan-naradiya Purana* (38.126), quoted in CC, Adi 17.21. The usual reading of the first line is *harer nāmaiva kevalam*, "the Name of Hari alone". Here it reads *nāmaiva śaraṇaṁ param*, "the Name alone is the supreme refuge". Continued on the next page.
[^02-46]: On Balarama Dasa and the epithet "Matta" see the note to 2.139. The first word is printed ଯେ + ହୁ + the sign ଟ with the stroke ି (ଯେହୁଟି, "the one who"; cf. ଯେହୁ in 2.164). The same ଟ appears in କଟି (2.154). ଦୟା ("of mercy", 2.143) is a tentative reading: the print looks like ଦବା. The plural in 2.144–2.146 ("they know", "they remain") is honorific and refers to Balarama. In 2.146 the print has ସେମ; it is taken as ପ୍ରେମ ("prema").
[^02-47]: Whose prema and bhakti "the Merciful One" sees is not stated. Perhaps Balarama sees the bhakti of Jagannatha Dasa. Another reading is also possible: Mahaprabhu sees Balarama's bhakti and therefore entrusts the initiation to him. ସିଦ୍ଧି କରି (2.148, "having performed siddhi") is understood tentatively; perhaps a preliminary rite is meant. The sign ସି differs from ଶୁ in ଶୁଦ୍ଧି (2.155).
[^02-48]: 2.149–2.154 describe the Vaishnava marks with which the guru endows the disciple. They are the "Hari-mandira" tilaka ("temple of Hari") from the middle of the nose up to the hair, tilaka on the chest and on other parts of the body, kanthi beads of lotus seeds, tulasi and dhatri (amalaki), and stamped marks of conch and disc. Gaudiya Vaishnavas know the same marks: the twelve urdhva-pundra tilakas, the "temple of Hari" on the forehead, tulasi beads, and the mudras of conch and disc (*Hari-bhakti-vilasa*, ch. 4). The last word of 2.150 is printed ଶୋ + ହ with a subscript (like ହୁ); it is taken as ଶୋହଇ ("shines"). ଧାତ୍ରୀ (2.151) is read from the sense: the second sign resembles ତ୍ରୀ/ଙ୍ଗୀ.
[^02-49]: The reading of 2.153–2.154 is unclear in places. ତାଲୁରେ is printed ତା + ଲୁ (the ଲ was checked against ପୁଲକିତ, 2.146; it differs from ହୁ in ବାହୁର, 2.152). It is taken as ତାଳୁରେ, "on the crown of the head (?)"; possibly a misprint for ବାହୁରେ, "on the arms". The word କଠାଗର is unidentified. Possible readings are କଣ୍ଠାଗ୍ର, "the front of the throat", or ଜଠର, "the belly". The translation leaves it blank. The "fourteen [places]" (2.154) differ from the usual twelve places of tilaka (*Hari-bhakti-vilasa*, ch. 4: forehead, belly, chest, throat, sides, arms, back, nape). Srivatsa and the moon as marks on the body are not mentioned by Gaudiyas.
[^02-50]: 2.155–2.157 describe the rite of diksha: purifying the seat, the sankalpa (a vow naming place and time), invoking the rishi and chandas of the mantra, kara-nyasa and anga-nyasa (placing the syllables of the mantra on the hands and limbs), and meditation. Diksha in the *Hari-bhakti-vilasa* (ch. 2) is arranged the same way. But the meditation here is on the form of Sri Jagannatha. The string and kaupina (*ḍora-kaupīna*) are the dress of a renunciant. It seems that Jagannatha Dasa also receives the renunciant's dress (*bheka*). This is not stated plainly: ଆରମ୍ଭିଲେ ("began, commenced") is understood tentatively. The last sign of କୌପିନ is printed as ନ with a dark subscript. Madhava also tells of Jagannatha Dasa's initiation by Balarama Dasa at Mahaprabhu's order (VL 7.1–7.18: "Take diksha — go to Balarama"). But there the rite is not described, and Svami (Jagannatha Dasa) goes to Balarama himself; he does not receive diksha "in the presence of Mahaprabhu". The episode is not in the CC or CB (see the note to 2.139).
[^02-51]: What Jagannatha Dasa receives (2.159–2.163): instruction in the Name (*nāma upadeśa*), the "king of mantras", the "sixteen [names] of prema", the "Gayatri of the Couple" Radha and Krishna, the mantra of Thakura and Thakurani (Radha and Krishna), and the "five names of Sri Jagannatha". The "sixteen [names]" are apparently the maha-mantra of sixteen names (*ṣoḍaśakaṁ nāmnām* of the *Kali-santarana Upanishad*). On the "king of mantras" cf. 2.135 and the note to it. Gaudiya Vaishnavas give the Gopala mantra and the kama-gayatri at diksha, and the Name at harinama initiation (*Hari-bhakti-vilasa*, ch. 2). "Five names of Jagannatha" are unknown in their practice; what these names are is unclear. ନାମାଦି (2.159, "beginning with the Name") is printed with a clear ନା, not ରା as in ରାମାଦି in 2.140. ସୁରସ (2.159) may be ସ୍ୱରସ.
[^02-52]: The sense of 2.164–2.165 seems to be this. At that time no one knew the maha-mantra beginning with the name of Krishna ("Hare Krishna…"): all followed the Veda and Brahma's command. In the *Kali-santarana Upanishad*, Brahma gives Narada the maha-mantra beginning with "Hare Rama". Cf. 2.135, 2.140 ("beginning with [the name] Rama") and VL 6.1–6.18. Gaudiya Vaishnavas chant the maha-mantra beginning with "Hare Krishna", as Mahaprabhu Himself gave it (CB, Madhya 23). For more, see the note to 2.135.
[^02-53]: The author's signature (*bhaṇitā*). The first sign of ଆଶ has the hook of ଆ; it is taken as ଆଶ, "hope, reliance". ଅଂଶ ("share") is also possible: "in such ones Divakara Dasa has a share". Cf. ଆଶେ / ଅଂଶେ in 2.105.
[^02-54]: *Kuḷa-mantra* is the mantra of [one's own] line (lineage). 2.169–2.171 are a warning: this mantra is one's own, and one should not go to someone who shows the mantra of another line and leads a person away. Against whom the warning is aimed is not said. If the "other" mantra is the "Hare Krishna" order (2.164), the warning may be aimed at the Gaudiyas. Gaudiya Vaishnavas recognise one line of Mahaprabhu, the Brahma-Madhva-Gaudiya sampradaya, and consider the "ativadi" an apasampradaya (Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati; see Ch. 1, the note to 1.1). The sense of 2.171a is unclear. Perhaps it means "association [with such a one] is like [dangerous] association with a young woman". Another reading is also possible: "[the mantra] should be kept close, like a young [wife]". ପ୍ରାୟେ ("like") is read clearly; the syntax is what is unclear.
[^02-55]: The colophon is set in two lines, as at the end of Chapter 1 (see the note to the colophon of Ch. 1). Chapter 2 bears the same name as Chapter 1, "Anukramani"; the print has ଅନୁକର୍ମଣି, taken as ଅନୁକ୍ରମଣି. Instead of "Jagannatha Dasa" (Ch. 1), it has "Jagannatha Gosain". Below the colophon there is a printer's ornament.

---

# Divakara Dasa. *Jagannatha-charitamrita*, Chapter 3
## Jagannatha Dasa Serves Sri Chaitanya; Mahaprabhu Asks about His Former Form


*Chapter Three*

**3.1.** Listen attentively, kinsfolk, to the deeds of Atibada Gosain.  
**3.2.** Thus some days passed. And that Jagannatha Dasa,  
**3.3.** day by day, with ever-new love, served Sri Chaitanya with devotion.[^03-1]  
**3.4.** Rising at dawn (?), [he offered Him] full prostration, and gave [Him water] to wash His holy face;  
**3.5.** he cleaned the bed for sleeping (?); [he gave] clay to cleanse the holy hands;  
**3.6.** the tooth-stick for cleaning the teeth; the bath, the rubbing down, drinking water;  
**3.7.** sprinkling the holy body; putting on new cloth;  
**3.8.** the daily duties of sannyasa; cleaning the staff and kamandalu.  
**3.9.** All such tasks — service to Prabhu — were [his] daily duty;  
**3.10.** [also] the darshan of Jagannatha, and the routine of serving [Prabhu] in [His] retinue.  
**3.11.** Seeing this service, Chaitanya put a question to Dasa:  
**3.12.** "O Dasa, seeing your service, We have become very happy at heart.  
**3.13.** About your former divine form — tell Me that story."[^03-2]  
**3.14.** Hearing this command, Dasa was amazed and rejoiced.  
**3.15.** That (?) Lord is the embodiment of bhakti, the lamp (?) … (??) of the Kali age.[^03-3]  
**3.16.** "[You are] the Purusha, the Lord of all (?); nothing is hidden from You.  

## Jagannatha Dasa's Account of His Former Form; Mahaprabhu Calls Him Atibada; the Vaishnavas Murmur


*Shloka*  
**3.17.** *[Skt.]* "The hidden (?) supreme abode (?) of Vishnu is the glorious, wondrous Vaikuntha; it spreads out [like] a lotus with its pericarp — this is declared a secret.[^03-4]  
**3.18.** Above it, on a golden throne, adorned with a jewelled pavilion, in the pericarp is the great abode where Govinda dwells (?), great and imperishable.
