**3.19.** Its southern petal is called the most secret of secrets, the highest. Then [He] entered the rasa on the great throne, which is hard to reach.  
**3.20.** In its midst dwells Krishna, intent on embracing Radha; with one self, with mind and intellect, He manifests (?) the eternal lila."  
**3.21.** "In eternal Vaikuntha, [His] own abode, Bhagavan [was] together … (??) [with Radha].[^03-5]  
**3.22.** [They] abode as one self, and with great happiness cast a glance [at each other].  
**3.23.** Seeing Radha's face, the Life of the world laughed.[^03-6]  
**3.24.** That laughter by which crores upon crores of worlds are born in a single moment —  
**3.25.** from that laughter You appeared (lit. "were born"), and You stand in front of the Lord.  
**3.26.** From Radha's laughter I was born, and I stand behind You.  
**3.27.** Looking [at You], [the Lord] gave You the command: 'Becoming incarnate in the mortal world —  
**3.28.** this age of Kali is mighty (?) — perform the kirtan of Hari's Name.'  
**3.29.** You make the unconscious conscious; therefore Your name is Chaitanya.[^03-7]  
**3.30.** Then Sri Krishna, looking at me, said: 'Be born on earth.[^03-8]  
**3.31.** You will worship this form, and at the end you will attain that same goal.'  
**3.32.** Therefore, apart from the love of the Couple, my mind finds taste in nothing else."  
**3.33.** Hearing this, Sri Chaitanya became absorbed in meditation (?) on His own abode.[^03-9]  
**3.34.** Attaining recognition (?) of the former form, the Gosain rejoiced.  
**3.35.** He removed His own wrap (??) from His sacred body with His sacred hand[^03-10]  
**3.36.** and tied it on Dasa's head, saying: "[You are] very great (ati bada)!  
**3.37.** You have told this most rare account today before Me.  

*Shloka*  
**3.38.** *[Skt.]* "Hard to attain even for yogis, true, accessible [only] to the Purusha (?)."[^03-11]  
**3.39.** You have told a very great account; therefore you have become Atibada."  
**3.40.** [Since then] the world calls Dasa "Sri Atibada Jagannatha":  
**3.41.** having heard it from Mahaprabhu's mouth, everyone speaks this word.  
**3.42.** Thus some days passed. Sri Chaitanya in Purushottama  
**3.43.** every day, with an affectionate heart, constantly calls [him] Atibada.  
**3.44.** When [He] called [him] Atibada, the Vaishnavas grieved at heart.[^03-12]  
**3.45.** Looking at the Odia brahmana, [they] said: "You (?) are Atibada!"  
**3.46.** "Until today we have served, and all of us have fallen to a lower position.  
**3.47.** If we stay with Him, we shall hear nothing but such reproach."  
**3.48.** He indeed is the Lord over all (?); He accomplishes everything [by a mere] word.  

*Shloka*  
**3.49.** *[Skt.]* "The Maker, the maker of the two makers, the cause of the cause of makers; God is all-pervading — what then can I think of Him?"[^03-13]  
**3.50.** Such is the glory of the Lord that there is no gainsaying it (?):  
**3.51.** what is done He makes undone, what is not done He does;  
**3.52.** at times He establishes what is done, at times He does not do what is not done;  
**3.53.** at times He delivers great sinners, at times He destroys the pious.  
**3.54.** Since He abides in all bodies, He is called the greatest of all.  
**3.55.** Every day Sri Chaitanya calls [Dasa] by the name Atibada.  
**3.56.** Thus some days passed; the name "Atibada" took hold (?).[^03-14]  
**3.57.** Unable to bear [it], the devotees went and reported [it] before the Prabhu.  

## The Vaishnavas Leave for Yajanagara; Mahaprabhu's Letter "More Humble than a Blade of Grass"; the Lord Goes After Them


**3.58.** "You brought an Odia brahmin and called [him] by the title 'Atibada' (?).  
**3.59.** All the servants have become small, [while] his name is 'Atibada'."  
**3.60.** Sri Chaitanya commanded: "Know [this place] as Purushottama.[^03-15]  

*Shloka*  
**3.61.** *[Skt.]* 'Even from insects, moths, wood, stones and shells (?) [here] the best (?) on earth is born — what then to speak of a human!'  
**3.62.** The pebbles (?) and stones of this Kshetra, the shells (??) and the wood — regard them thus:  
**3.63.** for other realms they are gods. What then to say of the people!  
**3.64.** Take this into account: the word 'Atibada' is not at all too great for the Dasa.  
**3.65.** As many men and women as there are [here], [all] attain bhakti without sadhana:  
**3.66.** it enters [them] of itself; in other realms this is unattainable."  
**3.67.** Hearing such words from the Prabhu's mouth, all the Vaishnavas were aggrieved.  
**3.68.** They deliberated (?) in their hearts: "It is not proper to stay [here]."  
**3.69.** So they all decided, and cast away their attachment to the Prabhu.  
**3.70.** In despair over the title 'Atibada', they gave up hope of the best of kshetras.  
**3.71.** They left Purushottama and went away, saying: "[We go] to Yajanagara."[^03-16]  
**3.72.** For six months they stayed there. Thus Chaitanya Gosain,  
**3.73.** together with six Vaishnavas, remained in Purushottama.[^03-17]  
**3.74.** They worshipped (?) Jagannatha and, [apart from] service, knew nothing else.  
**3.75.** With affection for the host of devotees in His heart, [the Lord] bore compassion for them deep in His heart.  
**3.76.** [There was] a Vaishnava named Haridasa, dearest to all the mahantas.[^03-18]  
**3.77.** The Merciful One sent him: "Go and bring all the Vaishnavas."  
**3.78.** He wrote a shloka in a letter and gave [it] into Haridasa's hand.  

*Shloka*  
**3.79.** *[Skt.]* 'Being more humble than a blade of grass, more tolerant than a tree, seeking no honour (?) and giving honour [to others], one should always chant [the name of] Hari.'[^03-19]  
**3.80.** At the command Haridasa went and entered Yajanagara.  
**3.81.** Paying respects to all, he conveyed (?) [the message] to the assembly in detail.  
**3.82.** With the consent of all the mahantas, he handed over the letter before [them].  
**3.83.** Jagadananda Chakravarti reads out in the assembly the words of the holy mouth:[^03-20]  
**3.84.** "One should be lower than a blade of grass, endure everything like a tree,  
**3.85.** not rejoice at honour, not grieve at dishonour:  
**3.86.** then a Vaishnava becomes perfect. Where there is division, bhakti does not hold."[^03-21]  
**3.87.** Hearing this, the Vaishnavas were pleased and said, looking at Haridasa:  
**3.88.** "If He has affection for us, then let Him [Himself] take [us] away from here.  
**3.89.** Without seeing Chaitanya's feet, we shall not go to the best of kshetras again."  
**3.90.** Hearing [this], Haridasa went and told these words to the Prabhu.  
**3.91.** Hearing it, Chaitanya Gosain, looking at the Atibada Dasa,  
**3.92.** said: "Let us go to Yajapura and bring back all the Vaishnavas."[^03-22]  
**3.93.** Jagannatha Dasa said: "Because of me they have all taken offence.  
**3.94.** How shall I go there? There will be anger in their hearts."  
**3.95.** Hearing this, the Prabhu commanded, uniting [him] with the gods (?):[^03-23]  
**3.96.** "Our word will stand, [and] between them and you there will be love."  
**3.97.** Together with the six (?) Vaishnavas, the Prabhu went to Yajanagara.  
**3.98.** Arriving in Yajanagara, where the host of Vaishnavas was, He met [them].  
**3.99.** Hearing [of Him], the Vaishnavas were pleased and went out some distance to receive [Him] (?).  
**3.100.** Welcome, greeting, feasting — thus some days passed.  
**3.101.** One day Chaitanya joyfully called [him]: "Atibada Dasa!"  
**3.102.** Hearing the former word, all the Vaishnavas grieved:  
**3.103.** "The very words on account of which [we went] so far — the same reply He has spoken now.  
**3.104.** If we go to Purushottama, we shall hear only these words."[^03-24]  

## The Vaishnavas Leave for Vishnupur; Mahaprabhu's Letter about Purushottama; the Gaudiyas Go to Vrindavana


**3.105.** "Let us leave the company of the Odia and go to the land of Gauda," [they] said.  
**3.106.** Looking at Chaitanya, they said: "Truly, [one should] not stay in one kingdom.[^03-25]  
**3.107.** [Take] bath at Gaya (?), in the Ganga, [at] the sea — make a pilgrimage to the tirthas!"  
**3.108.** Hearing these words, Sri Chaitanya — this is how He spoke:  

*Shloka*  
**3.109.** *[Skt.]* "Attachment to Krishna, mind on Krishna, wandering (?) along every bank of the Ganga — [yet] in life and at departure [from the body] [My] refuge is the Lord of Nilachala."[^03-26]  
**3.110.** "My mind, intellect and feeling seek refuge with Jagannatha.  
**3.111.** Whether I live or depart [from this world], apart from Jagannatha I have no other.  
**3.112.** Why then do you grieve at heart? This is Atibada Jagannatha.[^03-27]  
**3.113.** From My [word] there will be no other release (?): this befits the very great one."  
**3.114.** Hearing these words, the Vaishnavas then left the Prabhu and went away.  
**3.115.** Coming to Bana-Vishnupur, they set up a matha there and stayed [there].[^03-28]  
**3.116.** The Prabhu graciously returned and remained in Purushottama.  
**3.117.** And at the end of a year all the Vaishnavas, pure in heart,  
**3.118.** because they had no darshan of Purushottama, were dejected (?) at heart.  
**3.119.** They chose (?) one Vaishnava [and] said: "You will tell the Prabhu:  
**3.120.** [we have had] darshan of the holy places and bathing in the holy places."  
**3.121.** Hearing [this], the Vaishnava set out and bowed down to the Prabhu.  
**3.122.** Seeing [him], Sri Chaitanya asked: "Are the chief Vaishnavas well?"  
**3.123.** That Madho Dasa speaks: "By the grace of Your feet,[^03-29]  
**3.124.** all is well, there is success in all; they practise bhakti to perfection."  
**3.125.** Hearing [this], the Prabhu was joyful: from prema (?) His tears flowed.[^03-30]  
**3.126.** Thus, after some days, Madho Dasa speaks before [the Prabhu]:  
**3.127.** "The Vaishnavas say: [let Him] make a pilgrimage to the tirthas.  
**3.128.** [You] have stayed in the Odia kingdom, [You] have taken to observing Odia practices."[^03-31]  
**3.129.** Hearing [this], Sri Chaitanya, lovingly embracing the Vaishnava, said:  
**3.130.** "Madho Dasa, go and tell [them], and give this letter there."  

*Shloka*  
**3.131.** *[Skt.]* "I have seen the city of Kashi and the Yamuna, beheld (?) the nearness of Madhupuri, seen Gangadvara, many roads (?) … (??) the city of Ayodhya. But in Sri Purushottama — remembering, remembering day and night the world-enchanting form of the Foe of Mura, my mind stays nowhere [else]."[^03-32]  
**3.132.** That Madho Dasa took the command and gave [the letter] in the assembly of Vaishnavas.  
**3.133.** In it was written thus: "I have seen Kashi,  
**3.134.** Madhupura, the town of cowherds, Vrindavana, the city of Dvaraka,  
**3.135.** Gangadvara, the confluence of the Ganga, Ayodhya, Badarikashrama.  
**3.136.** In these Krishna formerly abode; at the end of the yuga He left them.  
**3.137.** Leaving all the kshetras and tirthas, Jagannatha in Nila-kandara[^03-33]  
**3.138.** enchants the world by [His] form — therefore I have taken shelter [in Him].  
**3.139.** As long as I live [I shall be] here: I will never leave this [place]."  
**3.140.** Hearing these words, the Vaishnavas all deliberated again:  
**3.141.** "We thought in our hearts: the Son of Shachi, showing mercy,  
**3.142.** will abolish the title 'Atibada', [and] He (?) will take [us] to Purushottama.[^03-34]  
**3.143.** We shall not go to Purushottama, then! By what shelter would we gain bhakti [there]?  
**3.144.** The former place of Govinda's lila — let us go away to Sri Vrindavana."[^03-35]  
**3.145.** [Though] this word [is given] in the Vedas and Puranas, they rejected [it — took up] another injunction.  
**3.146.** Whatever customs Purushottama had — those matters they made into another custom.  
**3.147.** They abandoned "Hare Rama Krishna" and began to say "Hare Krishna Rama".[^03-36]  
**3.148.** They cast away the honouring of nirmalya [and] began to worship (?) the dust of Vrindavana.[^03-37]  

## The Gaudiyas in Vrindavana; Rupa and Sanatana at the Gundicha; End of the Chapter


**3.149.** Leaving bathing in the sea, they turned their minds to the waters of the Yamuna;  
**3.150.** leaving the form of Jagannatha, [they turned their] love to Madana-mohana.[^03-38]  
**3.151.** They cast off the 'Hari-mandira' [tilaka] [and] took to honouring (?) a black (?) tilaka.[^03-39]  
**3.152.** Leaving the hope of Purushottama, they settled in Vrindavana.  
**3.153.** They cast off acts by rule [and] took to worshipping Krishna by the path of raga.[^03-40]  
**3.154.** They reasoned in their minds: "Bhagavan is subject to bhakti.  
**3.155.** Since Hari is subject to feeling, He delivers and takes away [one] who thinks [of Him].  
**3.156.** He is pleased by the devotee's feeling (?) [and] for feeling grants the hard-to-attain abode.  
**3.157.** Where devotees dwell — know that [place] as Purushottama."[^03-41]  
**3.158.** Having reasoned thus in their minds, they took up residence in Vrindavana.  
**3.159.** Circling the kshetra, they wander round the hill of Govardhana,[^03-42]  
**3.160.** bathe in the Yamuna, [have] darshan of the kadamba tree.  
**3.161.** Thus the glory of the six Gosains became manifest there.[^03-43]  
**3.162.** Hearing this news, Sri Chaitanya, looking (?) at Mathura Dasa,  
**3.163.** said [to him]: "Take the order, go [and] bring [them]" (?).[^03-44]  
**3.164.** Mathura Dasa set off and arrived in Vrindavana.  
**3.165.** He told [them] Prabhu's orders. Hearing [them], Rupa [and] Sanatana  
**3.166.** came to the Gundicha-yatra together (?) with twenty companions.  
**3.167.** They saw Chaitanya Gosain pushing the chariot with [His] head.[^03-45]  
**3.168.** They made full prostrations [to Him], and Prabhu Himself embraced [them].  
**3.169.** Overwhelmed by prema, they did not know their own from others.  
**3.170.** First — Prabhu Nityananda; Sri Sanatana — [full of] joy (?);[^03-46]  
**3.171.** Sri Rupa Gosain; Sri Raghunatha Dasa — [also] there;  
**3.172.** Sri Jiva Gosain; Sri Gopala Dasa — [also] abiding [with them].  
**3.173.** [These are] the six Vaishnavas of liberation (?). Who will write more of that?  
**3.174.** Having had darshan of Jagannatha, [they] go to Vrindavana.  
**3.175.** Every year they come [and] serve in the Gundicha procession;[^03-47]  
**3.176.** having completed the darshan of Gundicha, they go again to Vrindavana.  
**3.177.** Sri Chaitanya showed [them] honour, [but] they did not take it to heart.  
**3.178.** Whoever stays near [something great] does not esteem it, though it be great.[^03-48]  
**3.179.** [Bhrigu], not acknowledging [Him] as Vishnu, struck the Srivatsa with his foot;  
**3.180.** Nanda beat [Krishna], seizing Him by the tuft of hair; the cowherd boys leapt [onto Him].  
**3.181.** [Those who] share the company of the Lord — [such] devotees do not speak of awe (??).[^03-49]  
**3.182.** Whatever and wherever the devotees speak, there the Lord is found as such.  
**3.183.** With them Sri Chaitanya, saying nothing at all, [remained] silent.  
**3.184.** Offended at the title 'Atibada', they returned (?) [and] went to Vrindavana.[^03-50]  
**3.185.** But this Sri Chaitanya Gosain remains abiding in Purushottama;  
**3.186.** He chants 'Hare Rama Krishna', bearing love for Sri Jagannatha.[^03-51]  
**3.187.** Says the brahmin Divakara: "At Chaitanya's feet is my head.  
**3.188.** That Prabhu is the ocean of bhakti: in the boat of bhakti He will carry [us] across."  

*Thus [ends], in the Sri Jagannathamrita, [the life of] Sri Ati Bada Jagannatha, the third chapter, called 'Anukramani' (?).*[^03-52]

[^03-1]: 3.3–3.10 present Jagannatha Dasa as Mahaprabhu's personal servant. He carries out the Lord's morning routine: the prostration, washing the face, cleaning the bed, clay for the hands, the tooth-stick, the bath and change of cloth, cleaning the sannyasi's staff and kamandalu. He also accompanies the Lord to the darshan of Jagannatha. According to the CC, Mahaprabhu's personal servant in Puri was Govinda, together with Kashishvara (CC, Madhya 10; cf. MP 1.95 and the note to it). Jagannatha Dasa is not mentioned in the CC or CB (cf. the note to VL 9.63). ପ୍ରଭାତୁଁ (*prabhātu̐*, "from dawn", 3.4) is a tentative reading: the second sign is filled with ink and resembles ଭା/ସ୍ୱ. ମୃତିକା (*mṛtikā*, 3.5) = *mṛttikā*, "clay". *Karāi … dhaüta* (3.4) is understood as "giving [the Lord water] to wash the face". ଗହଣ ଖଟିବା (*gahaṇa khaṭibā*, 3.10) means "to serve in the retinue, to attend".
[^03-2]: The "former divine form" (*pūrba dibya aṅga*) is apparently a question about who Jagannatha Dasa was in Krishna's eternal pastimes. The answer apparently follows on the next page. Cf. 1.27 and 1.75 (he is a part of Radha) and 1.61, 1.68 (he was born from Radha's laughter). Establishing Jagannatha Dasa's pre-eminence is a running theme of the Atibadi line; for the Gaudiya Vaishnava position see the note to 2.22. Mahaprabhu speaks of Himself as "We" (*hoilu̐*, 1st pers. pl., 3.12), the honorific plural.
[^03-3]: The reading and sense of 3.15 are unclear. The first sign is filled with ink and taken as ସେ, "that". If so, 3.15 is the author's description of Mahaprabhu, and Jagannatha Dasa's speech begins in 3.16 ("nothing is hidden from You"). Another possibility is that Dasa already begins to answer in 3.15. The word ଅଙ୍ଗ (*aṅga*, "body, limb") is unclear in sense; ଅନ୍ଧ (*andha*) would be expected: "a lamp for the blind of the Kali age". ପ୍ରଦୀପ (*pradīpa*, "lamp") is read from the indistinct signs ପ୍ର + ଦୀ + ପ. ସର୍ବଙ୍କ (*sarbaṅka*, "of all", 3.16): the third sign is smudged. Gaudiya Vaishnavas also worship Mahaprabhu as the Supreme Lord, Krishna Himself (CC, Adi 1–2). The speech continues on the next page.
[^03-4]: 3.17–3.20 are eight lines (four shlokas in anushtubh metre) after the mark "Shloka". The supreme abode is described as a lotus: in the pericarp, a golden throne under a jewelled pavilion and Govinda's abode; the southern petal is the most secret; in the centre Krishna, embraced by Radha, manifests the eternal lila. The source has not been found. The imagery is close to *Brahma-samhita* 5.2–5.5: Gokula is a thousand-petalled lotus, and its pericarp is Krishna's abode. The reading of the first words of 3.17 is uncertain. ବିଷ୍ଣୁ: the first sign is ବ with a lower stroke, as in ବିସ୍ତାରଂ. ଛଦ୍ମ: the first sign is round with a ring below, like ଛ in ପଛରେ (3.26); the OCR gives ବଞ୍ଚ ର୍ଷ. ପରଧାନ: the last sign is ନ, not ମ; taken as *para-dhāma*, "supreme abode" (or *pradhāna*, "chief"). ମଦ୍ଭୁତଂ = *-m adbhutam*. In 3.18 the signs ସ୍ଥ and ୟ look alike (ଗୋବିନ୍ଦସ୍ଥଂ, ମହାବ୍ୟୟଂ); ପ୍ରକାଶୟେତ୍ (3.20) is a tentative reading. As in 1.62, Divakara places Radha and Krishna in Vaikuntha (see the note to 1.62). For Gaudiya Vaishnavas, Goloka-Vrindavana is above Vaikuntha (CC, Adi 5).
[^03-5]: Printed ରାବେ (*rābe*): the first sign is ରା or ଚା with a tail (see the note to 3.17), the second a clear ବେ. No word with this meaning exists. Possibly a misprint for ରାଧେ (*rādhe*, "with Radha"). ଭାବେ (*bhābe*, "in bhava") is also possible, but ଭା in the next line (ଭାବରେଣ, 3.22) looks different. The translation follows the context; cf. 1.62–1.63: "Sri Radha and Krishna on one seat… abide in a single bhava".
[^03-6]: 3.23–3.30 repeat the account of 1.61–1.75, but here it is put in the mouth of Jagannatha Dasa himself, as in VL 5.113–5.121. Sri Chaitanya appeared from Krishna's laughter (3.24–3.25), Jagannatha Dasa of Radha's laughter (3.26); both are sent to earth (3.27, 3.30). On this Atibadi teaching and the Gaudiya Vaishnava position see the notes to 1.27 and 1.61 and to VL 5.117. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas, Sri Chaitanya is Krishna Himself, who has taken Radha's mood and complexion (CC, Adi 1.5; Adi 4). He descends Himself to give the Name (CC, Adi 3); He is not born of Krishna's laughter. "The Lord" (*prabhu*) in 3.25 is Krishna. "In front of the Lord… behind You" (3.25–3.26) apparently describes their order in the eternal abode.
[^03-7]: An explanation of the name: "You make the unconscious (*aceta*) conscious (*caitanya*)". According to CB (Madhya 28), the name "Krishna-Chaitanya" was given to the Lord at His sannyasa by Keshava Bharati: He awakens Krishna consciousness in the world.
[^03-8]: Krishna's command to Jagannatha Dasa to be born on earth: cf. 1.75 and the note there (where ତୁହି / ମହୀ also occur). "This form" (*ehi swarūpa*, 3.31) is apparently the Couple, Radha and Krishna (cf. 3.32). Gaudiya Vaishnavas do not accept the teaching that Jagannatha Dasa is a portion of Radha (note to 1.27).
[^03-9]: Printed ଧାନ (*dhāna*). Taken as *dhyāna*, "meditation"; the ୍ୟ is not visible. ଧାମରୁ (*dhāmaru*) literally means "from the abode"; translated "[meditation] on His own abode". ଚିହ୍ନାଙ୍ଗ (*cihnāṅga*, 3.34; printed ଚି + ହ୍ନ + ା + ଙ୍ଗ) is understood tentatively as "recognition of the form" (*cihnā*, "recognition", + *aṅga*). The sense of 3.33–3.34: in meditation Mahaprabhu recognizes His former form and confirms Dasa's account. Cf. 3.16: "for You nothing is hidden".
[^03-10]: The word has not been identified. It is printed ପା + େ + a dark sign like ଛ (cf. ପଛରେ, 3.26) + ଡ଼; no ି is visible. Tentatively ପାଛେଡ଼ି (*pācheṛi*), "wrap, shawl" (cf. Beng. *pāchṛā*, "cloth"); the rhyme with କାଢ଼ି supports this. The OCR gives ପାଶ୍ମେଡ଼. In the parallel VL 5.122 the Lord ties His uttariya on the Swami's head (see the note there). The episode of the title "Atibada" (3.35–3.41): cf. VL 5.119–5.129. It is absent from CC and CB. See the notes to 1.20 and to VL 5.119.
[^03-11]: A single line (half-shloka); the source has not been found. *Puruṣa-gocaram* is taken as "accessible [only] to the Purusha", i.e. to the Lord; cf. 3.16. It apparently refers to Dasa's account, the secret of his origin. 3.39 is almost word for word VL 5.121 and 5.129: "you have told a very great account; from today you have become Atibada". A pun: *atibaṛa kathā*, "a very great account", and *atibaṛa*, "very great".
[^03-12]: The "Vaishnavas" (3.44) and the "devotees" (3.57) are apparently Mahaprabhu's companions, the Gaudiyas. In VL 5.124–5.128, by contrast, they follow Svarupa and accept the form of address "Atibada". In Divakara they murmur and complain that they "have fallen to a lower position" (3.46): the author thus stresses Jagannatha Dasa's superiority over the Gaudiyas. Gaudiya Vaishnavas see Mahaprabhu's companions (Svarupa Damodara, Ramananda Raya and others) as His eternal associates, free from envy. A claim to the title "very great" contradicts the Lord's instruction to be "humbler than a blade of grass" (*Shikshashtaka* 3; CC, Antya 20.21). This is why Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati counts the "ativadi" among the apasampradayas (see the note to 1.27). ତୁହି (*tuhi*, familiar "you", 3.45) is read as in 1.75; here it apparently carries mockery. Who speaks in 3.48, the Vaishnavas or the author, is unclear. Given the link with 3.49–3.54, these are the author's words on the Lord's sovereignty.
[^03-13]: The source of the shloka has not been found. Printed କର୍ତ୍ତ (without ା and ୃ) three times and ଯଥାଦେବ ସ୍ତସ୍ୟ; restored by metre and sense: *kartā kartṛ-dvayoḥ kartā… devas tasya kiṁ cintayāmy aham*. Who the "two makers" are is unclear (Brahma and Shiva? prakriti and purusha?). 3.50–3.54 are an Odia gloss: the Lord is almighty; He can make the done undone and the undone done. Cf. the well-known phrase *kartum akartum anyathā kartuṁ samarthaḥ*, "able to do, not to do, and to do otherwise". With this the author apparently justifies the exaltation of Jagannatha Dasa above the Lord's former servants (3.46). ମହିମାଟି (3.50; printed ମହ + ମା + a sign like ଣ୍ଟ) and ତହୁଁକ (printed ତ + ହୁଁ + କ) are tentative readings.
[^03-14]: କାଟିଲା (*kāṭilā*) is understood by the Odia idiom *nā̃ kāṭibā*, "to make a name, to become renowned". The second sign of the word is indistinct. 3.57 leads into the devotees' complaint; it continues on the next page.
[^03-15]: 3.60–3.66 are Mahaprabhu's answer to the complaint: the glory of Purushottama-kshetra (Puri). Even the stones and wood of this Kshetra would be worshipped as gods in other lands, and the people here attain bhakti without sadhana. Hence the title 'Atibada' is not excessive for the Dasa (3.64). The source of the shloka 3.61 has not been found. The readings of the shloka are uncertain. ଶୁକ୍ତିତଃ (*śuktitaḥ*, 'from shells'): printed ଶୁ + a ligature like କ୍ତି; possibly ଗୁଳ୍ମତଃ, 'from shrubs'. ଶ୍ରେଷ୍ଠୋଽଗ୍ରେ: the avagraha is printed like ୨. In the Odia paraphrase (3.62), ଟେକା (*ṭekā*, 'pebble, clod') is read from the signs ଟେ and କା printed apart. ଝିଙ୍କର is not identified: perhaps 'shells' (cf. ଝିନୁକ, the *śukti* of the shloka) or 'crickets' (ଝିଙ୍କାରି, the *kīṭa*). 'Know as Purushottama' (*puruṣottama boli cihna*, 3.60) is taken as 'know [the glory of] Purushottama'. Gaudiya Vaishnavas also honour Puri (Nilachala) as a holy dhama. But they see a Vaishnava's greatness in humility, not in a title (see the notes to 3.44 and 3.79).
[^03-16]: Yajanagara (3.92: Yajapura) is Jajpur on the Vaitarani river, on the road from Puri to Bengal (cf. the note to VL 6.78). The departure of the offended Vaishnavas from Puri to Yajanagara is also in Madhava (VL 6.59–6.80). There the Gaudiyas are offended because the Prabhu honours Jagannatha Dasa as 'Svami', though he has received no initiation. Here the cause is the title 'Atibada'. This is not in the CC/CB. According to the CC, the Gaudiyas led by Shivananda Sena came to Mahaprabhu every year for the Ratha-yatra and spent the chaturmasya with Him (Madhya 16; Antya 10, 12); see the note to VL 6.65.
[^03-17]: 'Six Vaishnavas' (*ṣaṛamūrtti baiṣṇaba*, lit. 'six forms'; in 3.97 printed ଛତମୂର୍ତ୍ତି, cf. Odia ଛ 'six'). Who they are is not said. Possibly the Prabhu's closest companions who stayed with Him, probably including Jagannatha Dasa (cf. 3.91–3.97). ଭରୁଥାନ୍ତି (3.74) is taken as a misprint for ଭଜୁଥାନ୍ତି, 'worshipped': the second sign is printed as ରୁ.
[^03-18]: Which Haridasa is meant is not said. According to the CC, Haridasa Thakura lived in Puri (his disappearance: CC, Antya 11), and Junior Haridasa was among the kirtan singers (CC, Antya 2). In Madhava, Junior Haridasa leaves Puri before the Gaudiyas leave (VL 6.57–6.58). In Madhava the letter to those who left is carried by Shivananda (VL 6.77). The rhyme ଦୟାବନ୍ତ / ଯାଇଁ (3.77) is imperfect.
[^03-19]: The third verse of the *Shikshashtaka*. According to the CC (Adi 17.31; Antya 20.21), Mahaprabhu Himself spoke this verse; the *Padyavali* of Rupa Gosvami also gives it as the Lord's words. Here Mahaprabhu Himself writes it to the Vaishnavas who left. In this Divakara agrees with the CC and differs from VL 6.75, where the eight shlokas are written by the Svami, Jagannatha Dasa (see the note to VL 6.75). Printed ଅମାନିନ୍ୟେ (ନ with ୍ୟ and େ); we read *amāninā*, as in the CC. There is a stray stroke above ମା in ମାନଦେନ, and ର୍ତ୍ତ in କୀର୍ତ୍ତନୀୟଃ looks like ର୍ତ୍ତି. 3.84–3.86 are an Odia paraphrase of the verse. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati sees in this verse the foundation of a Vaishnava's conduct. For this very reason, in the Gaudiya Vaishnava view, the claim to the title 'Atibada' ('very great') contradicts the Lord's teaching (see the note to 3.44).
[^03-20]: Jagadananda is apparently Jagadananda Pandita, a close companion of Mahaprabhu (CC, Adi 10; Antya 12). The title 'Chakravarti' with his name does not occur in the CC. *Śrīmukha* ('holy mouth') here is the Lord's letter, His own words. ସନମତେ (3.82) is taken as *sammate*, 'with the consent'. ବହିଲେ (3.81, lit. 'carried') is taken as 'conveyed'; possibly a misprint for କହିଲେ, 'told'.
[^03-21]: *Bhedare bhakti na sajāi* — 'where there is division (discrimination), bhakti does not hold (is not fitting)'. Apparently a call not to divide Vaishnavas into 'ours' and 'others', Gaudiyas and Odias. The understanding is uncertain.
[^03-22]: Mahaprabhu Himself goes after those who left and takes Jagannatha Dasa with Him. Cf. VL 6.71–6.73: there the Prabhu asks the Svami to go with Him, and the Svami refuses. This episode is not in the CC/CB.
[^03-23]: The sense of 3.95b is unclear. Literally: 'uniting [him] with the gods' (*debatāṅka tule*). Perhaps the 'gods' are the Vaishnavas: cf. 3.63, where even the stones of the Kshetra 'are gods for other realms'. Then the Prabhu 'unites' Jagannatha Dasa with the Vaishnavas and promises that His word (the title 'Atibada') will stand and there will be love between them (3.96).
[^03-24]: In Yajanagara Mahaprabhu again calls Jagannatha Dasa 'Atibada', and the Vaishnavas grieve again. Continued on the next page. ପାଛୋଟି (3.99, 'going out to receive') is a tentative reading: no ା is visible after ଛ. For the Gaudiya Vaishnava position see the note to 3.44.
[^03-25]: In 3.105–3.107 the offended Vaishnavas decide to leave for Gauda (Bengal) and invite Mahaprabhu to come with them on pilgrimage: Gaya, the Ganga, the sea (perhaps Ganga-sagara, the mouth of the Ganga). ସତଏକ (*sataeka*) is taken as 'truly'. 'Not [stay] in one kingdom' is also possible. ଗୟା (3.107): printed ଗ + a sign like ସ୍ଥୁ (ୟ with a tail, ା merged); the reading is tentative, from the sense. Mahaprabhu's answer (3.109–3.113): He will not leave Jagannatha. According to CC (Madhya 3), Mahaprabhu settled in Puri at the request of His mother Shachi. The Gaudiyas came to Him there every year (see the note to 3.71).
[^03-26]: Source of the shloka not found. ଗତିର୍ (*gatir*): the first sign resembles ସ (ସତିର୍?). The reading follows the chime *ratiḥ — matiḥ — gatiḥ*. ଜୀବତେ is evidently a misprint for ଜୀବିତେ (*jīvite*, 'in life'). *Gaṅgā-taṭe taṭe* means 'on bank after bank', that is, along every bank of the Ganga. The sense: let the heart be devoted to Krishna and the road lead through the holy places of the Ganga — yet Mahaprabhu's refuge in life and at departure [from the body] is Jagannatha. *Maraṇe* is rendered 'at departure', following the project's usage regarding the Lord. 3.110–3.111 are its Odia paraphrase.
[^03-27]: *E atibaṛa jagannātha* means either 'this is Atibada Jagannatha' or 'this Jagannatha is very great'. There may be a pun: Jagannatha Dasa's name is the same as that of the Lord of Puri. 3.113 is understood tentatively. Literally: 'My release (*mocana*) is not otherwise; this befits the very great one.' Perhaps the sense is: 'I will not take [My word] back; [the title] befits the very great one.' Mahaprabhu confirms the title again, and the Vaishnavas leave. For the Gaudiya Vaishnava position, see the notes to 3.44 and 3.79.
[^03-28]: Bana-Vishnupur ('forest Vishnupur') is evidently Vishnupur (Bishnupur) in West Bengal, the capital of the Malla kings (Mallabhum). Gaudiya Vaishnavism took root there under Srinivasa Acharya and King Vira Hambira, that is, after Mahaprabhu's disappearance. In Divakara the Vaishnavas leave Mahaprabhu a second time, now for Bengal; cf. their first departure to Yajanagara (3.71) and VL 6.59–6.80. There is no such departure in CC/CB.
[^03-29]: Madho (Madhava) Dasa is named for the first time in 3.123; he is the Vaishnava sent in 3.119. He is unidentified. CC knows several associates of Mahaprabhu named Madhava, for example the kirtan singer Madhava Ghosha (CC, Adi 10). But the same name does not prove the same person. The rhyme ଚୈତନ୍ୟ / ପ୍ରଧାନ (3.122) is imperfect.
[^03-30]: ପ୍ରେମାଶ (?): printed ପ୍ରେ + ମା + a sign resembling ଶ or ଘ. It may be ପ୍ରେମାଶ୍ରୁ ('tears of prema') with the subscripts lost. Then the line repeats 'tears' (*aśru jaḷa*). The translation follows the sense.
[^03-31]: 3.127–3.128 are the Vaishnavas' reproach, conveyed by Madho Dasa: Mahaprabhu has stayed in the 'Odia kingdom' and 'taken to observing Odia practices'. Cf. 3.105 and 3.58 ('the Odia brahmin'). The episode of the letter (3.129–3.139) repeats 3.76–3.86: once again Mahaprabhu sends a letter with a shloka to those who left.
[^03-32]: A shloka in the shardula-vikridita metre (four lines). Source not found. The second line is poorly read. Printed ଗଙ୍ଗାଦ୍ୱାରମଦର୍ଶୀ ନେକପଦବୀନାସ୍ଥାମଯୋଧ୍ୟା ପୁରଂ. The metre requires a short ି in *adarśi* ('[by me] was seen'), but ୀ is printed. ନେକପଦବୀ is taken as *naika-padavī*, 'many roads'. The sign after ନା resembles ସ୍ଥା or ରା; *nāsthām* (= *na āsthām*, 'no attachment'?) is only a guess. In the third line ମୁରରିପୁମୂର୍ତ୍ତିଂ is printed; the metre requires *mura-ripor mūrtiṁ*, 'the form of the Foe of Mura' (Krishna). *Kutrāpi*: ତ୍ର is printed with a tail below. 3.133–3.139 are the Odia paraphrase of the letter. Gangadvara is Haridvar; 'the confluence of the Ganga' is evidently Prayaga (or Ganga-sagara); 'the town of cowherds' is probably Gokula. According to CC (Madhya 17–19), Mahaprabhu visited Kashi, Prayaga, Mathura and Vrindavana. According to CC, He did not go to Dvaraka, Ayodhya or Badarikashrama. The words 'I have seen' here may refer to the pilgrimages of those same Vaishnavas (3.120).
[^03-33]: Nila-kandara ('the Blue Cave') is a name of Puri, like Nilachala ('the Blue Mountain'). 3.136–3.137 give an Odia teaching: Krishna left the former places of His pastimes at the end of the yuga and now abides in Puri as Jagannatha. Gaudiya Vaishnavas worship Jagannatha as Krishna Himself. Mahaprabhu saw Krishna in Him and longed to take Him to Vrindavana (CC, Madhya 13). But they hold Krishna's pastimes in Vrindavana to be eternal, and Vrindavana to be the highest abode, which Krishna never leaves (CC, Adi 5).
[^03-34]: 3.141–3.142 are understood tentatively. *Nebe sehi* is 'He will take' or 'that one will take', and *puruṣottame* is 'to Purushottama' or 'in Purushottama'. The sense adopted: the Vaishnavas hoped that Mahaprabhu would abolish the title 'Atibada' for their sake and bring them back to Puri. The letter, however, only repeats that He will not leave Jagannatha. Hence the decision of 3.143–3.144.
[^03-35]: From 3.145 the author speaks. According to Divakara, the Gaudiyas, offended with Puri, go to Vrindavana. There they 'reject' the injunctions of the Vedas and Puranas and the customs of Purushottama (3.145–3.148). According to CC, Mahaprabhu Himself sent Rupa and Sanatana Gosvami to Vrindavana to uncover the lost places of Krishna's pastimes and to write books on bhakti (CC, Madhya 19, 23; Antya 4). Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, hold their teaching to be grounded in shastra. Their guides are the *Hari-bhakti-vilasa* of Sanatana and Gopala Bhatta and the works of the Gosvamis.
[^03-36]: The dispute over the beginning of the mahamantra: 'Hare Rama…' (the Odia custom) or 'Hare Krishna…' (the Gaudiyas). Cf. VL 6.1–6.18 and the note to VL 6.15: there Mahaprabhu Himself decides that in Nilachala the mantra begins with 'Hare Rama' and in Gauda with 'Hare Krishna'. In Divakara the Gaudiyas abandon the order of Purushottama on their own. On the 'sixteen [names]' see the note to 2.160. Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, chant the mahamantra beginning with 'Hare Krishna'.
[^03-37]: Nirmalya is the dried mahaprasad of Jagannatha, and also the flowers and other items offered to the Deity. In Puri it is honoured and sent out with pilgrims. Divakara's charge differs from CC: there Mahaprabhu and His Gaudiya associates receive Jagannatha's mahaprasad with reverence (CC, Madhya 12; Antya 16). ଭଜିଲେ (?): printed ଭ + a sign ଜ or ବ + ଲେ, with ି not visible. ରଜ is taken as 'dust' (*raja*). 'Vraja' may also be meant.
[^03-38]: Madana-mohana ('He who enchants even Cupid') is Krishna, the Deity of Sanatana Gosvami in Vrindavana. Gaudiya Vaishnavas honour Madana-mohana, Govinda and Gopinatha as the principal Deities of Vrindavana, installed by the Gosvamis. The 'abandoning' of Jagannatha's form is Divakara's accusation. According to the CC, the Gaudiyas worshipped Jagannatha as Krishna Himself (see the note to 3.137).
[^03-39]: On the 'Hari-mandira' tilaka see the note to 2.150. The second half-line is read with doubt. କଳା (?) is printed as କ + ଲା; understood as *kaḷā*, 'black'. *Kalā* is also possible. The last word is printed as flagged ଅ + a sign like ସ୍ୱ (= ରା) + ଧ + ଲେ; understood as ଆରାଧିଲେ, 'they honoured'. It may be ଆଦରିଲେ, 'they adopted with regard'. Which tilaka Divakara calls 'black' is unclear. Gaudiya Vaishnavas wear the same urdhva-pundra (the 'temple of Hari'), of gopi-chandana or Vraja clay (*Hari-bhakti-vilasa*, ch. 4). The charge of abandoning the 'Hari-mandira' is the author's view.
[^03-40]: *Baidha karma* is service by the rules of scripture (vaidhi-bhakti); *rāga-mārga* is the path of spontaneous love (raganuga-bhakti). According to the CC (Madhya 22) and Rupa Gosvami's *Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu* (1.2), Mahaprabhu Himself taught both paths. Raganuga-bhakti follows the example of the eternal residents of Vraja but does not reject scripture. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati warned against imitating raga without purity of heart. He did not approve of abandoning the rules: the Gosvamis themselves compiled the *Hari-bhakti-vilasa* on vaidhi-bhakti. Divakara, however, presents the Gaudiyas as rejecting the rules (cf. 3.145).
[^03-41]: 3.154–3.157 give the Gaudiyas' reasoning as Divakara reports it: the Lord is subject to the devotee's feeling; where devotees dwell, there is Purushottama. Cf. the well-known verse ascribed to the *Padma Purana*: 'I abide where My devotees sing.' This argument answers 3.60–3.66, where Mahaprabhu glorifies the Purushottama-kshetra. In 3.178–3.182 Divakara apparently grants the first half of the argument: the Lord 'is found there' where devotees speak of Him. ଭବେ (3.156) is printed without ା; understood as ଭାବେ, 'by feeling', as in the second half-line.
[^03-42]: Govardhana Hill, the Yamuna and the kadamba trees are places of Krishna's pastimes in Vraja. The parikrama of Vraja is the circuit of its holy places. According to the CC (Madhya 17–18), Mahaprabhu Himself went round Vrindavana, Govardhana and the forests of Vraja. Here this is described as the doing of the Gaudiyas who turned away from Purushottama.
[^03-43]: The 'six Gosains' (*ṣaṛa gosāi̐*) are the six Gosvamis of Vrindavana. Their list is given in 3.170–3.172 (see the note to 3.170). *Emantare* is understood as 'thus'; possibly 'meanwhile'.
[^03-44]: Mathura Dasa is not identified; no such companion appears in the CC/CB. The reading ଚାଇଣ (3.162) is tentative: printed a sign like ସ୍ୱ + ଇଣ (cf. ଚାହିଁ in 3.91, 3.106). 3.163b literally reads 'you will bring Mathura Dasa'. It is understood thus: -ku here marks the addressee — '[He said] to Mathura Dasa' (cf. 'they said, looking at Chaitanya', 3.106) — and those to be brought are the Gosvamis (3.165–3.166). According to the CC, Mahaprabhu Himself sent Rupa and Sanatana to Vrindavana (Madhya 19, 23). They came to Puri separately, not at a summons: Rupa through Bengal (Antya 1), Sanatana through the Jharikhanda forest at the time of the Ratha-yatra (Antya 4; cf. VL 5.40, 5.73–5.76).
[^03-45]: The Gundicha-yatra is the Ratha-yatra: the Deities ride on chariots to the Gundicha temple (cf. the note to VL 5.75). According to the CC (Madhya 14), when Jagannatha's chariot stopped and the elephants could not move it, Mahaprabhu pushed it with His head, and the chariot moved. ସଙ୍ଗତୁଲେ (3.166) is read tentatively: printed ସଙ୍ଗ + a ligature like ଭୁ or ତୁ, + ଲେ (cf. ତୁଲେ, 'together with', 3.73). 'Twenty persons' (*koṛie mūrtti*): cf. 'six persons' in 3.73.
[^03-46]: The syntax of 3.170–3.172 is uncertain. 3.170b literally reads 'Sri Sanatana's joy' (or 'Sri Sanatana — joy'). *Yeu̐ gosāi̐* means 'who [is] Gosain'. Divakara's six are Nityananda, Sanatana, Rupa, Raghunatha Dasa, Jiva and Gopala Dasa. Gaudiya Vaishnavas name as the six Gosvamis Rupa, Sanatana, Raghunatha Bhatta, Raghunatha Dasa, Jiva and Gopala Bhatta. Nityananda is not among them: according to the CC (Madhya 15) and CB (Antya 5), Mahaprabhu sent Him to preach in Gauda. Divakara lacks Raghunatha Bhatta; 'Gopala Dasa' is presumably Gopala Bhatta. Jiva Gosvami, the nephew of Rupa and Sanatana, came to Vrindavana, by Gaudiya tradition, only after Mahaprabhu's disappearance. ମୋକ୍ଷ (3.173) is printed clearly; it may be a misprint for ମୁଖ୍ୟ, 'chief'.
[^03-47]: 3.174–3.176: the Gosvamis come every year to the Gundicha and return to Vrindavana. According to the CC, it was the Gaudiyas from Bengal who came every year for the Ratha-yatra (Madhya 16; Antya 10, 12; see the note to 3.71). The Gosvamis from Vrindavana, by the CC, came to Puri only once each — Rupa (Antya 1) and Sanatana (Antya 4).
[^03-48]: 3.177–3.183 give the author's explanation: the Gaudiyas did not value Mahaprabhu's honour because they were close to Him. Examples of such closeness follow. Bhrigu struck Vishnu on the chest with his foot (*Bhagavata* 10.89); tradition links this blow with a mark on Vishnu's chest (here the Srivatsa). Nanda beats Krishna, seizing Him by the tuft: in the *Bhagavata* it is Yashoda who punishes Krishna (10.9). In their games the cowherd boys leap onto Krishna's shoulders (10.18). Gaudiya Vaishnavas see in such closeness the higher rasas of sakhya and vatsalya, free from reverential awe (CC, Adi 4). With Divakara it serves as a reproach.
[^03-49]: The word କମ୍ପା (??) is unclear: printed କ + ମ with a subscript resembling ପ or ଭ, + ା. Understood as *kampa*, 'trembling', i.e. reverential fear. The translation is tentative.
[^03-50]: 3.184 sums up the chapter: the Gaudiyas finally went to Vrindavana because of the title 'Atibada'. ନେଉଟି is understood as *leuṭi*, 'returning' (cf. ଲେଉଟି 3.116). Mahaprabhu, however, remains in Puri with Jagannatha. On the Gaudiya Vaishnava position see the notes to 3.44 and 3.79. According to the CC, the Gaudiyas did not leave Mahaprabhu; the Gosvamis lived in Vrindavana by His order.
[^03-51]: On the order of the mahamantra ('Hare Rama Krishna' among the Odias, 'Hare Krishna Rama' among the Gaudiyas) see the note to 3.147. According to Divakara, Mahaprabhu Himself chants it in the Odia order.
[^03-52]: The colophon is set in two lines, as at the end of Chapters 1 and 2 (see the note to the colophon of Ch. 2). Chapter 3 is also called 'Anukramani'; printed ଅନୁକର୍ମଣି, understood as ଅନୁକ୍ରମଣି. After Jagannatha's name there is here neither 'Dasa' (Ch. 1) nor 'Gosain' (Ch. 2): 'Sri Atibada Jagannatha'. The avagraha in ତୃତୀୟୋଽଧ୍ୟାୟଃ is printed like ୧. 3.187–3.188 are the author's signature (bhanita).

---

# Divakara Dasa. *Jagannatha-charitamrita*, Chapter 4
## Mahaprabhu Goes with the Swami to Sarvabhauma


*Chapter Four*

**4.1.** Listen attentively, good people, to the doings of Prabhu Chaitanya.  
**4.2.** Taking the Swami (?) with Him, He met Sarvabhauma.[^04-1]  
**4.3.** That Sarvabhauma Bhattacharya [performed] his duties by Vedic injunctions;  
**4.4.** he did not know devotion to Hari [and] abode in pride of learning.[^04-2]  
**4.5.** In the hope of delivering him, Prabhu graciously went to him.  

## Sarvabhauma Asks about Mahaprasada; Unoffered Food and the Naivedya to the Three Deities


**4.6.** With Him is Jagannatha Dasa, in the hope of hearing.  
**4.7.** Seeing [Prabhu], Sarvabhauma came and fell prostrate before Prabhu.[^04-3]  

*Shloka*  
**4.8.** *[Skt.]* "Hard to obtain is the sight of You, [the fruit] of the good fortune of ten million kalpas. At Your feet (??) I was purified in soul that very moment."[^04-4]  
**4.9.** "[The fruit] of the good fortune of my ten million kalpas is Your darshan, now attained (?).  
**4.10.** What I had heard before [has come true]: I have been purified in soul."  

*Shloka*  
**4.11.** *[Skt.]* "And the unoffered (?) savoury foods — in which (??) vessel should one feed with them? In what (?) vessel, offering the unoffered, does it become mahaprasada?"[^04-5]  
**4.12.** [In this] wave of the truth of Krishna-prema, Sarvabhauma asks (?) this:  
**4.13.** "What food is unoffered (amaneha)? With what (?) vessel is it filled?  
**4.14.** In what manner is the offering made? When does [the food] become mahaprasada?"  

*Shloka*  
**4.15.** *[Skt.]* "And the unoffered savoury foods — in which (??) vessel should one feed with them? Be pleased to tell this, O Lord of mercy! Protect the three worlds (?), O Protector!"[^04-6]  
**4.16.** "The unoffered savoury foods that Jagannatha enjoys —  
**4.17.** out of mercy tell me [of them], O Lord: You constantly (?) keep the three worlds (?)."  
**4.18.** Hearing this, Sri Chaitanya speaks a word to him:  

*Shloka*  
**4.19.** *[Skt.]* "Amoha destroys (?) all karma; amoha is rare [even] for Brahma. Through the exposition (?) of guru to disciple — that supreme, by the attainment of Brahman (?)."[^04-7]  
**4.20.** "O Sarvabhauma, hear the exposition [that] the guru [gives] to disciples.  
**4.21.** Calling it amaneha, [know]: it is itself perfect Brahman (?)."  

*Shloka*  
**4.22.** *[Skt.]* "The kshetra is Sri Purushottama, and in it abides (?) the amoha; mahaprasada is only there, in the kshetra of Sri Purushottama."[^04-8]  
**4.23.** "Amaneha [and] mahaprasada — [this is] the excellent kshetra, a distinct liberation (?).  
**4.24.** This kshetra of liberation (?) is here; in other kshetras no such abiding is found."  

*Shloka*  
**4.25.** *[Skt.]* "Balabhadra, Subhadra, Jagannatha [and] Sudarshana — the manifestation in a fourfold form; so long as (?) Your story is told…"[^04-9]  
**4.26.** "Hear: Balabhadra, Subhadra, Jagannatha and Sudarshana —  
**4.27.** in this kshetra the fourfold form has become manifest and abides.  
**4.28.** The unoffered (amoha) naivedya becomes renowned [as mahaprasada] in just this way."  

*Shloka*  
**4.29.** *[Skt.]* "'Shrim' — this is the manifestation of Shesha; 'hrim' — this is Bhadra, likewise; 'klim' — Krishna Jagannatha. With these three [bijas] one should duly feed [Them] that food (?)."[^04-10]  
**4.30.** "The bija 'shri' (?) is Shesha-deva; with the bija 'hri' they call Subhadra;  
**4.31.** the bija 'kli' is Jagannatha-deva. Understand the naivedya thus.  
**4.32.** Whatever naivedya you prepare, offer [it] to Sri Balabhadra;  
**4.33.** he will pass [it] to Subhadra; she will give [it] into the holy mouth of Prabhu.  
**4.34.** What Prabhu has tasted — all that is mahaprasada."  

*Shloka*  
**4.35.** *[Skt.]* "Partaking [of the nectar] of the lips of Shri (?), [with the bija] 'klim' one should feed the brahmanas (??). One who does not know prema thus — [and yet passes for] a sadhu and a Vaishnava!"[^04-11]  
**4.36.** "In this mood make the naivedya; eating the remnants, cross over samsara.  
**4.37.** People do not know this — and [yet] are called sadhus [and] Vaishnavas.  
**4.38.** This mood is hard to attain [even] for Brahma; O sadhus, cultivate this mood!"  
**4.39.** Again Sarvabhauma says: "How then does bhakti arise in this?"  

## Sarvabhauma Asks about Bhakti; the Mantra, the Name, and Radha in Jagannatha's Body


*Shloka*  
**4.40.** *[Skt.]* "The divisions of bhakti must be told; [let one] practise (??) the truth of bhakti (?). Mercifully tell me of bhakti (?), O Mahaprabhu (?)."[^04-12]  
**4.41.** "Shall I obtain all these (??) [kinds of] bhakti? O Prabhu, give me instruction."  
**4.42.** Hearing the devotee's words, Sri Chaitanya gives instruction.  

*Shloka*  
**4.43.** *[Skt.]* "In the common tongue (?) — [the sense] of the king of mantras, which has the form of mantra and yantra: Jagannatha is the highest truth, and wholly (??), as is fitting."[^04-13]  
**4.44.** Rendering the king of mantras into the common tongue, worship the body of Jagannatha.  
**4.45.** Contemplate [Him] in mantra, yantra [and] tantra — with little [effort] you will attain bhakti.  

*Shloka*  
**4.46.** *[Skt.]* "For one who always sings the name of Hari, the devising of mantras is blunted (?); [his] always are attachment and love, and the joy of hearing and relishing."[^04-14]  
**4.47.** Singing the name of Hari [and] casting contrivances far away,  
**4.48.** contemplate attachment [and] love in hearing — you will obtain the boat of the joy of bhakti.  

*Shloka*  
**4.49.** *[Skt.]* "She whose body is prema, named Radhika, is the form of sweet prema. 'Klim' — [She] who knows the rasa of Krishna (?); [Her] mark is called [that of] a Vaishnavi (?)."[^04-15]  
**4.50.** Radhika is the sweet form; in the body of prema (?) abides the form of Krishna.  
**4.51.** Knowing the love of Radha [and] Krishna, count Her among the Vaishnavas.  

*Shloka*  
**4.52.** *[Skt.]* "The Parabrahman Jagannatha is higher than the highest (?) above the avatars; ten million avatars are born (??) [from Him]; … (??) is released."[^04-16]  
**4.53.** The Supreme Brahman is Jagannatha: beyond the highest is the Highest — such (?) [is He].  
**4.54.** … (??) tens of millions of avatars come out of Jagannatha's body.  

*Shloka*  
**4.55.** *[Skt.]* "Appearing (lit. 'born') (?) in Radha's body, the gopi named (?) Radhika comes to be; this arose in Sri Krishna, who sports in Vrindavana."[^04-17]  
**4.56.** [She] who appeared (lit. 'is born') from Jagannatha's body is the nectar of the Govardhana (?) pastimes in Vraja.  
**4.57.** She appeared (lit. 'was born') from Him [and] sported in Vrindavana.  
**4.58.** This Radha arose from Radha; [She] is renowned [as] the daughter (lit. 'joy') of Vrishabhanu.  
**4.59.** *[Skt.]* "'Shrim' … (??) is shakti; 'hrim' is declared to be Radha; 'klim' is Krishna, Kamaraja; childhood, boyhood [and] youth."[^04-18]  
**4.60.** The syllable 'shri' is the supreme king (?) [of syllables]; the syllable 'hri' is called the king (?) of Rama.  
**4.61.** The syllable 'klim' is the essence of Kamaraja. Childhood, boyhood [and] youth —  
**4.62.** five, ten [and] sixteen years: such is the age of the three forms.[^04-19]  
**4.63.** 'Shri' (?), which is the supreme king (?) — in it are the syllables of the name Hari.[^04-20]  
**4.64.** Know that 'hrim' is the king (?) of Rama: in it, they expound, is the name Rama.  
**4.65.** 'Klim', which is Kamaraja — know it as 'Krishna'.  
**4.66.** Red, white [and] dark hue — [thus] distinguish (?) the three names.  

*Shloka*  
**4.67.** *[Skt.]* "Jagannatha in the form of mantra is upon the head of Shesha-deva — [the form] of the yantra, of the king of mantras, joined (?) with the sixteen-syllabled mantra."[^04-21]  
**4.68.** This Jagannatha in the form of mantra abides upon Shesha-deva.  
**4.69.** Yantra, mantra, sixteen syllables — this is the utterance (?) of the king of mantras.  

*Shloka*  
**4.70.** *[Skt.]* "And in the body of Sri Jagannatha is Radha, ever (?) white; and in the middle (?) of the white chita Krishna [and] Radhika are seen."[^04-22]  
**4.71.** In this body of Jagannatha abides Radha, full of prema.  
**4.72.** Shining (?), the white chita of the Lord joins the bodies (?) of Radha [and] Krishna.  

## The Glory of Radha; Radha and Jagannatha; the King of Mantras and Harinama


*Shloka*  
**4.73.** *[Skt.]* "The round (?), youthful and reddish [form] of Krishna [and] Radha (?), unseen by Brahma and Rudra — about that you asked."[^04-23]  
**4.74.** Very youthful, beautifully round — [such is] the body of Radha [and] Krishna.  
**4.75.** The truth (?) you asked about (?) is beyond the reach of Brahma [and] Rudra.  

*Shloka*  
**4.76.** *[Skt.]* "Not in pastimes (?) [only] — the absorption of Krishna [and] Radha in rasa is always. Mercifully tell me of another pastime of Radha, O Prabhu."[^04-24]  
**4.77.** Finding happiness in Radha's rasa, He, Jagannatha, becomes subject [to Her].  
**4.78.** From here appeared (lit. 'were born') Radha [and] Krishna, who perform pastimes in the world.  

*Shloka*  
**4.79.** *[Skt.]* "As the substance of the body of prema (?), so Radha too is in the form of substance. Radha, the yantra (??) of the king of mantras, abides Herself."[^04-25]  
**4.80.** Radha is the body of prema; She is called the excellent substance (?).  
**4.81.** Radha's [mantra] is the king of mantras; Radha is called the very form of the yantra.  

*Shloka*  
**4.82.** *[Skt.]* "Radhika is equal to Krishna, [She is] Krishna's body (?); in Radha is the highest essence. In Radha are bhakti and liberation, in Radha are all desires (?)."[^04-26]  
**4.83.** Radha's form is equal to Krishna; Radha abides beyond the highest (*parapara*).  
**4.84.** Radha grants bhakti [and] liberation and fulfils all desires.  

*Shloka*  
**4.85.** *[Skt.]* "In Radha are the mantra (?), the worship [and] the purashcharana — Radhika; in Radha are ten million worlds (??), in Radha are ten million mantras."[^04-27]  
**4.86.** By Radha's mantra [the Deities] receive worship (?); in the invocation (avahana) (?) Radha is present.  
**4.87.** Worlds, tens of millions of expanses, are born from Radha's glance.  

*Shloka*  
**4.88.** *[Skt.]* "Radha is Jagannatha Himself; Radhika is in visible (lit. 'gross') form; Radhika is beyond prakriti; Radhika is on the head (?) of Shesha."[^04-28]  
**4.89.** Radha (?) is Jagannatha Himself; Radha (?) abides in visible form.  
**4.90.** Radha is beyond prakriti; Radha (?) is [on] the head of Shesha-deva.  
**4.91.** Thus the guru (?) Vishvakarma told the glory of Radha.[^04-29]  
**4.92.** Radha is the giver of bhakti: say 'Radha', and bhakti arises.  
**4.93.** Jagannatha is subject to Radha; from Radha the world has flowed forth (?).  
**4.94.** The expanse of worlds, all the lands there are — all these are born from Radha.  

*Shloka*  
**4.95.** *[Skt.]* "Brahma, Vishnu and Mahesha, Indra and the [other] gods of the quarters (?), Rama and the [other] avataras (?) — dwellers (?) … (??)."[^04-30]  
**4.96.** These Brahma, Vishnu, Maheshvara, Indra and the [other] guardians of the ten quarters,  
**4.97.** all the hosts of gods, all the divine places (?) of worship,  
**4.98.** Rama and all the [other] avataras — their home is in Purushottama.  
**4.99.** This king of mantras of sixteen syllables (?) came out of Jagannatha's body.  
**4.100.** By half (??) — three bijas (?); doubled — six bijas (?), they say.[^04-31]  
**4.101.** As the king of mantras is, so visibly is this Jagannatha.  
**4.102.** Again Sri Chaitanya says: "Now listen about Harinama."  

*Shloka*  
**4.103.** *[Skt.]* "The mantra 'Hare Rama Krishna' is hard to obtain [even] among all mantras. This maha-mantra is desired above (?) all and grants every perfection."[^04-32]  
**4.104.** This king of mantras is Harinama; these two (?) are the abode of the fulfilment of desires.  
**4.105.** What is called Harinama is hard to obtain [even] among all mantras.  

## The King of Mantras and the Five States; the Glory of Purushottama-kshetra


*Shloka*  
**4.106.** *[Skt.]* "Receive (lit. 'make by you') the instruction in the sixteen-syllabled (?) king of mantras. The king of mantras … (??); there is no steadfastness (?) in any other bhakti (?)."[^04-33]  
**4.107.** You, O, do as I say: keep the king of mantras on your lips (lit. 'in the throat').  
**4.108.** When such instruction is given, it destroys all desires.  
**4.109.** All bhakti will arise, [and] at the end you will obtain the service of [His] feet.  
**4.110.** Without this mantra, by another way, the devotee will never obtain [bhakti].  
**4.111.** Jagannatha, who is the king of mantras — that truth is 'Hare Rama Krishna'.  
**4.112.** [He] who is in the form of Bala-Mukunda — that is the Gopala mantra.  
**4.113.** Even if people [merely] receive the instruction in the Shri king of mantras,  
**4.114.** they at once obtain the increase (?) of rare (?) bhakti in this way (?).  
**4.115.** When [the Name] sounds (?) in a loud voice (?) among animals, insects (?) [and] worms (?),  
**4.116.** by [its] power their bhakti grows; at the end they attain the supreme goal (?).[^04-34]  
**4.117.** The external (?) state is [the state] of the supreme (??) bija (??); the state of absorption (?) is the ardha-matra.  
**4.118.** The principal state that is spoken of — that is Shri Jagannatha's body.  
**4.119.** These five states are Jagannatha; hence (?) [He] is called the Pancha-tattva.  
**4.120.** Such is the unfolding (?) of the king of mantras, and such [is] the Harinama:  
**4.121.** the state of the jiva is [the name] 'Hari'; the inner state is called 'Rama';  
**4.122.** the supreme state is called 'Krishna'; 'Hare' is the external state;  
**4.123.** and the principal state that is spoken of is the form of the mantra 'Om'.[^04-35]  
**4.124.** Fifty crores [of yojanas] — the load of the earth — [rest] on Ananta's head.  
**4.125.** In the midst of that are five kroshas: Purushottama, the abode of bhakti.[^04-36]  
**4.126.** Dharma, artha, kama [and] moksha — [all] perfection Purushottama grants.  
**4.127.** Whether one desires bhakti [or] liberation, for beings (?) [it] is fulfilled by the power [of this place].  
**4.128.** Do not strive (??) for dharma, artha, kama [and] moksha: [they come from] the perfection of this city.  
**4.129.** At the end (?) all the perfections, as many as there are, perforce dance (?), having come to the feet.  
**4.130.** They say: 'We shall come to your feet; we shall become [your] siddhis in Purushottama.'[^04-37]  

*Shloka*  
**4.131.** *[Skt.]* "Purushottama is the best (??) and the chief of kshetras on earth; [in it are] likewise Mathura, Dvaraka, Ayodhya, Kashi [and] Vrindavana."  
**4.132.** Whoever abides (??) in Purushottama, being born in the kshetra, obtains the truth.  
**4.133.** Being born from this kshetra, at the end, having passed away (?), they obtain liberation (?).  
**4.134.** Mathura, Dvaraka and Kashi, Ayodhya, Vrindavan — [are here] together (?).[^04-38]  

*Shloka*  
**4.135.** *[Skt.]* "North of the Kalpavriksha, beside [it], is Shri Jagannatha … (??). Those who enter the middle region — a man (?) will obtain bhakti and liberation."  
**4.136.** The Kalpavriksha [and] Jagannatha: the devotee who enters between [them] —  
**4.137.** whether he entered (?) knowingly or unknowingly — into him bhakti enters, even by mistake (?).[^04-39]  
**4.138.** The unknowing obtains prema-bhakti; the knowing obtains every [kind of] bhakti.  
**4.139.** This [place] surpasses in all qualities; its [Lord] is the Lord of all kshetras.  
**4.140.** Since Brahma [and] Shiva (?) know [this], they therefore contemplate this city.  
**4.141.** Vyasa, Narada and the others, as many as there are, contemplate this city in the heart (?).  
**4.142.** Whoever does not accept this understanding — know him to be born an animal.  
**4.143.** One whose desire for the Name is gone (?) [and who], leaving (?) this city, desires something else —  
**4.144.** the Creator will write him down — has written (?) him down for birth as an asura.  
**4.145.** I (?), leaving (?) all kshetras [and] tirthas, abide (lit. 'abides') in Purushottama-kshetra.  
**4.146.** Who is the greatest fool, so foolish [as to] leave this kshetra [and] go elsewhere?[^04-40]  
**4.147.** Pushkara and the other tirthas, as many as there are, all abide in the ocean.  
**4.148.** All intelligence [and] perfections — [these are granted by] Yameshvara (?), the one who … (??).  
**4.149.** Mastery of all Vedic learning [is granted by] Ganesha, who abides beneath the banyan.[^04-41]  

## The Goddesses of Purushottama; Mahaprabhu's Ashtaka before Sarvabhauma; End of the Chapter


**4.150.** With all words of praise Sarasvati abides [here] (?).[^04-42]  
**4.151.** Over pleas (?) of the heart's pain that Vimalakshi holds charge.  
**4.152.** With all sovereignty Varuna's daughter abides [here].  
**4.153.** With dominion (?) [abides] Mangala (?), through whom [comes] the wooden body [of the Deities].[^04-43]  
**4.154.** A being who becomes wise — why (?) would he go to other kshetras?  
**4.155.** He whom (?) the Creator … (??) wanders in the ocean of samsara.[^04-44]  
**4.156.** Before Sarvabhauma Prabhu Chaitanya uttered  
**4.157.** the longing (??) for Krishna-prema — the ashtaka from Prabhu's holy mouth (?).[^04-45]  
**4.158.** All the Vaishnavas heard [it], [and] Jagannatha Dasa, who were [there].  
**4.159.** They heard [the words] from Prabhu's holy mouth [and] pondered these verses in their hearts.  
**4.160.** The brahmin Divakara says, bowing his head at Chaitanya's feet:  
**4.161.** "Pandits (?), do not hold it against me: I am the servant of your servants."[^04-46]  

*Thus [ends], in the Sri Jagannathamrita, [the life of] Sri Ati Bada Jagannatha Gosain, the fourth chapter, called 'Anukramani' (?).*[^04-47]

[^04-1]: The Swami is Jagannatha Dasa, as the Odia authors call him (cf. VL). Printed ସ୍ୱାନିଙ୍କି: ସ୍ୱା + ନି + ଙ୍କି. Understood as ସ୍ୱାମିଙ୍କି (ନ for ମ), with the ending ‑ṅki for ‑ṅku. According to the CC (Madhya 6), Mahaprabhu met Sarvabhauma immediately on arriving in Puri. On seeing Jagannatha He fell unconscious in the temple, and Sarvabhauma had Him carried to his house. Jagannatha Dasa does not appear in that account. In Divakara the meeting with Sarvabhauma comes after the departure of the Gaudiyas (Ch. 3), and Mahaprabhu comes to him together with Jagannatha Dasa.
[^04-2]: Here Divakara is close to the CC (Madhya 6): Sarvabhauma Bhattacharya is a great scholar and teacher of Vedanta, proud of his learning. Mahaprabhu turned him to bhakti by explaining the *ātmārāma* verse in eighteen ways and showed him His six-armed form.
[^04-3]: Mahaprabhu has come to Sarvabhauma, and Jagannatha Dasa listens nearby (4.6). Sarvabhauma bows at once and glorifies the Lord (4.8–4.10). In CC (Madhya 6), Sarvabhauma at first took Mahaprabhu for a young sannyasi and taught Him Vedanta for seven days. He bowed to the Lord only after the explanation of the *atmarama* verse and the revelation of the six-armed form. Jagannatha Dasa is absent from this account in CC (see the note to 4.2).
[^04-4]: The shloka is spoken by Sarvabhauma. Its source has not been found. The beginning of the second line is poorly read. The print has ତବପାଦପଦାନୂକ୍ଷୌ: ପଦା + ନ with a subscript hook (ୂ or ୍ୱ) + େ + a sign like କ୍ଷ + ୗ. Sense and metre suggest something like *pāda-padaṁ vīkṣya*, "having beheld the feet"; this is only a guess. 4.9: the last word (ସମ୍ଭର?) is read uncertainly and taken as "gathered, attained" (cf. *sambhṛ*). 4.10, literally: "what I had heard before — I became a purified soul". Apparently Sarvabhauma had heard of Mahaprabhu before, and what he heard has come true.
[^04-5]: Sarvabhauma asks about mahaprasada. The *amoha* of the shlokas and the *amaṇehā* of the Odia paraphrase (4.13, 4.16, 4.21, 4.23) mean "unoffered". This is apparently the Odia *amaṇiā*: food not yet offered to the Deity; ମଣୋହି (4.14) is the offering to the Deity. The Sanskrit *amoha* seems to be the author's rendering of this Odia word; this is a guess. The print has the single word କମ୍ପାପାତ୍ରେତୁ (in 4.15, କିମ୍ପାପାତ୍ରେତୁ). It is taken as "in which vessel then", but the first part (*kampā-*, *kimpā-*) is unclear. ପାତ୍ରେ and ପାତ୍ରେଣ (4.13) are printed with the two-loop sign (see the note to 4.6). ପଚାରି (4.12) is read by sense. In 4.12 "a wave of the truth of Krishna-prema" apparently names the conversation, but the construction is unclear. In CC (Madhya 6), Mahaprabhu Himself brought Sarvabhauma the mahaprasada of Jagannatha. Without washing his mouth, Sarvabhauma ate it at once and cited a verse of the *Padma Purana*: mahaprasada is to be eaten at once, wherever and however it comes. This was the sign of his conversion. Cf. MP 1.107 and the note there. In Divakara, Sarvabhauma instead asks about mahaprasada, and Mahaprabhu instructs him.
[^04-6]: The first line repeats the first line of 4.11. The second is Sarvabhauma's request. In 4.17 the first sign of ସିଲୋକ୍ୟ resembles ସି, not the ତ୍ରି of 4.15. The correction [ତ୍ରିଲୋକ୍ୟ] follows the shloka (*trilokaṁ rakṣa*). ନିରତେ ("constantly"): the ି is not visible; the word rhymes with ମୋତେ.
[^04-7]: Mahaprabhu's answer (4.19–4.21) is understood uncertainly. ସର୍ବକର୍ମଘ୍ନଂ: the sign may be ଘ୍ନ or ଜ୍ଞ; ଘ୍ନ ("destroying") fits the sense better. ବିବରେଣ and ସିଦ୍ଧୟା are also doubtful. In 4.20 "the exposition of guru [and] disciples" is taken as instruction handed down from guru to disciple. Divakara apparently teaches that the food of Purushottama-kshetra is holy even as "unoffered" — "itself Brahman" (4.21). Mahaprasada, however, exists only in Puri (4.22–4.24). For Gaudiya Vaishnavas food becomes prasada through offering to the Lord. They honour the mahaprasada of Jagannatha as non-different from Him (CC, Madhya 6; Antya 16).
[^04-8]: Purushottama-kshetra is Puri; on Nila-kandara and the teaching of Puri as the highest kshetra, see the note to 3.137. ମୁକ୍ତିଭେଦ (4.23) and the first word of 4.24 are read uncertainly. In 4.24 the first sign resembles ସ୍ୱ (ମୁ? ରା? ଚା?), the second ର with an inner stroke. ମୁକ୍ତିକ୍ଷେତ୍ର ("kshetra of liberation") is adopted after 4.23.
[^04-9]: Chaturdha-murti, "the fourfold form", are the four Deities on the altar (ratna-vedi) of the Jagannatha temple: Balabhadra, Subhadra, Jagannatha and Sudarshana (the disc-pillar). The second line of the shloka is unclear. ଯାବତ୍ତବ is taken as *yāvat tava*, "so long as Your [story]". In 4.28 *amoha* is the unoffered naivedya, which "thus" (through the Chaturdha-murti, see 4.29–4.34) becomes mahaprasada.
[^04-10]: Bijas are one-syllable mantras. "Shrim" is usually the bija of Lakshmi; here it is Shesha's, i.e. Balabhadra's. "Hrim" is the bija of Shakti (Bhuvaneshvari); here it is Subhadra's ("Bhadra"). "Klim" is the kama-bija, the bija of Krishna; here it is Jagannatha's. ତ୍ରିଭିସ୍ତନ୍ନଂ is taken as *tribhis tad annam*, "with the three [bijas] — that food"; the metre of the second line is short. ବୀଜ (4.30) is printed like ସାଜ; the reading follows the sense. The order of offering in 4.32–4.34: the naivedya is offered to Balabhadra, he passes it to Subhadra, and she to Jagannatha. What Jagannatha has tasted is mahaprasada. This order is not in CC/CB. The bija "klim" is part of the Gopala mantras that Gaudiya Vaishnavas receive at initiation. They offer food to Krishna through the guru, according to the rules of the *Hari-bhakti-vilasa*.
[^04-11]: The shloka is poorly read. ଶ୍ରିୟାଧର is taken as *śrī-adhara*, "the lips of Shri [Krishna]": mahaprasada is the nectar of His lips. ବ୍ରହ୍ମସୁ: the last sign resembles ସୁ or ସ୍ୱ. The sense (*brahmasu*, "among brahmanas"?) is unclear. ସସାଧୁଃ is taken as *sa sādhuḥ*. The sense of the second line is clear from the paraphrase (4.37): people do not know this prema, yet pass for sadhus and Vaishnavas. The reproach is apparently aimed at the Gaudiyas: cf. 3.145–3.148 and the note to 3.148 (they "cast away" the honouring of nirmalya). According to CC, Mahaprabhu and His Gaudiya associates received the mahaprasada of Jagannatha with reverence (Madhya 6, 12; Antya 16).
[^04-12]: Sarvabhauma's shloka is poorly legible. The end of the first line is printed as ଭକ୍ତି + a sign like ଚ + the ligature ତ୍ତ୍ୱ + ନ + ଷେ + ବ + େ + a sign like ଯ + ତ୍. It is taken as *bhakti-tattva niṣevayet*, 'let one practise the truth of bhakti'; this is a guess. ଅଇଂ is taken as *aham*, 'I'. After ମହା stands a damaged sign; taken as *mahāprabho*. The first word of 4.41 is covered by a blot; taken as ଏ, 'these'. In the CC (Madhya 6), Sarvabhauma is converted after the explanation of the 'atmarama' verse. Mahaprabhu discusses sadhya and sadhana (the kinds of bhakti) not with him but with Ramananda Raya (Madhya 8). In Divakara it is Sarvabhauma who receives the teaching on bhakti.
[^04-13]: Shloka 4.43 is unclear. ଭାଷାଯ୍ୟଂ is taken as *bhāṣāyāṁ*, 'in the common tongue', after the paraphrase in 4.44 (ଭାଷା କରଣ). The word before ଯଥୋଚିତଂ (ନି + ଭ + a sign like ରା with a subscript ଞ) is unidentified; perhaps *nirbharaṁ ca*, 'and wholly'. The 'king of mantras' (*mantra-rāja*) here is apparently the mantra of Jagannatha (cf. 4.67–4.69: the sixteen-syllabled mantra, the yantra). Mantra, yantra and tantra are the path of Tantric worship; according to Divakara, bhakti is easily attained with them. Gaudiya Vaishnavas worship the Deity of Jagannatha as Krishna Himself (CC, Madhya 6, 13). Their chief means, however, is the Name (see the note to 4.46).
[^04-14]: *Kuṇṭhate*, 'is blunted', is a tentative reading (printed କୁ + a sign like ଣ୍ଠ + େତ). The sense is apparently: for one who sings the Name, devising mantras is superfluous. This is close to Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching. In the CC (Madhya 15), the Name does not depend on diksha or purashcharya: it delivers everyone who utters it. Cf. also the 'harer nama' shloka (note to 2.141). *Nāba* (4.48) is taken as 'boat' (*nāu*): the boat of the joy of bhakti. Cf. 3.188: 'on the boat of bhakti He will carry [us] across'.
[^04-15]: Shloka 4.49 is read tentatively. ସ୍ୱରସାଭିଜ୍ଞା is taken as *kṛṣṇa-svarasābhijñā*, 'knower of Krishna's rasa'. ବୈଷ୍ଣବୀ: after ବୈଷ୍ଣ stands a sign like ବ with an upward hook; taken as *vaiṣṇavī*. The syllable 'klim' is linked here with Radha; in 4.59–4.65 'klim' is Krishna (and in 4.29, Jagannatha). In 4.50 the print has ପ୍ରେମ + େଙ୍ଗ; taken as ପ୍ରେମାଙ୍ଗେ, 'in the body of prema'. In 4.51 Radha is to be 'counted among the Vaishnavas'. Gaudiya Vaishnavas see Radha differently. She is Krishna's hladini-shakti, His eternal internal energy, one with Him in essence and above all devotees (CC, Adi 4). Cf. the *Padma Purana* verse 'as Radha is dear to Vishnu…' (CC, Madhya 8). Cf. MP 2.39–2.54, the tattva of Radha in the language of bijas, and the note to MP 2.39.
[^04-16]: The second line of shloka 4.52 is poorly legible. Printed: a damaged ଜ + a sign like ନ/ୟ + େ + ନ୍ତ; taken as *jāyante*, 'are born'. Then ନ + ତ୍ୟ + ସ + େ + a sign like ଦ/ଯ + ହ; the word is not understood. ପରାତ୍ପରଂ: printed ପ + ରା + ପ with a subscript sign + ରଂ. ଏତ (4.53) is taken as 'such'; the rhyme with ଜଗନ୍ନାଥ is loose, as in 4.68. The first word of 4.54 stands at the page edge, and only part of it is visible (a sign + ସ). Here Jagannatha is the Parabrahman and the source of all avatars. Below (4.55–4.58) Radha too comes from His body. Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, also regard Jagannatha as Krishna Himself (CC, Madhya 13). But for them the source of the avatars is Krishna, Svayam Bhagavan (*Bhagavata*, 1.3.28; CC, Adi 2). Jagannatha does not stand above Krishna as the Shunya-Brahman. On the teaching of the Atibadi, see the note to 1.1.
[^04-17]: The construction of shloka 4.55 is unclear: ଭୂତ୍ୱା and ନାମସ୍ୟ are read tentatively. The sense is clear from the paraphrase (4.56–4.58). According to Divakara, Radha is "born" from Jagannatha's body; the Radha of Vrindavana, Vrishabhanu's daughter, comes from this Radha. ଗୋବଧନ (4.56) is taken as *gobarddhana*, 'Govardhana' (no repha visible; OCR ଗୋବନ). ନନ୍ଦ (4.58), lit. 'joy', is taken as 'daughter' (*nandinī* expected). None of this is in the CC/CB. In Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching, Radha is eternal and unborn. She is Krishna's hladini-shakti: Radha and Krishna are one in essence and eternally two in lila (CC, Adi 4). Cf. MP 2.46: Radha is 'not born from a womb'.
[^04-18]: On the bijas, see the note to 4.29. Here they are distributed differently: 'shrim' is shakti, 'hrim' is Radha (in 4.60, 4.64 'Rama'), 'klim' is Krishna-Kamaraja. In 4.29 it was otherwise: Shesha, Subhadra, Jagannatha. ପରେକାର (4.59) is not understood (the OCR reads it the same way). The sign like ସ୍ୱ + ଜ in 4.59–4.65 is read as ରାଜ, 'king'. The standard *kāmarāja* (the name of the bija 'klim') and ମନ୍ତ୍ରରାଜ (4.43, 4.67, 4.69) are printed the same way. In 4.30 the same sign was taken as ବୀ, 'bija'. That reading is possible here too ('the supreme bija', 'the bija of Rama', 'the kama-bija'). In 4.63 the first word is printed as an ଇ-like sign with an ି arc + a sign like ସ୍ୱ. It is taken as 'shri' after 4.60 and the series 4.63–4.65 ('shri', 'hrim', 'klim').
[^04-19]: 'Five, ten, sixteen' is printed jointly (ପଞ୍ଚଦଶ ଷୋଳ). It is taken as the limits of three ages: childhood (*bālya*, *kaumāra*), boyhood (*paugaṇḍa*) and youth (*kaiśora*). This agrees with the Gaudiya Vaishnava division in Rupa Gosvami's *Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu* (2.1): kaumara up to five years, pauganda up to ten, kaishora up to sixteen. Gaudiyas worship Krishna as the eternal kishora. The link of the ages with the three bijas is Divakara's.
[^04-20]: 4.63–4.66: the three words of the mahamantra — Hari, Rama, Krishna — are matched with the bijas 'shri', 'hrim', 'klim' and with three colours. Which colour belongs to which name is not said. The order 'Hari — Rama — Krishna' is the Odia order of the mahamantra ('Hare Rama Krishna'; see the note to 3.147). On the 'sixteen [names]', see the note to 2.160 and 4.67–4.69. Gaudiya Vaishnavas chant the mahamantra without bijas or Tantric correspondences. For them the Name is Krishna Himself (CC, Madhya 17).
[^04-21]: Jagannatha 'in the form of mantra' abides 'upon the head of Shesha-deva'; Shesha is Balabhadra (4.29–4.30). The link with the arrangement of the altar is unclear. ମନ୍ତ୍ରଯୁତ୍ (4.67): after ମନ୍ତ୍ର stands an unclear sign. ଉଚ୍ଚାର (4.69): printed a sign like ଉ + a sign like ସ/ଚ with a subscript + ର, ା not visible; taken as 'utterance'. 'Sixteen-syllabled' (*ṣoḍaśākṣara*) is apparently the mahamantra of sixteen names (for the Gaudiyas, 16 names, 32 syllables). The teaching of Jagannatha as mantra and yantra belongs to the Odia Tantric tradition. It is not in the CC/CB.
[^04-22]: The chita (ଚିତା) is taken as the ornament on Jagannatha's forehead (cf. the Chita-lagi festival, when it is put on). This understanding is tentative. ସର୍ବଦା (4.70): the middle sign is unclear (like ଙ୍ଗ/ର୍ବ). ଯ୍ୟାମଧ୍ୟେ is taken as *citāyā madhye*, 'in the middle of the chita'. ଜଳି (4.72) is taken as 'shining' (printed a sign like ଜ + ଲ/ଳ with ି; OCR ଜଲ). The word ଅଙ୍ଗ (4.72) is worn; only ଅ is visible. ରାଏ means 'king, Lord', i.e. Jagannatha. According to Divakara, Radha and Krishna abide together in Jagannatha's body. Gaudiya Vaishnavas see Radha and Krishna united in one form in Sri Chaitanya Himself (CC, Adi 1, 4). Mahaprabhu saw Jagannatha as Krishna, the Lord of Vrindavana (CC, Madhya 13).
[^04-23]: The theme of 4.70–4.72 apparently continues: Radha and Krishna in Jagannatha's body. 'Round, youthful, reddish' may describe Jagannatha's form with His round eyes; this is a guess. ବର୍ତ୍ତୁଳଂ: printed ବ + repha + a sign like ତ୍ତ + ଳଂ. ରଧୟୋଃ lacks ା in print; the correction [ରାଧୟୋଃ] follows sense and metre. *Paripṛcchata* is 2nd person plural ('you asked' or 'ask'); the retelling in 4.75 has 'what you asked'. ତତ୍ତ୍ୱ (4.75): printed ତ + a looped sign. ପଚାରିଲ: printed ପ + a sign like ସ୍ୱ (= ଚା) + ରି + ଲ (cf. ପଚାରି in 4.12). Gaudiya Vaishnavas see Krishna Himself in Jagannatha (CC, Madhya 13), and Radha and Krishna united in Sri Chaitanya; see the note to 4.70.
[^04-24]: ବିହାରେ: ବି is printed as ବ with an inner hook, as in ବିହାରସ୍ତୁ in the second line. The sense of the first line ('not [only] in pastimes'?) is unclear. The retelling in 4.77–4.78 does not repeat the shloka's request. Instead it says that Jagannatha is subject to Radha's rasa and that Radha and Krishna were 'born' from here, that is, from Jagannatha (cf. 4.55–4.58). Gaudiya Vaishnavas also teach that Krishna is conquered by Radha's prema (CC, Adi 4). But for them Radha and Krishna are eternal and are not born from anything; see the note to 4.55.
[^04-25]: Shloka 4.79 is unclear. ପ୍ରେମାଙ୍ଗସ୍ୟ: printed ପ୍ରେ + ମା + ଙ୍ଗ + a sign like େ + ସ + a tall stroke. *Dravya* means 'substance'; cf. 'body of prema' (*premāṅga*) in 4.49–4.50. The second line is one syllable short. After ଯନ୍ତ୍ର stands a sign like ସ୍ୱ (= ରା?) + ଃ, then '।', then ସ୍ୱୟଂ ସ୍ଥିତା. The inner '।' is not reproduced in our text. The retelling in 4.81 (ଯନ୍ତ୍ର ସ୍ୱରୂପ) suggests *yantra-rūpā*, 'in the form of the yantra'. ସୁଦ୍ରବ୍ୟ (4.80): the ର-phala under ଦ is not visible. On mantra and yantra see the note to 4.43; on Radha, the notes to 4.49 and 4.55.
[^04-26]: କୃଷ୍ଣାଙ୍ଗ: the last sign resembles ଙ୍ଗ. କାମଶ୍ଚ: printed a sign like ଦ + ା + ମ + a sign like ଶ୍ଚ in ମୁକ୍ତିଶ୍ଚ. ଧାମଶ୍ଚ ('and abodes') is also possible. 'Desires' follows the retelling in 4.84 ('fulfils all desires'). *Parāpara* (4.83) means 'the higher and the lower' or 'beyond the highest'; the second is adopted. In Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching, Radha is Krishna's hladini-shakti, one with Him in essence (CC, Adi 4). But what She grants above all is prema. Pure devotees do not seek liberation or the fulfilment of desires: 'those who desire bhukti, mukti and siddhi are all without peace' (CC, Madhya 19.149).
[^04-27]: Shloka 4.85 is poorly legible. In ରଧାୟାଂ no ା is printed after ର. The second word is ମ + a sign like ର with a dot; it does not look like ମନ୍ତ୍ର in the second line. It may be ସର୍ବ ('all [worship]'; OCR ମର୍ବ). କୋଟିଖଣ୍ଡଂ: printed କୋଟି + a sign like ବ with a dot + ଣ୍ଡଂ. Taken as 'ten million parts, worlds' after 4.87; perhaps *koṭi-brahmāṇḍam* was meant. Purashcharana is a vow to repeat a mantra a set number of times. ଅବାହନେ (4.86) is printed like ଅବାଦନେ (the ହ/ଦ trap). It is taken as *āvāhana*, the invocation of the Deity at the start of puja; ଅ/ଆ is not corrected. Who 'receives worship' in 4.86 is not said. ଜନନ୍ତି (4.87) = ଜନ୍ମନ୍ତି, 'are born'. According to CC (Adi 5), the universes arise from Maha-Vishnu when He glances at maya. Radha is Krishna's internal energy, not the source of the material world. The same applies to 4.93–4.94.
[^04-28]: Shloka 4.88 and 4.89–4.90 identify Radha with Jagannatha. The 'gross' (*sthūla*) form is perhaps Jagannatha's visible wooden (daru) form; this is a guess. 'On the head of Shesha': cf. 4.67–4.68 and the note to 4.67, where Jagannatha in the form of the mantra abides on Shesha. ମୂର୍ଦ୍ଧ୍ନି: printed ମୂ + repha + a sign like ଦ୍ଧ + ନ, ି not visible. ରାଧାଟି (4.89–4.90): printed ରାଧା + a sign like ଟ with ି; it does not look like ରାଧାଙ୍କ (4.90). Taken as ରାଧା with the particle ‑ṭi. In Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching, Jagannatha is Krishna Himself, and Radha is His hladini-shakti (CC, Adi 4). Radha and Krishna united in one form are Sri Chaitanya (CC, Adi 1.5); see the note to 4.70.
[^04-29]: Vishvakarma is the architect of the gods. In the Puranic account of King Indradyumna, he carved the Deities of Purushottama from wood (daru) (Skanda Purana, Purushottama-kshetra-mahatmya). Why he 'told' the glory of Radha here is unclear. ଗରୁ: printed ଗ + ରୁ; taken as ଗୁରୁ, 'guru'.
[^04-30]: The second line of the shloka is poorly legible. ଅବତାରଶ୍ଚ: the ା in ତା is not visible. Next comes a sign like ଛ/କ୍ଷ + ତ + a dot + a sign like ପୁ + ର/ଳ; the word is not identified. Then ନିବାସିକା, 'dwellers'. The retelling in 4.98 ('their home is in Purushottama') suggests 'dwellers of the city [of Purushottama]'. ଇନ୍ଦ୍ରାଦିଗ୍ଦେବତା is printed as one word with ଦି written once; taken as *indrādi-dig-devatā*. ଦିବ୍ୟସ୍ଥାନ (4.97): printed ଦି + ବ + two dots + ସ୍ଥା + ନ; possibly ଦେବସ୍ଥାନ, 'temples'. All gods and avataras live in Purushottama: cf. 4.22–4.24, 4.52–4.54 and the notes to 4.22 and 4.52. Gaudiya Vaishnavas honour Nilachala as Krishna's abode. But for them the source of the avataras is Krishna (CC, Adi 2).
[^04-31]: 'Of sixteen syllables' (4.99): printed ଷୋ + two dots + ଳ + a sign like କ୍ଷ + ର; perhaps ଷୋଳାକ୍ଷର with a damaged ା. This is apparently the maha-mantra; see the note to 4.67. 4.100 is unclear. ଅଦେଂଣ: printed ଅ + େ + ଦ + a sign like ଂ + ଣ. Taken as *ardhena*, 'by half'; this is a guess. ବୀଜ: the same sign like ସ୍ୱ + ଜ as in ରାଜ (see the note to 4.59). Here ବୀଜ is adopted because of ଷଡ଼ବୀଜ (printed like ଛଡ଼ବାଜ). The three bijas of 4.59–4.65 ('shrim', 'hrim', 'klim') may be meant, but the count is unclear. 4.101: the king of mantras is 'visibly' Jagannatha. Gaudiya Vaishnavas teach that the Name is non-different from Krishna (CC, Madhya 17.131–133); they add no bijas to the Name (see the note to 4.63).
[^04-32]: The source of the shloka was not found. 'Hare Rama Krishna' is the Odia order of the maha-mantra; see the notes to 3.147 and 4.63. ସର୍ବେଭ୍ୟୋପି: printed ସ + େ + a sign like ସ୍ୱ (apparently ବ with repha) + ଭ୍ୟୋ + ପି; taken as *sarvebhyo 'pi*, 'above all'. ବେନି (4.104), 'both', is an uncertain reading.
