Which 'two' are meant is unclear: the king of mantras and Harinama, or the two halves of the maha-mantra (cf. the note to 2.135). Cf. CC, Adi 7.73: from the Krishna mantra comes release from samsara; from the Krishna Name one attains Krishna's feet. Gaudiya Vaishnavas chant the maha-mantra beginning with 'Hare Krishna'.
[^04-33]: The second line of the shloka is poorly read. The word after ମନ୍ତ୍ରରାଜ (printed a sign like ତ/ଦ with a subscript, a sign like ଷ with a subscript, ନ, ଦ୍ୟ) was not identified. ଭକ୍ତିର୍ନିଷ୍ଠ ନହି: over ନ stand a reph and an ି stroke, then a sign like ଷ୍ଠ, then ନ + ହ. Taken as *anya-bhaktir niṣṭhā na hi*, 'there is no steadfastness in any other bhakti'; this is a guess, based on the paraphrase in 4.110 ('without this mantra … never'). The 'sixteen-syllabled king of mantras' is the maha-mantra 'Hare Rama Krishna' (4.111; see the notes to 4.67, 4.100, 4.103). Bala-Mukunda (4.112) is Krishna as a child; the Gopala mantra is the Krishna mantra given at initiation. Here it is identified with Jagannatha and the maha-mantra. According to the CC (Adi 17), Mahaprabhu Himself accepted initiation into the Krishna mantra from Ishvara Puri at Gaya. Gaudiya Vaishnavas distinguish the diksha mantra from the maha-mantra: the Name does not depend on diksha and liberates whoever utters it (CC, Madhya 15; see the note to 4.46).
[^04-34]: ପତଙ୍ଗ (printed second sign like ଦ), କୀଟଠାରେ, ଶୁଭଇ (OCR ଶୁଭ୍‌ଇ) and ଉଚ୍ଚସ୍ୱରେ (printed a sign like ଦ/ଉ + ଚ୍ଚ + a sign like ସ୍ୱ + ରେ) are uncertain readings. The sense: loud chanting of the Name benefits even animals and insects. This is close to Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching. In the CB (Adi 16), Haridasa Thakura explains that loud kirtana delivers all who hear it, even animals, birds and insects. According to the CC (Madhya 17), in the Jharikhanda forest the animals sang and danced with Mahaprabhu. ଗତି (4.116): printed ଗ + ତ + a sign like େ before ପା; taken as 'goal'.
[^04-35]: 4.117–4.123 teach five 'states' (*asthā* = *avasthā*): the external (*bāhya*), absorption (*līna*?), the ardha-matra, the principal (*pradhāna*). According to 4.121–4.123, the names of the maha-mantra correspond to the states: 'Hari' to the state of the jiva, 'Rama' to the inner, 'Krishna' to the supreme, 'Hare' to the external; the principal is 'Om'. The ardha-matra is the 'half-syllable' after the sounds a-u-m of 'Om'. This is Odia esoteric teaching, close to the Panchasakha; it is not in the CC/CB. The readings of 4.117 are uncertain. ବାହ୍ୟ: printed ବା + a sign like ହ/ଦ with ୍ୟ; the same sign is in 4.122 (ବାଦ୍ୟ, 'music, sound', is also possible). ପରା ବୀଜର: printed ପ + a sign like ସ୍ୱ (= ରା), then ବା with a dot above + a sign like ଜ + ର; the sense is unclear. ଲୀନ: printed ଲ + ନ with an ି hook. ଏହୁଁ (4.119): printed ଏ + ହୁ + a sign like ଁ. ବିସ୍ତର (4.120): ି not visible, the second sign like ସ୍ତ. 'Pancha-tattva' here means the five states of Jagannatha. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas the Pancha-tattva is Shri Chaitanya and His associates: Nityananda, Advaita, Gadadhara and Shrivasa (CC, Adi 7).
[^04-36]: 'Fifty crores' is a Puranic measure: according to the *Bhagavata* (5.20.38), the whole universe extends fifty crores of yojanas. Ananta (Shesha) holds it on His hood (ibid., 5.25.2). Purushottama-kshetra, according to the Puranas, extends five kroshas. ପଞ୍ଚାଶ: the two dots after ଞ୍ଚ are taken as ା. ଅଛି: printed ଅଛ + a separate sign like ି. On Purushottama as the highest kshetra see the notes to 3.60, 3.137 and 4.22.
[^04-37]: 4.126–4.130: Purushottama grants the four aims of life (dharma, artha, kama, moksha), and the siddhis come to the feet of their own accord. The readings are uncertain. ପ୍ରାଣୀଦି (4.127): printed ପ୍ରାଣୀ + a sign like ଦ with an ି hook. 4.128 ନ ସାଧ: printed ନ + a damaged sign like ସ/ପ + ା + a sign like ଧ/ଥ; taken as 'do not strive'; this is a guess. ଅନ୍ତେ (4.129): possibly ଅନ୍ୟେ, 'other'. ନାଶନ୍ତି (4.129): the sign after ନା looks like ଶ, but 'destroy' gives no sense. After 4.130 ('we shall come to your feet'), ନାଚନ୍ତି, 'dance', is adopted. 'Your' (*to*) refers, apparently, to Jagannatha or to the devotee living in Purushottama. Pure devotees seek neither liberation nor siddhis (CC, Madhya 19.149; see the note to 4.82).
[^04-38]: The shloka 4.131 and the paraphrase 4.132–4.134: Purushottama is the chief of kshetras, and Mathura, Dvaraka, Ayodhya, Kashi and Vrindavan abide in it 'together'. The readings are uncertain. ଶ୍ରେଷ୍ଠ: printed a dark sign like ଶ୍ର/ସ୍ଥ + a sign like ପ with a subscript and a stroke. ସ୍ଥଇ (4.132): printed a sign like ସ with a dark subscript ଥ + a sign like ଇ; possibly *sthāyī*, 'abiding'. ମୁକ୍ତି (4.133): the second sign is damaged. ମରି (4.133): printed ମ + a sign like ସ୍ୱ with a stroke (= ରି), rhyming with ହୋଇ. ଶେଷେ (4.133): printed େ + ଶ + େ + ଷ. ମିଶି (4.134) is an uncertain reading. Cf. 3.144 and the note to 3.144 (Vrindavan). That passing away in Puri gives liberation is also taught by the *Skanda Purana* (Purushottama-kshetra-mahatmya). Gaudiya Vaishnavas revere Nilachala as Krishna's dhama. But they hold Vrindavan, the place of His lilas with the gopis, to be the highest abode. According to the CC (Madhya 17–19), Mahaprabhu Himself went from Puri to Vrindavan.
[^04-39]: The Kalpavriksha (Kalpa-vata) is the sacred banyan in the Jagannatha temple compound, beside which stands Bata-Ganesha (see the notes to 1.94 and 2.10; cf. 4.149). Under this banyan, according to Divakara, Mahaprabhu listened to Jagannatha Dasa's *Bhagavata* (note to 2.16). The 'middle region' (4.135) is the place between the banyan and Jagannatha (4.136). ଶ୍ୱେତ (4.135): printed େ + a sign like ଶ/ସ with a subscript loop + a dark ତ; the sense is unclear (possibly ସେତୁ, 'bridge'?). ନୃ: printed ନ with a subscript ୃ hook; the stem *nṛ* instead of *naraḥ*. ପ୍ରବଶନ୍ତି: ି not printed; the correction [ପ୍ରବିଶନ୍ତି] follows the sense and ପ୍ରବେଶ in 4.136. ପଶିଗଲେ (4.137): printed ପ + a sign like ଟି/ଶି (OCR ସଟିଗଲେ). ଭୁଲେ (4.137): printed ଭୁ; possibly କୁଳେ, 'into their family' (the ତୁ/ଭୁ/କୁ trap). Gaudiya Vaishnavas also value living in a holy place: it is one of the five most potent practices of bhakti, together with hearing the *Bhagavata*, the association of devotees, chanting the Name and serving the Deity (CC, Madhya 22). But they see the root of bhakti in the association and mercy of devotees (ibid.).
[^04-40]: 4.142–4.146: whoever does not accept the supremacy of Purushottama and goes elsewhere will be born an animal or an asura. The readings are uncertain. ଗତବାଞ୍ଛା (4.143): printed a sign like ଗ/ଶ + ତ + ବା + ଞ୍ଛା; 'the Name' here may be the Harinama, or something else. ଛାଡ଼ି (4.143, 4.145): printed a sign like ଛ + ଡ଼. ଲେଖିଲ (4.144): printed a sign like େ with a loop + ଖି + ଲ. ମୁଁ (4.145): printed ମୁ with a large blot above; the verb ଅଛଇ is in the 3rd person. If this is 'I', the speaker is apparently Mahaprabhu; the CC has no such words. Gaudiya Vaishnavas revere Puri but do not condemn pilgrimage to other holy places: Mahaprabhu Himself went to the South and to Vrindavan (CC, Madhya 7–9, 17), and sent Rupa and Sanatana to live in Vrindavan (CC, Madhya 19, 23).
[^04-41]: 4.147: all the tirthas, beginning with Pushkara, abide in the ocean at Puri. ଯମେଶ୍ୱର (4.148): printed ଜ + େ + ମ with a subscript + ଶ୍ୱ + ର (the ଯ/ଜ trap). Yameshvara is a Shiva temple in Puri; the reading is conjectural. ଯେଉଣ: printed େ + ଯ + a sign like ଦ/ଉ + ଣ; word not identified. 4.149: Ganesha beneath the banyan is Bata-Ganesha (see the note to 1.94). ବେଦବିଦ୍ୟା: ି in ବି not visible.
[^04-42]: Sarasvati is the goddess of speech and learning. Vimala (Vimalakshi) is the goddess whose temple stands within the enclosure of Jagannatha's temple; in Puri she is honoured as the guardian of the kshetra. 'Varuna's daughter' is Lakshmi: she rose from the ocean, and Varuna is the lord of the waters. In Puri Lakshmi has her own temple in the same enclosure. Gaudiya Vaishnavas honour Lakshmi as Jagannatha's consort; cf. the Hera-panchami festival in CC (Madhya 14). The readings are uncertain. ସକଳ (4.150): the last sign is damaged. ପ୍ରତି: printed ପ with a subscript ର-phala + ତି; possibly ପ୍ରୀତି, 'lovingly'. ହୃଦ (4.151): printed a sign like ହ/ତ with subscript ୃ + ଦ (OCR ଦୃଦ). ଗୁହାରି means 'complaint, plea'.
[^04-43]: 4.153 is poorly read. Before ଙ୍ଗଳ there is a blot; it is taken as ମଙ୍ଗଳ, Mangala. This is a guess from the neighbouring Sarasvati, Vimala and Lakshmi. Mangala is the goddess of Kakatpur. By Puri tradition, at the Navakalevara (the renewal of the Deities' bodies) she shows the seekers in a dream the trees for the new daru. If the reading is right, 'through whom [comes] the wooden body' refers to this. ଚକ୍ରବର୍ତ୍ତ (printed ଚ + କ୍ର + ବ + ର୍ତ୍ତ) is taken as 'dominion' (= ଚକ୍ରବର୍ତ୍ତୀ, 'sovereign'). A link with the wheel (chakra) marks that, by tradition, the tree for the daru must bear is also possible; this too is a guess. The rhyme ଳ / ର is imperfect.
[^04-44]: The first half-line is not read. Printed: ବିଧାତା, then େ + a dark sign like ଯ/ପ + comma + ନ (OCR ସେଳ), then a sign like ଯ + ା + a damaged ହ + ା + ରେ. Taken as 'he whom the Creator [has so appointed]'; cf. 4.144, where the Creator 'writes down' one who leaves Puri for birth as an asura. କାହିଁ (4.154): printed a sign like କ + ହିଁ + a vertical stroke (ା displaced?). On the Gaudiya Vaishnava position (Mahaprabhu Himself went on pilgrimage to holy places) see the note to 4.143.
[^04-45]: 4.157 is poorly read. ପ୍ରେମନ୍ତୁ: printed ପ୍ରେମ + a sign like ନ + a sign like ନ୍ତୁ/ଙ୍କୁ; taken as ପ୍ରେମକୁ. ଲାଳସ: printed a dark ଲ + a damaged sign + ସ; taken as *lāḷasa*, 'longing'. ଶ୍ରୀମୁଖ: after ଶ୍ରୀମୁ the signs are damaged. ଅଷ୍ଟକର: printed ଅ + ଷ୍ଟ + କ + ର. If the reading is right, the *Shikshashtaka* is meant — Mahaprabhu's eight verses, full of longing for prema (cf. the verse 'nayanam galad-ashru-dharaya…'). By CC (Antya 20), Mahaprabhu recited these verses in His last days, in the Gambhira, before Svarupa Damodara and Ramananda Raya, not before Sarvabhauma. To Sarvabhauma He explained bhakti and the 'atmarama' verse (CC, Madhya 6; see the note to 4.4). Jagannatha Dasa among the listeners (4.158) is Divakara's account (cf. the note to 4.2).
[^04-46]: 4.160–4.161 are the author's signature (bhanita), as at the end of Chapters 1–3. ପଣ୍ଡିତେ: printed ପ + ଣ୍ଡି + a sign like େତ; taken as the address 'pandits'. ଘେନମା: printed େଘ + ନ + ମା. Taken as ଘେନ ମୋ, '[do not] hold against me', with ୋ lacking its left part. The Sanskrit prohibitive *mā* is also possible.
[^04-47]: The colophon is set in two lines, as at the end of Chapters 1–3 (see the note to the colophon of Ch. 3). Chapter 4 too is called 'Anukramani': printed ଅନୁକର୍ମଣି, understood as ଅନୁକ୍ରମଣି. As in the colophon of Ch. 2, it reads 'Jagannatha Gosain'. The avagraha in ଚତୁର୍ଥୋଽଧ୍ୟାୟଃ looks like ୧. Below the colophon is a printer's ornament, then the heading of Chapter 5.

---

# Divakara Dasa. *Jagannatha-charitamrita*, Chapter 5
## The Author on Himself and His Book; Nityananda's Question and the Kavacha of the Mantra


*Chapter Five*

**5.1.** Listen attentively (?), good people: I tell the *Jagannathamrita*.  
**5.2.** For fear of wicked people I write a little [and] in a veiled way (?).[^05-1]  
**5.3.** Wicked people who expound (?) learning (?) will revile (?) [this] book.  
**5.4.** My name is the brahmin Divakara; my father is Janadeva (?).[^05-2]  
**5.5.** Jagu Dasa is my father's father; Nanda Dasa is his father.  
**5.6.** His father is Baidhara (?); my mother was named Champa.  
**5.7.** My gotra is Bharadvaja, the pravara Brihaspati [and] Angiras (?).  
**5.8.** Apart from (?) Jagannatha alone, nothing else delights my mind.  

*Shloka*  
**5.9.** *[Skt.]* "Not Vrindavana, not the cowherds' town (?) with its groves and dwellings, not Ayodhya, [not] Dvaraka, not … (??), [not] Kashi [and] Madhupuri; not Kuru… (?), not … (??), not … (??) or [another] kshetra (??): [only] Jagannatha's abode gladdens (?) my heart (?) with love (??)."[^05-3]  
**5.10.** All the kshetras I described before do not delight my mind.  
**5.11.** I wrote books — the Vedas; in them my mind finds no delight.[^05-4]  
**5.12.** Jagannatha alone I worship (?) with all my heart.  
**5.13.** I took refuge at [His] holy lotus (?) [feet] — that is all my worship.  
**5.14.** I wished to [clothe] bhakti in verse (?) and, gathering (?), wrote the book.  
**5.15.** What I was to compose (?) exists [as] shlokas from the Vedas (?).  
**5.16.** I have rendered the meaning of those shlokas and, making [Odia] verse, wrote the book.  
**5.17.** Whatever instructions the Gosain formerly gave, wherever [he gave them],  
**5.18.** that book I wrote, making it exact, following [their] sense.  
**5.19.** I myself am not so (?) worthy (?): Sri Jagannatha is pleased to speak [it].[^05-5]  
**5.20.** His Name is a wish-fulfilling tree, the Mount Meru of the devotees' hopes.[^05-6]  
**5.21.** Receiving His command, I set out (??) into the wealth of bhakti (?).  
**5.22.** He who expounded bhakti to Sarvabhauma — Chaitanya Gosain —  
**5.23.** stayed free of care in the kshetra for twenty-four years.[^05-7]  
**5.24.** At that time a host of Vaishnavas, who were in Prabhu's company, [was there].  
**5.25.** One day Nityananda joyfully asked Prabhu.[^05-8]  

*Shloka*  
**5.26.** *[Skt.]* "[The mantra] beginning with 'Hare Rama' is the king of mantras, bestowing the fruit of five (?); it gives everyone what they desire [and] grants sayujya with Krishna."[^05-9]  
**5.27.** With the mantra 'Hare Rama Krishna' [devotees] put (?) a kavacha on the body…  

## The Kavacha of the Mantra 'Hare Rama Krishna': Syllables on the Body, the Sakhis and the Cowherd Friends


**5.28.** "If I am worthy to hear (?), O Prabhu, be pleased to speak (?) joyfully."  
**5.29.** Hearing these words, Sri Chaitanya joyfully speaks:  

*Shloka*  
**5.30.** *[Skt.]* "Having heard that, Mahadeva, the best of the gods headed by Brahma, [said]: 'Whoever is endowed with devotion to Hari (??) will obtain all power (?).'"  
**5.31.** "O Nityananda (?), listen: for Brahma and the [other] gods this is the best.[^05-10]  

*Shloka*  
**5.32.** *[Skt.]* May the syllable 'ha' protect the nose, the syllable 're' the mouth, the syllable 'ra' (?) the eyes (?) … (??), the syllable 'ma' the middle of the chest.  
**5.33.** *[Skt.]* The syllable 'kri' (?) the middle of the brows, and the syllable 'kha' the palate.  
**5.34.** Brahma, Mahesha, Indra at the head (?), and all the guardians of the quarters there are —  
**5.35.** put the name 'Hara' in front: [their] power is immense (??).[^05-11]  
**5.36.** You shall place the syllable 'ha' on the head, you shall put the syllable 're' in the mouth;  
**5.37.** put the syllable 'ra' (?) in the eyes, place the syllable 'ma' between the breasts;  
**5.38.** the syllable 'kri' (?) abides between the brows, the syllable 'kha', they say, on the palate.[^05-12]  

*Shloka*  
**5.39.** *[Skt.]* May Lalita protect the head, Vishakha the right arm, Champakalata the throat, Chitra the left arm; … (??) Rangadevi, may Sudevi protect the crown of the head (?); Tungavidya the face, Indulekha the ears; Shashirekha the middle of the brows, and Vimala the right side; may Palika protect the forehead (?), Manjari the chest; Shyamala the middle of the navel, Madhumati (?) the middle [of the body]; may Dhanya protect the fingers, Mangala … (??); may Sridama protect the hips, Sudama both thighs; Vasudama both knees, may [his] friend Arjuna protect the genitals; may Subala protect the right [foot], Kinkini the left foot (??); the east … (??) Krishna, may Varuthapa (??) protect the outside.[^05-13]  
**5.40.** May Lalita protect my head, taking with her [the syllable] 'ha'.  
**5.41.** May Vishakha protect both arms, taking with her the syllable 're'.  
**5.42.** May Champakalata protect the throat, joining with her the syllable 'ra'.  
**5.43.** Chitra will protect my left [side], taking along [the syllable] 'ma' (?).  
**5.44.** … (??) Rangadevi, taking Sudevi with her, —  
**5.45.** [and] these three (?) syllables 'ha-re-ra' (?) — may they protect my head.  
**5.46.** May Tungavidya protect the face, joining with her the syllable 'ma'.  
**5.47.** May Indulekha protect the ears, joined with the syllable 'ra'.  
**5.48.** May Shashirekha, with the syllable 'ma', protect the middle of my forehead.  
**5.49.** The right brow — both places (?) — [is protected by] Vimala, abiding together with [the syllable] 'ra'.  
**5.50.** May Palika abide (?) on my forehead (?), and may the syllable 'ma' be with her.  
**5.51.** On the chest is Ananga-manjari: taking [the syllable] 'ha', may she protect [it].  
**5.52.** … (??) may protect the navel, holding (?) [the syllable] 're' with her.  
**5.53.** Madhumati (?) — the middle of the belly: may she protect [it], taking the syllable 'ha'.  
**5.54.** Dhanya — on the hand (?): may she be on the thumb, and may the syllable 're' abide with her.  
**5.55.** May Mangala protect my belly, taking along [the syllable] 'ha' (?).  
**5.56.** Sudama will protect my shanks (?); the syllable 're' will be together with him.  
**5.57.** May Sridama protect both thighs, joining with him the syllable 'kri' (?).  
**5.58.** Sudama will protect [the legs] down to the knees; the syllable 'kha' will be there with him.  
**5.59.** [His] friend Arjuna will protect the genitals, making the syllable 'ha' his companion.  
**5.60.** Subala — the right foot, taking along the syllable 're'.  
**5.61.** May Kinkini protect the left foot, taking along the syllable 'kri' (?).[^05-14]  

## The kavacha of the Name: guardians of the quarters, colours of the syllables, its fruits; end of Chapter 5


**5.62.** The eastern quarter [is guarded by] Stoka-Krishna, taking the syllable 'shna' as companion.  
**5.63.** Let Mahabahu abide in the west and take the syllable 'kri' as companion.  
**5.64.** In the south — … (??) [his] place (?); taking the syllable 'kha' (?) [= 'shna'] as companion.[^05-15]  
**5.65.** Vishala will guard the south-west; two (?) syllables will be with him.  
**5.66.** Mahabala will be in the north, taking the syllable 'shna' (?) as companion.  
**5.67.** Vrishabha will guard the north-west; two (?) syllables will be with him.  
**5.68.** Let Devaprastha abide in the north (?), taking the syllable 're' as companion.  
**5.69.** Ujjvala will guard the north-east; the syllable 'ha' will be [his] companion.  
**5.70.** Let Mahabahu guard the upper [quarter]; let the syllable 're' be his companion.  
**5.71.** Ananta will guard the lower [quarter], taking the syllable 'Om' (?) as companion.[^05-16]  
**5.72.** The wise (?) Shyama will guard the eight (??) [quarters]; He in all beings is … (??) love (??).[^05-17]  
**5.73.** May that Krishna, beloved of the gopis (?), abide in [my] intellect [and] in my whole body.  
**5.74.** The syllable 'ha' (?) is white in colour, the best among all letters.[^05-18]  
**5.75.** However many sins there are in this body, on remembering [it] it quickly removes [them].  
**5.76.** The syllable 're' is red in colour; it pervades (??) … (??) in a person's body.  
**5.77.** However many sins of former births there are, the syllable 're' removes them when it is uttered.  
**5.78.** The syllable 'ra' is golden in colour, [like] Radha's (?) incomparable body.[^05-19]  
**5.79.** However many sins arise at an eclipse (?), they are destroyed when 'ra' is uttered.  
**5.80.** The syllable 'ma' is of the form of light: it is directly Niranjana.  
**5.81.** However many sins of false speech there are, on remembering [it] it quickly removes [them].  
**5.82.** The syllable 'kri' is like collyrium: it removes the lesser sins.  
**5.83.** Sins of betrayal of trust (?) it removes at once when it is chanted.  
**5.84.** The syllable 'shna' appears white, like [the light] of the sun and moon (?).  
**5.85.** Grave faults and great sins are destroyed by mere chanting.[^05-20]  
**5.86.** The person who, saying (?) 'Hare Rama Krishna', holds to this Name —  
**5.87.** in him bhakti arises (?), and he attains it flawlessly (?).  

*Shloka*  
**5.88.** *[Skt.]* "To the hypocrite, the non-devotee, … (??) one who has not subdued the senses, the babbler (?), the proud, the fool, one averse to love for Krishna [this should not be given]."[^05-21]  
**5.89.** The hypocritical, non-devoted person, and also the disciple of another path;  
**5.90.** one who is in the grip (?) of his own greatness, talkative (?) [and] proud;  
**5.91.** one who does not know love for Krishna — let this book not be told before him.  
**5.92.** People of demonic mind, hearing [it], laugh, clapping (?) their hands.  
**5.93.** The man who reviles (?) this scripture is an asura of Lanka:  
**5.94.** he had heard the name of Sri Rama, and therefore obtained a human body.[^05-22]  
**5.95.** Whoever puts on this kavacha of the Name of Hari as armour on the body,  
**5.96.** in him bhakti arises for Krishna's feet, and he has no fear of Yama's punishment.  
**5.97.** Afflictions from the planets, bhutas [and] pretas, dakinis, pishachis — all of them;  
**5.98.** king, tiger, crocodile, thief (??) — of all these he becomes fearless (?).  
**5.99.** Enemies, on seeing [him], are pacified; wealth and grain are obtained in abundance.  
**5.100.** Whether high-born (?) [or] wicked (?), whoever sees [it] attains bhakti near Krishna.[^05-23]  
**5.101.** Hearing the words of the kavacha of the Name of Hari, cross over samsara (?), O beings!"  
**5.102.** Before Sri Nityananda Prabhu, Sri Chaitanya lovingly  
**5.103.** expounded the whole rasa of bhakti. Brahmins were nearby;  
**5.104.** all the Vaishnavas who were [there] learned this knowledge.  
**5.105.** Sri Ati Bada Jagannatha heard [it] together with the Vaishnavas,  
**5.106.** cast off all worldliness (?) and held this bhakti in his heart.  
**5.107.** Let all the people under his shelter (?) practise contemplation of this bhakti.[^05-24]  
**5.108.** Says the brahmin Divakara: my head is at the feet of Sri Guru.  

*Thus [ends], in the Sri Jagannathamrita, [the life of] Sri Ati Bada Jagannatha Gosain, the fifth chapter, called 'Anukramani' (?).*[^05-25]

[^05-1]: The 'wicked people' are not named. They may be opponents of the Atibadi line, including the Gaudiyas whom the author denounced in Ch. 3; this is a guess. ଗୁପ୍ତରେ: printed a small sign like ʃ + a sign like ସ୍ତ/ପ୍ତ + ରେ; taken as 'secretly, in a veiled way'. 5.3 is read uncertainly: ବିଦ୍ୟାର (printed ବ + a sign like ନ୍ୟା/ଦ୍ୟା + ର), ବର୍ଣ୍ଣନ (printed ବ + a sign with repha like ଣ୍ଣ + ନ), ନିନ୍ଦିବ (printed ନ + ନ୍ଦ + ବ). The link between the half-lines of 5.3 is unclear.
[^05-2]: The author's genealogy: Divakara, son of Janadeva (?) and Champa; grandfather Jagu Dasa, great-grandfather Nanda Dasa, great-great-grandfather Baidhara (?). The names are read uncertainly. ଜନଦେବ: printed a sign like ଜ + a sign like ନ, then ଦେବ (OCR ଛମମ ଦେଵ୍ଵ). ବାଇଧର: printed a sign like ବ/କ + ା + a dark ଇ/ଳ + ଧ + ର. These names have not been checked against other sources. Divakara is called fifth in succession from Jagannatha Dasa in the spiritual line, not by family (see Ch. 1, note to 1.1). The pravara of the Bharadvaja gotra is traditionally three rishis: Angiras, Brihaspati (Barhaspatya) and Bharadvaja. Printed ବୃହସ୍ପତ୍ୟଙ୍ଗିସ: ର and ା are not printed; the correction [ବୃହସ୍ପତ୍ୟାଙ୍ଗିରସ] is by sense. ବିନେ (5.8): printed ବ with a hook ି + େନ (OCR ବଚନେ); taken as 'apart from'.
[^05-3]: The source of the shloka has not been found. The metre is shikharini (17 syllables); it is clearly seen in the first and fourth lines of the shloka. The second and third lines are poorly printed. ଗୋ-ନଗରୀ: printed େଗା + dots + ନଗରୀ; the metre wants a light syllable here (ନ ଚ?). After ଦ୍ୱାରକା ନ comes a word of four signs (ବି/ଚି + a sign like ନ୍ଲ with subscript + a sign with a hook ି + a sign like ପ/ବ); unidentified. Third line: ନ କୁରୁ (Kurukshetra?) + ପୁ + a sign like େ/ଟ + ନ/ମ + କୁ (Pushkara?); ନ + a sign like ଗ/ଦ + a sign like ଙ୍ଗ + dots + a sign like ରା (Ganga?); ବା କ୍ଷେତ୍ରମ… (printed େକ୍ଷ + ତ୍ର + ମ + ୃ + େ + a sign like ମ/ସ; 'another kshetra'?). Fourth line: ହୃଦୟ (printed ହୃଦ + a sign like ୟ), ପ୍ରେମ୍ନାପି (printed a sign like ପ୍ର + େ + ମ୍ନ + ା + a sign like ପି), ନନ୍ଦତି (printed a sign like ନ + ନ୍ଦ/ନ୍ଧ + ତି) are guesses. The sense is clear from the paraphrase 5.10. 'The kshetras described before' are apparently 4.131–4.134, where Mathura, the Dvarakas, Ayodhya, Kashi and Vrindavana are said to abide in Purushottama. Madhupuri is Mathura. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas the highest abode is Vrindavana, the place of Krishna's lilas with the gopis (see the note to 4.131).
[^05-4]: *Puthi lekhili bedamāna* — 'I wrote (copied) books — the Vedas'; the author apparently speaks of his former occupation as a brahmin scribe. Gaudiya Vaishnavas accept the Vedas as the highest authority (shabda-pramana). For them the aim of the Vedas is Krishna: 'By all the Vedas I am to be known' (BG 15.15). So Mahaprabhu explained the Vedanta to Sarvabhauma and Prakashananda (CC, Madhya 6; Adi 7). Giving up the Vedas for 'Jagannatha alone' is foreign to the Gaudiyas. But they too place bhakti above book learning (CC, Madhya 8).
[^05-5]: 5.14–5.19 are the author on his method: Sanskrit shlokas 'from the Vedas (?)' with an Odia verse paraphrase. Chapters 4–5 are built this way. The readings are uncertain. ଛନ୍ଦକୁ (5.14): printed a dark ଛ/ଜ + a sign like ନ୍ଦ + କୁ; possibly ଜନକୁ, 'for people'. ତୋଳାକର (5.14; OCR ତୋଳାକର): taken as ତୋଳା କରି, 'gathering'; this is a guess. ରଚନା (5.15): printed a sign like ସ (= ର) + a sign like ୟ (= ଚ?) + ନା + a separate sign like ଁ/ି. ବେଦନ୍ତୁଁ (5.15): printed େବ + a sign like ଦ/ଳ + ନ୍ତୁଁ; taken as 'from the Vedas', possibly 'from the Vedanta'. The shlokas of Chapters 4–5 are not Vedic texts; their source has not been found (see the note to 4.73). The 'Gosain' (5.17) is not named. He is probably Jagannatha Dasa: in the colophons of Chs. 2 and 4 he is 'Ati Bada Jagannatha Gosain'. ଏଡ଼େ, ଯୋଗ୍ୟ (5.19): printed ଏ + a sign like ଡ଼ୁ/ତୁ; େଯା + ଗ + a sign like ଂ. ଦ୍ୟନ୍ତି = ଦିଅନ୍ତି. The words 'Jagannatha Himself speaks the book' are the author's claim. Cf. the humility of Krishnadasa Kaviraja, who wrote the CC by the order of Madana-Gopala (CC, Adi 8).
[^05-6]: 'His Name' is the Name of Jagannatha. Gaudiya Vaishnavas also teach that the Name fulfils desires and is not different from the Lord: 'The Name of Krishna is a chintamani, Krishna Himself' (CC, Madhya 17.133). ସମ୍ପଦେ (5.21): printed ସ + ମ୍ପ + େ + ଦ + comma. ଚଳିଲିଁ: printed a sign like ଚ/ପ + a sign like େଳ with an arc ି + a dark ଲ + ି + ଁ; possibly ପଶିଲିଁ, 'I entered'. The second half of 5.21 is unclear.
[^05-7]: By CC (Adi 13; Madhya 1), Mahaprabhu spent twenty-four years in sannyasa: the first six travelling (the South, Bengal, Vrindavana), the last eighteen without leaving Nilachala. Divakara places all twenty-four years in the kshetra. 'Free of care' (*niścinte*): printed ନଶ୍ଚିନ୍ତେ, ି not visible. ଯେହୁ ଯେ (5.22) means 'He who'. On Sarvabhauma see the notes to 4.4 and 4.157.
[^05-8]: ନିତ୍ୟାନନ୍ଦେ: printed ନ with a hook ି + ତ୍ୟା + ନ + େ + ନ୍ଦ (OCR ଛତ୍ଯୟାଳନହେ). In Divakara, Nityananda is among the Gaudiyas (see the note to 3.170). By CC (Madhya 15), Mahaprabhu sent Nityananda to preach in Gauda. He came to Puri with the Gaudiyas for the Ratha-yatra. Nityananda's question and Mahaprabhu's answer continue on p. 34.
[^05-9]: The source of the shloka has not been found. 'Hare Rama…' is the maha-mantra in the Odia order (see the notes to 3.147 and 4.103). The 'fruit of five' (*pañca*) is unclear; perhaps the five 'states' of 4.117–4.123 or the five kinds of liberation. Gaudiya Vaishnavas reject sayujya (merging with the Lord): pure devotees do not accept any of the five kinds of mukti even if the Lord Himself offers them (*Bhagavata* 3.29.13; cf. CC, Madhya 6 — Mahaprabhu's talk with Sarvabhauma on mukti). A kavacha (5.27) is an 'armour': protecting the parts of the body with the syllables of a mantra (nyasa); the list apparently continues on p. 34. Gaudiya Vaishnavas teach that the Name does not depend on diksha, purashcharana or other rites (CC, Madhya 15.108). କରୁଥେ: printed a dark ଭ/କ + ରୁ + େ + ଥ/ଯ (OCR ଭର୍ସୁସ); taken as 'they make, put on'.
[^05-10]: The source of the shloka has not been found. In it Mahadeva (Shiva), 'the best of the gods', speaks. Apparently the shloka is taken from a tantric dialogue of Shiva, and Mahaprabhu quotes it; in the Odia rendering (5.31) He addresses Nityananda. The beginning of the second line is poorly legible (printed ହ + a sign like ଦ + ଭ + a sign like ର୍ଯ + କ୍ତି). It is taken as *hari-bhakti-yuktaḥ*, 'endowed with devotion to Hari'; this is a guess. ନିତ୍ୟାନନ୍ଦ (5.31): printed a sign like ନ/କ with an ି hook + ତ୍ୟା + ନ + ନ୍ଦ, as in 5.25. Gaudiya Vaishnavas do not seek 'all power' (*sarvaiśvarya*) as the fruit of bhakti: pure bhakti is free from the desire for mystic powers (CC, Madhya 19.149; see the note to 4.126). Mahaprabhu, according to the CC, taught that the Name needs neither diksha nor rituals (CC, Madhya 15.108; see the note to 5.26). The CC and CB say nothing of a nyasa or kavacha of the mantra.
[^05-11]: 5.34–5.35 are unclear. The sense seems to be: Brahma, Shiva, Indra and the guardians of the quarters have power because they put the name 'Hara' in front. The connection of the half-verses is unclear; ମୁଳେ (printed ମ + a subscript hook + େଳ) and ଅଘୋର (printed ଅ + େ + a sign like ଦ/ଚ/ଘ + a stroke + ର) are read with doubt. 'Hara' here is apparently 'Hare', the first word of the mantra. In Gaudiya explanations of the mahamantra 'Hare' is the vocative of 'Hara' (Radha, the energy of Krishna). The order with 'Hare Rama' before 'Hare Krishna' is the Odia one (see the notes to 2.135 and 5.26).
[^05-12]: 5.32–5.38 are a nyasa: the six different syllables of the mantra 'Hare Rama Hare Krishna' (ha, re, ra, ma, kri, shna) are placed on parts of the body. In the shloka (5.32–5.33) 'ha' is placed on the nose, in the Odia rendering (5.36) on the head; the rest agrees. The syllable 'shna' (*ṣṇa*) is printed with the letter ଖ (*kha*): in Odia pronunciation ଷ sounds like 'kh', and old manuscripts often write ଖ for ଷ. Likewise in 5.58. ରାକାର (5.32) and ରାକ୍ଷର (5.37): printed a sign like ସ/ଷ, taken as the ସ୍ୱ-like ରା; this is a guess based on the syllables of the mantra (otherwise 'sha'). ମଧ୍ୟେଣ (5.32) is not identified. କୃକାରୋ (5.33): printed a bold sign like ଖ + a subscript ୃ; accepted from the Odia rendering in 5.38 (କୃକାର).
[^05-13]: Shloka 5.39 is a kavacha made of the names of gopis and cowherd boys. The first eight are Radha's eight principal sakhis, as with the Gaudiya Vaishnavas: Lalita, Vishakha, Champakalata, Chitra, Rangadevi, Sudevi, Tungavidya, Indulekha (Rupa Gosvami, *Radha-Krishna-ganoddesha-dipika*). Next come Shashirekha, Vimala, Palika, Manjari (in 5.51, Ananga-manjari; for the Gaudiyas, Radha's younger sister), Shyamala, Madhumati, Dhanya, Mangala. Then Krishna's friends: Sridama, Sudama, Vasudama, Arjuna, Subala, Kinkini. The last word of the shloka (printed ବରୁଥ… ≈ ନା/ଳା) is apparently the name of Varuthapa, one of Krishna's friends (it occurs in the Gaudiya lists of sakhas). According to the OCR, p. 36 goes on to name Vrishabha, Devaprastha, Vishala. Radha and Krishna Himself are not named in the list (apart from the unclear 'Krishna' in the last line). Unclear places: ପାଳଶଙ୍ଗଂ (printed ପା + a sign like ଳ + ଶ + ଙ୍ଗଂ; likewise 5.44; perhaps *pārśvāṅgam*, 'the side'). ମୂର୍ଦ୍ଧନିଂ — 'the crown of the head'. ଭାଲଂ: printed a sign like ଭ/ଗ + ଲଂ (or ଗଳଂ, 'the neck'). ମଙ୍ଗଳାର୍ଦ୍ଧଂ ଉଦାବସ୍ଥା: by 5.55, apparently 'the belly'. ପାଦବାଚୈକ: printed ପାଦବା + େ + a sign like ଚ with the ୈ flag + କ; by 5.61, 'the left foot'. The word before କୃଷ୍ଣଃ (printed ଦ + a sign like ଶ/ଝ + a sign like ଟ + a sign like ସ୍ତ୍ରା + ≈ କ) is not identified; perhaps '[in] the eastern [and other] quarters'. Gaudiya Vaishnavas honour the sakhis and manjaris and serve them in raga-bhakti under the guidance of the guru. But a kavacha made of their names and the syllables of the mantra is not described in the CC or CB; it is an Odia practice.
[^05-14]: 5.40–5.61 are the Odia rendering of shloka 5.39. Each gopi or cowherd boy is given a syllable of the mantra. The syllables follow the order of the mahamantra 'Hare Rama Hare Rama Rama Rama Hare Hare / Hare Krishna Hare Krishna…': ha (Lalita), re (Vishakha), ra (Champakalata), ma (Chitra); ha-re-ra (Rangadevi with Sudevi), ma (Tungavidya); ra (Indulekha), ma (Shashirekha), ra (Vimala), ma (Palika); ha (Ananga-manjari), re (5.52), ha (Madhumati), re (Dhanya); ha (Mangala), re (Sudama), kri (Sridama), shna (Sudama), ha (Arjuna), re (Subala), kri (Kinkini). The list continues on p. 36. Unclear places: ଚତା[ଚିତ୍ରା] (5.43, ି and ୍ର not visible); ମଆକୁ (5.43): printed ମ + a sign like ଥ/ଅ + ା + କୁ, possibly ମଥାକୁ, 'the head'. ହରେରା ଏ ତିନି (5.45): printed ହରେ + a sign like ସ/ଷ; then a sign like େ/ତ + ନ; taken as 'these three [syllables] ha-re-ra' from the sequence of syllables. ଭାଲରେ (5.50): printed a sign like ଭ/ଢ + ଲ + ରେ. ରହୁ (5.50): possibly ରକ୍ଷୁ. In 5.52, instead of a name (in the shloka, Shyamala) ମକାର is printed (printed ମ + a dark sign like କ + ା + ର), 'the syllable "ma"'; this does not fit the sequence of syllables, which requires 're' here. ଧରୁ (5.52): printed a sign like ଧ/ଭ + ରୁ. ହୁଅ (5.55): printed ହ with a subscript ୁ + ଅ. 5.56–5.58: in the shloka Sridama protects the hips (*jaghana*), Sudama the thighs, Vasudama the knees. In the rendering: Sudama the shanks (ଜଙ୍ଘ, printed ଜ + a sign like ଘ with a dot), Sridama the thighs, Sudama (instead of Vasudama) [the legs] down to the knees. କୃକ୍ଷିର[କୃକ୍ଷର] (5.61) = *kṛ-akṣara*; the printed ି is probably superfluous.
[^05-15]: The name of the guardian of the south is not identified. Printed ଜ + a sign like ସ with a bold subscript + a sign like ଶ/ଷ with ୁ + ଙ୍କ (OCR କଗ୍ପଗ୍ିଙଂ). One would expect the name of one of Krishna's friends, as in the neighbouring lines. ଘର (printed a sign like ଘ/ଧ with a dot + ର) is taken as 'place'; possibly ଧର, 'hold'. ଖକାର: the first sign is a loop like ε, as ଖ; ା is printed as a tiny stroke.
[^05-16]: 5.62–5.71 give the guardians of the ten directions: the eight quarters, above and below. They are Krishna's friends from Vraja: Stoka-Krishna (east, as in the shloka on p. 35: *prācīṁ … stokakṛṣṇaḥ*), Mahabahu (west, and again above, 5.70), an unidentified name (south), Vishala (south-west), Mahabala (north), Vrishabha (north-west), Devaprastha (5.68, printed 'in the north', although the north was already named in 5.66; the south-east is not named and may be meant), Ujjvala (north-east). Ananta guards below. Stoka-Krishna, Vishala, Vrishabha, Devaprastha and Ujjvala are named as Krishna's friends also in Rupa Gosvami's *Radha-Krishna-ganoddesha-dipika*. The syllables continue the sequence of the maha-mantra from 5.61 (Kinkini — 'kri'): 'shna', 'kri', 'shna' (?), 'two syllables' (Vishala), 'shna' (?), 'two syllables' (Vrishabha), 're', 'ha', 're', and for Ananta 'Om' (?) (printed a sign like ଓ + ଁ), which is not in the mantra. There are more syllables here than remain to the end of the mantra (9), so the sequence matches only roughly. ଦୁଇ (5.65, 5.67): printed a sign like କୁ/ଦୁ (in 5.67 like ହୃ with a knot) + ଇ, as ଦୁଇ in 5.57. Gaudiya Vaishnavas honour Krishna's friends, but no such kavacha is described in the CC or CB (see the note to 5.39).
[^05-17]: 5.72 is poorly legible. ଅଷ୍ଟା (printed ଅ + a sign like ଷ୍ଟ/ସ୍ଥ + ା) is taken as 'the eight [quarters]'; possibly ଆସ୍ଥା, 'faith' ('the wise will keep faith in Shyama'). ବୁଧା: printed ବୁ + ଧ + a broken ା/ଃ. The last word (printed େ + a dark loop + ମ) is taken as ପ୍ରେମ; possibly କ୍ଷେମ, 'welfare'. ଗୋପୀଜନ (5.73): printed େଗା + a hook ି + ଜନ; ପ apparently did not print.
[^05-18]: 5.74–5.85 give the colour and power of each syllable: 'ha' — white, the best of all letters (5.74); 're' — red (5.76); 'ra' — golden (5.78); 'ma' — of the form of light, Niranjana (5.80); 'kri' — black like collyrium (5.82); 'shna' — white like the light of the sun and moon (5.84). Each syllable removes its own kind of sin. ହୁଅ (5.74): printed ହ with a tail + a sign like ଅ without its right stroke; taken as ହଅ, '[the syllable] "ha"' (cf. ହଅକୁ 5.51, ହୁଅ 5.55; likewise ରାଅ 5.79). 5.76 is unclear: the ends of both half-lines (printed like ଯା + େ + ସ୍ତ/ପ୍ତ and like ବ/କ + ର୍ଣ୍ଣ + େ + ସ୍ତ/ପ୍ତ) are not identified; the first is taken as ବ୍ୟାପ୍ତେ, 'pervades'. *Grahaṇe* (5.79) — 'at an eclipse'; sins committed during an eclipse are held in the smriti to be especially grave; it may also mean 'in accepting [what is another's]'. *Nirañjana* (5.80) — 'stainless', in the Odia Panchasakha tradition a name of the Supreme (cf. the teaching on Shunya; see the note to 1.1). ଘାତ (5.83): printed ଜ + a broken ା + ତ; the correction 'betrayal of trust' is by sense. ଚନ୍ଦ୍ରମାଙ୍କ (5.84): the last sign is like ଙ୍କ. The colours of the syllables of the maha-mantra are not described in the CC, CB, VL or MP.
[^05-19]: The syllable 'ra' is compared to Radha's golden body. Printed a dot + ଧା + ଙ୍କ: ରା did not print. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas 'Hare' in the maha-mantra is the vocative of 'Hara', a name of Radha as Krishna's energy (see the note to 5.34); 'Rama' they understand as Krishna, 'Radha-ramana' (or as Balarama).
[^05-20]: According to the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, even the semblance of the Name (*namabhasa*) destroys all sins and gives liberation. So Haridasa Thakura explained in his dispute with a brahmin (CC, Antya 3; the example of Ajamila — *Bhagavata* 6.2). But the aim of chanting the Name is not freedom from sin but prema; sins depart incidentally, as darkness does at the first glimmer of the rising sun (CC, Antya 3). This is also the point of 5.86–5.87: bhakti is born of the Name.
[^05-21]: The source of the shloka is not found. The Sanskrit is irregular: dative and locative are mixed, and there is no verb ('should not be given' is implied). ପରେଷୋଽଜିତେନ୍ଦ୍ରିୟଃ: printed ପର + େଷା + avagraha + ଜ + େତ + ନ୍ଦ୍ର + ୟଃ, ି not visible; *pareṣo* is not identified, possibly *paradveṣo*, 'hating others'. ବାଚାଳେ: printed ବା + a sign like ଚ୍ଳ + େଳ. Cf. *Bhagavad-gita* 18.67: 'This should never be spoken to one who is not austere, not devoted…'. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas, instructing the faithless and averse in the Name is the tenth offence against the Name (*nama-aparadha*) according to the *Padma Purana* (quoted in *Hari-bhakti-vilasa* 11). 5.89–5.91 are the Odia rendering of the shloka; 'the disciple of another path' (5.89) is an addition of the rendering.
[^05-22]: The revilers of this book are called asuras of Lanka: they heard Rama's name (in the battle with Rama) and for this obtained human birth. This legend is not found in this form in the *Ramayana*. It is polemic: who the revilers are the author does not say (cf. the note to 5.2). Gaudiya Vaishnavas also teach that even hostile hearing of the Name purifies (thus Shishupala, who reviled Krishna all his life, attained liberation; *Bhagavata* 7.1). But they do not connect this with accepting a book of the Atibadi line.
[^05-23]: 5.95–5.101 give the fruits (*phala-shruti*) of the kavacha: bhakti for Krishna's feet, fearlessness of Yama, protection from planets, spirits, king, beasts and enemies, wealth and grain. Gaudiya Vaishnavas do not seek such fruits: pure bhakti is free from other desires (Rupa Gosvami, *Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu* 1.1.11). Mahaprabhu prays in the *Shikshashtaka*: 'I do not ask for wealth, followers…' (CC, Antya 20.29). ଉତ୍ତମ ଜାତ ଦୁଷ୍ଟ (5.100) is read and understood uncertainly. ଚୋର (5.98): printed େ + a sign like ସ୍ତ/ସ୍ୱ + ର, no ା visible; 'thief' by the series 'king, tiger, crocodile'. ନିର୍ଭୟ: printed a dark ନ with ି + ର୍ଭ + ୟ. ସଂସାରୁ (5.101): printed a sign like ଯ/ସ + ସା + ରୁ, ଂ not visible.
[^05-24]: According to Divakara, Mahaprabhu expounds the kavacha to Nityananda in the presence of brahmins and Vaishnavas; Jagannatha Dasa ('Ati Bada') hears it together with the Vaishnavas and accepts this bhakti, and the people under his shelter are to practise it. This is a claim of the Atibadi line: the teaching of the kavacha passes from Mahaprabhu through Jagannatha Dasa to his followers. The CC and CB do not know this episode and do not mention Jagannatha Dasa. In the VL Jagannatha Dasa meets Mahaprabhu at Bata-Ganesha (VL 4). Gaudiya Vaishnavas accept the succession of Mahaprabhu's teaching through Svarupa Damodara and Rupa and Sanatana Gosvamis, while Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati names the Atibadi line ('ativadi') in the list of apasampradayas (see the note to 1.1). On Nityananda in Puri see the note to 5.25. ଭବ (5.106): printed a sign like ଭ/ଗ୍ୱ + ବ; possibly ଗର୍ବ, 'pride'. ଆଶ୍ରୟେ (5.107): printed ଅ + a sign like ଷ/ଶ୍ର + a sign like େ + ୟ.
[^05-25]: The colophon is set in two lines, as at the end of Chapter 4 (see the note to the colophon of Ch. 4). Chapter 5 too is called 'Anukramani': printed ଅନୁକର୍ମଣି, taken as ଅନୁକ୍ରମଣି. A rule follows the colophon. The next page (PDF 38) apparently begins Chapter 6.

---

# Divakara Dasa. *Jagannatha-charitamrita*, Chapter 6
## The Murti of Jagannatha, the Lord above the Lord: the Lotus of the *Brahma-samhita*, Goloka in the Puri Temple, Mantra, Yantra and Tantra


*Chapter Six*

**6.1.** Listen, good people, to the teaching of Sri Chaitanya.  
**6.2.** At the darshan of Sri Jagannatha, Prabhu abides with a contented heart.  
**6.3.** All the Vaishnavas put a question to Prabhu:  
**6.4.** "Here is the murti of Jagannatha: by what feeling (?) does one obtain prema-bhakti [in Him]?"  
**6.5.** Hearing these words, Gosain said: "Listen attentively.  

*Shloka*  
**6.6.** *[Skt.]* The supreme Lord is Krishna; His form is eternal being, knowledge and bliss. He is beginningless and Himself the origin of all; He is Govinda, the cause of all causes.[^06-1]  
**6.7.** The Lord above the Lord, beyond the supreme, the Highest;  
**6.8.** this is Krishna above Krishna, Brahman above Brahman;  
**6.9.** the Beginningless above the beginningless, the cause of all, the supreme perfection (?).  
**6.10.** Sri Jagannatha, who [is] this murti: from Him indeed (?) is the origin (?) of all.[^06-2]  

*Shloka*  
**6.11.** *[Skt.]* In the whorl (?) [of the lotus] is the central yantra, hexagonal, with a diamond pivot; the place of the six-limbed, six-footed (??) [mantra], with prakriti and purusha. In the midst of the yantra (?) of the great lotus one should practise the hexagonal yantra.  
**6.12.** The place of six limbs and six feet: Radha and Krishna on one seat.  
**6.13.** Here, in the earthly world (?), They abide; … (??) having divided (?): of that I speak.  

*Shloka*  
**6.14.** *[Skt.]* The world (?) abides in the rasa of the bliss of prema and the great bliss, joined with the mantra (?) in the form of light, with the kama-bija.[^06-3]  
**6.15.** The bliss of prema and the great bliss become bliss through rasa.  
**6.16.** These two, being in the form of light, abide in the kama-bija.  
**6.17.** The beginning and end (?) of this shloka, that 'surrounded' (??), I shall tell further on.[^06-4]  

*Shloka*  
**6.18.** *[Skt.]* Let the placing of its bija (?) be fourfold (??); there are the petals, [the abodes] of the Shris (?). They are the four murtis, fourfold (??), inconceivable (??), supreme, 'pluta'.[^06-5]  
**6.19.** Amid the filaments of that lotus, however many petals are in it,  
**6.20.** in the four quarters (?) are dharma, moksha [and the other goals]: these They grant visibly.  
**6.21.** Thus the Chaturdha-murti: the four abide here.[^06-6]  
**6.22.** Goloka is nitya, [then] maha-nitya; in parama-nitya the four (?) abide.  
**6.23.** The Bada-danda is called Goloka; it is also the twenty-two steps (?).  
**6.24.** Maha-nitya is the jagamohana; parama-nitya is the throne.[^06-7]  
**6.25.** That which is called 'fourfold prolonged' (chatuh-pluta) is (??) in the svaras of the Veda (?).[^06-8]  
**6.26.** These words of prema … (??) Sri Chaitanya has spoken.[^06-9]  

*Shloka*  
**6.27.** *[Skt.]* The essence of mantra, the essence of yantra, the essence of tantra, the abode; … (??) and the essence of prema, and the brahma-vidya Shodashi.[^06-10]  
**6.28.** In the mantra the essence is the bija of the mantra; in the yantra, the Sri Murti in full (?).  
**6.29.** In the tantra is Sri Jagannatha: such is the place declared (??) by all.  
**6.30.** However many deeds one does with the body, however many vows (?) one keeps on earth,  
**6.31.** the aim of deeds at the end of the body: this is therefore (?) called tattva (?).  
**6.32.** Whoever meditates on this Jagannatha thereby (?) obtains the tantra fully.[^06-11]  
**6.33.** In this murti is prema itself; in Him is the play of rasa.  
**6.34.** This is Brahman manifest, [the true] Brahman; He bears the sixteen kalas.  
**6.35.** Taking all these kalas, They form the array (?) of the body (?).[^06-12]  

*Shloka*  
**6.36.** *[Skt.]* The yantra and tantra of the bliss of prema, adorned with the mandala of prema; in the tasting of prema the Devi … (??) Vipureshvara (??).[^06-13]  

## The murti of Jagannatha as the source of avataras, tirthas and Vedas; Omkara, Shesha and Subhadra; Radha and Krishna in the daru-murti


**6.37.** Radha, the bliss of prema, shines; this is the yantra and the tantra, infinite (?).[^06-14]  
**6.38.** Her radiance (?) has adorned this whole mandala of prema: [such is] the tantra.  
**6.39.** Filled (?) with bliss in prema is the rasika-deva Jagannatha.  

*Shloka*  
**6.40.** *[Skt.]* "The imperishable is the supreme Brahman, the perishable is all this world; in the form of the perishable and the imperishable abides Purushottama."[^06-15]  
**6.41.** That form which is not a perishable (??) creation is called Parabrahman.  
**6.42.** The world in which (?) birth takes place (?) is called perishable in form.  
**6.43.** This murti of Jagannatha is the essence, imperishable kalpa after kalpa.  

*Shloka*  
**6.44.** *[Skt.]* "The abode (??) of all avataras, worshipped first among the most excellent, abiding (?); the perishable and the imperishable, Tara-brahma, the Supreme Self (?), Purushottama."[^06-16]  
**6.45.** As many avataras of Vishnu as there are, all come forth from this murti.  
**6.46.** As many kshetras as there are on earth (?), all are born from this kshetra.  
**6.47.** As many tirthas as there are in this world (?), all come forth from these feet.  
**6.48.** As many things (?) as there are in the world, all are born from this womb.  
**6.49.** As many books of the Vedas and shastras as there are, all are born (?) from this word (?).  
**6.50.** As many moving and unmoving [beings] as there are, all are born from this body.  
**6.51.** This Jagannatha is the Supreme: from Him are born all the Brahmas (?).  
**6.52.** He is the highest among the purushas of the Veda; He (??) is incomparable in this body.  

*Shloka*  
**6.53.** *[Skt.]* "Omkara is the seed-measure; in the field is the circle of the knower of the field. The syllable 'a' is the middle measure, and brahma-vidya is not (?) established [in it]."[^06-17]  
**6.54.** Born from Omkara, the Vedas [and] Puranas (?) multiplied,  
**6.55.** all mantras, the essence of yantras, various kinds of knowledge, the expansion (?) of Brahman.  
**6.56.** If one examines and understands the matter, all is born from Omkara alone.  
**6.57.** Some (?), not understanding (?) Omkara, sit practising brahma-jnana.  
**6.58.** Born (?) in Purushottama, the kshetras and tirthas spread over the earth (?).  
**6.59.** From this (??) murti came forth countless avataras;  
**6.60.** by various feats they won fame and became the greatest in the world.  
**6.61.** The seeds (?) of the moving and unmoving … (??): all are born from this kshetra.  

*Shloka*  
**6.62.** *[Skt.]* "The syllable 'ma' is the full measure; one should remember the bindu (??) as Purna-brahman. Beyond it, with the mark (?) of Shesha, abides … (??) of the moon."[^06-18]  
**6.63.** 'A', 'e' [= 'u'] (?), 'ma': the Supreme Self, the essence of Brahman.  
**6.64.** On the head of Shesha-deva is the couch-bed: there He abides (?).  

*Shloka*  
**6.65.** *[Skt.]* "Beyond it is [the place] of Subhadra: on the bed of prema and of happiness (?); in her lap (?) is the kama-bija; one should remember it as fivefold (?)."[^06-19]  
**6.66.** Subhadra, exceedingly sweet, on (??) the bed of prema, full of happiness,  
**6.67.** unites the kama-bija: that is the fivefold … (??).  

*Shloka*  
**6.68.** *[Skt.]* "Having given the rasa of the bliss of rasa, having enjoyed (?) here before, the chief … (??), immersed in madness, is the contemplation of rasa."[^06-20]  
**6.69.** Sri Radha gives rasa, Sri Krishna relishes the rasa.  
**6.70.** Immersed in the highest rasa, the two abide in two bhavas.  
**6.71.** They know that happiness in the world (?); who else is there besides Them?  
**6.72.** In the bliss of prema, in the bhava of prema, They are immersed, full of happiness.  
**6.73.** Intoxicated by the embrace of prema, Jagannatha with Radha  
**6.74.** abide, obtaining great happiness: the two in this daru-murti.  

## End of Chapter 6: the Path of Raga to the Murti of Jagannatha; Ati Bada Records Mahaprabhu's Teaching


**6.75.** [Both] abide in the happiness of supreme bliss, intoxicated with the happiness of great bliss.[^06-21]  
**6.76.** Since the two blisses are one, therefore [this] is the form of sat-cit-ananda.  
**6.77.** That which the shastras call (?) prema: this feeling of prema one obtains here.  
**6.78.** The raga [sought] in worship by the path of raga is obtained right here.  
**6.79.** Whatever (?) bhakti there is with the feeling of prema, it is in the murti of Jagannatha.  
**6.80.** Worship Him by the path of raga; cast the service of others (?) far away.[^06-22]  
**6.81.** Bhakti is of nine kinds; mukti is obtained in four kinds.  
**6.82.** If one sets the mind on extensive injunctions, the eighteen siddhis arise.  
**6.83.** Of eighty-one (?) kinds of bhakti the saints have written in the shastras.  
**6.84.** In many ways there is disagreement: [so] are bhajana [and] sadhana arranged.[^06-23]  
**6.85.** Whoever meditates by whatever doctrine, all of them (?) will come here.  
**6.86.** At the darshan (?) [of Him] they will obtain bhakti [and] mukti, all perfections according to their wish (?).  
**6.87.** This Jagannatha is the fulfilment of desires; and in desirelessness, all perfection (?).[^06-24]  
**6.88.** Thus spoke the words that Prabhu, Krishna Chaitanya.  
**6.89.** All the Vaishnavas, as many as were [there], heard and were pleased.  
**6.90.** Ati Bada Gosain was [there]: he heard the command of Prabhu.  
**6.91.** He kept this discourse in mind [and] wrote [it] down in shlokas (?) [and] chaupadis.  
**6.92.** Moreover (?), that book of 'fruit' (??) he compiled, making a grantha.  
**6.93.** There he set [it] out in detail, as Prabhu's command was.  
**6.94.** All this discourse he reads in the jagamohana hall.[^06-25]  
**6.95.** Thus Chaitanya Hari, proceeding, went to [His] residence.  
**6.96.** This fivefold (?) word of prema Prabhu Chaitanya spoke.[^06-26]  
**6.97.** Says the brahmin Divakara: at the feet of that Prabhu is my head.  
**6.98.** This book is an exposition of bhakti, the practice of svakiya bhakti.  
**6.99.** Sri Ati Bada Gosain: at his feet I abide.  
**6.100.** For the svakiya Vaishnavas this book is their wealth.[^06-27]  

*Thus [ends], in the Sri Jagannathamrita, [the life of] Ati Bada Gosain, the sixth chapter, called 'Anukramani' (?).*[^06-28]

[^06-1]: Shloka 6.6 is *Brahma-samhita* 5.1, verbatim. Krishnadasa Kaviraja also quotes it (CC, Adi 2.107). Mahaprabhu found the *Brahma-samhita* in the South and brought it to Puri (CC, Madhya 9); Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati published it with his commentary. The reading is secure.
[^06-2]: 6.7–6.10 interpret shloka 6.6 in the spirit of the Atibadi line. The shloka calls Krishna the supreme Lord, while the author makes the murti of Jagannatha 'Krishna above Krishna' and 'Brahman above Brahman', from whom all originates. Cf. the notes to 4.52 (Jagannatha as Parabrahman and source of the avatars) and 2.22. For the Gaudiya Vaishnavas Jagannatha is Krishna Himself (CC, Madhya 13), not a stage above Krishna. There is nothing above Krishna, Svayam Bhagavan: *kṛṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam* (*Bhagavata* 1.3.28; CC, Adi 2). Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati counts the 'ativadis' among the apasampradayas, for such teachings among others. Unclear readings: ପରହିଦ୍ଧି (6.9): the third sign is ହି, not ସି; taken as ପରସିଦ୍ଧି, 'supreme perfection' (or *prasiddhi*, 'renowned'). ଏଥୁହି (6.10): after ଏଥୁ stands a sign that in 6.34 clearly reads ହି (ଏହି, rhyming with ବହନ୍ତି); taken as *ethu̐ hi̐*, 'from Him indeed'. ଉତପତ୍ତି: the third sign looks like ε; taken from the sense.
[^06-3]: Shlokas 6.11 and 6.14 together are *Brahma-samhita* 5.3, with differences. The *Brahma-samhita* reads: *karṇikāraṁ mahad yantraṁ ṣaṭ-koṇaṁ vajra-kīlakam / ṣaḍ-aṅga-ṣaṭ-padī-sthānaṁ prakṛtyā puruṣeṇa ca / premānanda-mahānanda-rasenāvasthitaṁ hi yat / jyotī-rūpeṇa manunā kāma-bījena saṅgatam* ('the whorl [of the lotus of Gokula] is a great hexagonal yantra with a diamond pivot; the place of the six-limbed, six-footed [eighteen-syllable] mantra, with prakriti and purusha; abiding in the rasa of the bliss of prema and great bliss, joined with the mantra of light, the kama-bija'). Here is printed *karṇikāre* (?) *madhyaṁ* for *karṇikāraṁ mahad*, and *jagat* for *hi yat*. The third line of 6.11 ('in the midst of the yantra of the great lotus one should practise the hexagonal yantra') is absent from the *Brahma-samhita*; it is a precept of tantric sadhana. ଷଡ୍ବାସ୍ପ (6.11): printed ଷଡ଼ଙ୍ଗଷଡ୍ + ବା + a sign like ସ୍ପ; corrected after the *Brahma-samhita* to ଷଟ୍ପଦୀ. ମନୁନା (6.14): the last sign looks like ଜା/ନା. In 6.12 'the place of six limbs and six feet' is identified with the seat where Radha and Krishna sit together. 6.13: ମର୍ତ୍ତ୍ୟରେ: a sign with an arc ≈ ର୍ତ୍ତ/ଣ୍ଡି; taken as 'in the earthly world' (or ମଣ୍ଡିରେ?). ଦେବା ଭେଦାଇ is not understood: 'God, having divided [into two]'? or 'to give, having distinguished'?
[^06-4]: 6.17 is unclear. ଅଦ୍ୟପ୍ରାନ୍ତ is taken as *ādya-prānta*, 'beginning and end'. ବୃତ may be *vṛta*, 'surrounded', as in *Brahma-samhita* 5.5 of the square around Gokula (*caturbhir hetubhir vṛtam*), or *vṛtta*, 'account'. The author apparently promises to give the continuation of the shloka; 6.18 is indeed close to *Brahma-samhita* 5.4–5.5.
[^06-5]: Shloka 6.18 is badly corrupted and only partly understood. The second half of the first line is *Brahma-samhita* 5.4 (*tat-patrāṇi śriyām api*, 'its petals are [the abodes] of the Shris', i.e. the gopis). 'The four murtis': cf. 5.5 (*catur-mūrteś catur-dhāma*). ତଦ୍ବୀଜନ୍ୟାସଂ: the dark sign is taken as ବୀ, as in କାମବୀଜେନ (6.14); the *Brahma-samhita* has here *tat-kiñjalkaṁ tad-aṁśānām* ('its filaments are [the abodes] of His portions'). ଚତସ୍ୟାତ୍ / ଚତସ୍ୟାଦଚିନ୍ତ୍ୟଃ: the second sign looks like ଛ; taken as *catuḥ syāt*, 'let it be fourfold', and *catuḥ syād acintyaḥ*, 'fourfold, inconceivable'. ଶ୍ରିୟାମପି: the ending is printed like ମତି. 'Pluta' is a prolonged (three-mora) vowel; cf. 6.25. There is no '॥' at the end.
[^06-6]: The Chaturdha-murti are the four Deities of the Puri altar (Balabhadra, Subhadra, Jagannatha, Sudarshana); see the note to 4.25. Here they are identified with the 'four murtis' of shloka 6.18, i.e. placed in the lotus of Gokula. In 6.20 ଚତୁଷ୍ପାଦ, 'four parts (quarters)', apparently means the four goals of life (dharma, artha, kama, moksha; cf. *Brahma-samhita* 5.5, *caturbhiḥ puruṣārthaiś ca*). The Gaudiya Vaishnavas see the highest goal beyond all four, in prema (CC, Adi 7.84).
[^06-7]: 6.22–6.24 transfer the abodes of Goloka onto the Puri temple. Goloka is the Bada-danda, the grand road of Puri (cf. VL 5.144, 5.163), and the Baisi Pahacha, the twenty-two steps leading from the Lion Gate to the main temple. Maha-nitya is the jagamohana, the hall before the sanctum. Parama-nitya is the throne, the ratna-vedi, where the four abide (6.22). ପାବଚ୍ଛ: printed ପାବ + a sign like ଛ/ଚ୍ଛ; taken as 'steps' (*pāhāca*). These names of abodes (nitya, maha-nitya, parama-nitya) do not occur in CC/CB. For the Gaudiyas too Puri (Nilachala) is a transcendental abode; Mahaprabhu saw in Jagannatha the Krishna of Vrindavana (CC, Madhya 13), and in the route of the Ratha-yatra the way from Dvaraka to Vrindavana.
[^06-8]: 6.25 is unclear. In Vedic phonetics 'pluta' is a vowel three morae long; 'chatuh-pluta', 'fourfold pluta', is apparently linked here with the four murtis (cf. 6.18 *paramaḥ plutaḥ*). The first word of the second half-verse: printed a dark ସ with a tail + ର; taken as ସ୍ୱର, 'svara', tone (or ସ୍ମୃତି?). Then a sign like ε + ଛି under an ି arc; taken as ଅଛି, 'is' (the metre gives 9 syllables).
[^06-9]: ଦଶାହ୍ନିକ is not identified: printed ଦ + a dark sign like ଶା/ଷା + ହ୍ନି + କ. 6.26 is the narrator's; apparently the end of the first part of Mahaprabhu's answer.
[^06-10]: The source of shloka 6.27 has not been found. Shodashi ('the sixteenfold') is, in the Shakta tantras, a name of the goddess Tripurasundari and her sixteen-syllable vidya. Brahma-vidya is knowledge of Brahman. Here all these are epithets of Jagannatha as the 'essence' of mantra, yantra, tantra and prema; cf. 6.34 (sixteen kalas). ବିତତ୍: printed ବି + ତ + a sign like ତ୍ତ୍/ଭ୍ with virama; not identified (perhaps *viddhi*, 'know'). The first word is printed like ମରସାରଂ; taken as ମନ୍ତ୍ରସାରଂ after the series yantra-, tantra-sara.
[^06-11]: 6.28–6.32 present Jagannatha as mantra, yantra and tantra; cf. the notes to 4.67 (Jagannatha in the form of the mantra) and 4.29. The teaching of Jagannatha as tantra belongs to the Odia tantric tradition; it is not in CC/CB. The Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, approach Jagannatha not by tantric sadhana but by pure bhakti, by hearing and chanting the Name (CC, Madhya 22; Antya 4.70–4.71). Unclear readings: ବିସ୍ତର (6.28): printed ବ + a sign like ସ୍ତୁ + ର. ଉକତ (6.29): printed ଉ + a dark sign like କ + ତ, taken as *ukta*, 'declared'; ଏତ, 'such' (the rhyme with ଜଗନ୍ନାଥ is imperfect, as in 4.53). ଭ୍ରତ (6.30) is corrected to ବ୍ରତ, 'vow'. ତେନ (6.31, 6.32): printed େ + ତ + ନ; taken as Skt. *tena*, 'therefore, thereby'. ତତ୍ତ୍ୱ (6.31): the last sign resembles ତନ୍ତ୍ର in 6.29; ତତ୍ତ୍ୱ is taken for the rhyme with ଅର୍ଥ.
[^06-12]: 'Sixteen kalas' are sixteen digits of fullness (like the sixteen digits of the moon); in the tantras, a mark of the complete Deity (cf. 'sixteen-syllable' in 4.67 and Shodashi in 6.27). The Gaudiya Vaishnavas, after *Bhagavata* 1.3.28, call Krishna the source of all avatars rather than the possessor of a certain number of portions. 6.35 is unclear. ଦେହର: printed ଦେ + a sign like ସ୍ୱ/ହ + ର. ଅବଳୀ is taken as *āvalī*, 'row, array'. 'They' are apparently the Four (6.21–6.22).
[^06-13]: The source of shloka 6.36 has not been found. The second line is poorly understood. କଥନ୍ତୁ: printed କ + ଥ + a sign like ନ୍ତୁ/ୟୁ. ବିପୁରେଶ୍ୱର: printed ବି + ପୁ + ରେ + ଶ୍ୱ + ର; perhaps ତ୍ରିପୁରେଶ୍ୱରୀ, 'the Mistress of Tripura' (cf. Shodashi in 6.27). There is no '॥' at the end. The rendering of the shloka is apparently on p. 40 (the next portion).
[^06-14]: 6.37–6.39 paraphrase shloka 6.36: its "Devi" who relishes prema is Radha (cf. the note to 6.36). Jagannatha is called "rasika-deva", the relisher of rasa. For the Gaudiya Vaishnavas Jagannatha is Krishna Himself, Radha's Beloved: at the Ratha-yatra Mahaprabhu, in Radha's mood, saw in Him Krishna met at Kurukshetra (CC, Madhya 13). But "the yantra and tantra of prema" is the author's tantric language; the CC/CB do not speak of Radha in this way. ଅନନ୍ତ (6.37): printed ଅ + a sign like ଃ + ନ୍ତ, the second ନ not visible. ତେଜ and ତନ୍ତ୍ର (6.38) are read from the shapes of the signs; possibly ତତ୍ତ୍ୱ, "tattva".
[^06-15]: The source of shloka 6.40 has not been found. The idea is close to *Bhagavad-gita* 15.16–15.18: all beings are the perishable (*kṣara*), the unchanging is the imperishable (*akṣara*), and Purushottama is beyond both. Here, however, Purushottama abides "in the form of both the perishable and the imperishable". Purushottama is also a name of Puri (Purushottama-kshetra) and of Jagannatha; cf. 6.58. In 6.41 the key word is not identified (printed କ୍ଷ + a sign like ସୃ + ଷ୍ଟି); taken as "perishable creation" (କ୍ଷର-ସୃଷ୍ଟି?). In 6.42 the sign like ହୁ (in ହୁଏ and ଯେହୁ) is ହ with a bold dot below.
[^06-16]: 6.44–6.61: the murti of Jagannatha is the source of all the avataras of Vishnu, of kshetras and tirthas, of the Vedas and shastras, of all beings and of "all the Brahmas" (?). Cf. the notes to 4.52 (Jagannatha as Parabrahman and source of the avataras) and to 6.7 (Jagannatha as "Krishna above Krishna"). For the Gaudiya Vaishnavas the source of all avataras is Krishna (*ete cāṁśa-kalāḥ puṁsaḥ kṛṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam*, *Bhagavata* 1.3.28; CC, Adi 2), and Jagannatha is Krishna Himself. They can therefore refer what is said here about the avataras to Jagannatha as Krishna; the divergence is that the author places the murti above Krishna (6.8). "Tara-brahma" (6.44) is probably *tāraka-brahma*, "the Brahman that carries [one] across [samsara]"; the Upanishads give this name to Om and to the mantra of Rama. Shloka 6.44 is poorly read: *kṣayāṇi* is taken as Vedic *kṣaya*, "abode"; ପରତ୍ମା as *parātmā* (the metre wants two syllables). ସର୍ବବ୍ରହ୍ମ (6.51) is either "all the Brahmas" (the creators of the universes) or "all Brahman". 6.52 ("the highest among the purushas of the Veda") plays on the name Purushottama; the lone sign like ଷ before ଏହି is not identified (ସେ?).
[^06-17]: 6.53–6.64 concern Omkara and its measures (*mātrā*): 'a', 'u' (printed ଏକାର, 'e'; ଉକାର is expected), 'm' and apparently the bindu (6.62). From Omkara are born the Vedas, the Puranas, the mantras and all else (6.54–6.56). Here Divakara is close to the CC: Mahaprabhu tells Prakashananda that the pranava is the maha-vakya and the form of the Lord, and from the pranava come all the Vedas and the world (CC, Adi 7). 6.57 reproaches those who, not understanding Omkara, "practise brahma-jnana". Shlokas 6.53 and 6.62 are understood only in part. The syllable 'a' is called the "middle measure" (usually it is the first). The second line of 6.53 is unclear (*brahma-vidyā na saṁsthitā*?). ବଲ୍ଲଂ (6.62) is taken as *binduṁ* from sense and metre. ଚନ୍ଦ୍ରଚନ୍ଦ୍ରମସ୍ୟଂ may refer to the crescent (*ardha-candra*) above the bindu in the written Om; this is our conjecture.
[^06-18]: 6.62–6.64: the measure 'm' is linked with Shesha: "on the head of Shesha-deva is the couch". Shesha here is apparently Balabhadra, as in 4.29–4.30 and 4.67 (see the notes there); next comes Subhadra (6.65–6.67), then Radha and Krishna-Jagannatha (6.68–6.74). The measures of Om thus seem to be matched with the Deities of the altar; this is our conjecture. For the Gaudiya Vaishnavas Shesha serves the Lord as His bed, seat, umbrella and so on (CC, Adi 5), and Baladeva is the source of Shesha. ବର୍ତ୍ତିରେ (6.64): printed ବ + a sign like ର୍ତ୍ତି + େର; taken as "abides".
[^06-19]: Subhadra on the "bed of prema" unites the kama-bija (*klīṁ*, the bija of Krishna; cf. the note to 4.29, where Subhadra is given the bija "hrim"). What "fivefold" (*pañcātmika*) means, the author does not explain. The CC/CB know no such interpretation of Subhadra. Shloka 6.65 and couplets 6.66–6.67 are poorly read. ତତ୍କ୍ରୋଡ଼େ: printed ତ + ତ୍କ + େକ୍ରା + a sign like ଢ; taken as *tat-kroḍe*, "in her lap". In 6.61, 6.66 and 6.67 the same unidentified sign occurs (like ଟ with a ring below): ଚରାଚରବାଟ, ଶଯ୍ୟାଟ, ପ୍ରବଟ (perhaps ପ୍ରକଟ, "manifest").
[^06-20]: 6.69–6.74: Radha gives rasa and Sri Krishna relishes it; Jagannatha with Radha in the embrace of prema are "the two in this daru-murti", the wooden murti of Jagannatha. Cf. 4.55–4.58 (Radha comes from the body of Jagannatha). For the Gaudiya Vaishnavas Radha and Krishna united in one form in Sri Chaitanya: "Radha and Krishna are one soul in two bodies; now They have become one again, in Chaitanya" (Svarupa Damodara's verse, CC, Adi 1.5; cf. Adi 4). For them Jagannatha is Krishna, and Radha's mood in Puri is shown by Mahaprabhu (CC, Madhya 13). Shloka 6.68 is poorly understood: ରମିତ୍ୱା is read from the sense (the second printed sign is like ହି); ଧାରସେ is not identified (perhaps [ରାଧା]ରସେ, "in the rasa of Radha").
[^06-21]: 6.75–6.76 complete the interpretation of the 'bliss of prema' and the 'great bliss' (shlokas 6.14, 6.36; cf. 6.15–6.16). 'Both' are presumably Radha and Jagannatha in the daru-murti (6.73–6.74). The two blisses are one, and therefore the murti is the form of sat-cit-ananda (cf. shloka 6.6). Gaudiya Vaishnavas also teach that Radha and Krishna are one essence in two bodies (CC, Adi 4). But for them the murti of Jagannatha is Krishna Himself (CC, Madhya 13), not a union of Radha and Jagannatha in one daru-murti.
[^06-22]: 6.77–6.80: the path of raga leads to the murti of Jagannatha. On vaidhi and raganuga bhakti see the note to 3.153. According to the CC (Madhya 22) and the *Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu* (1.2), raganuga bhakti follows the eternal residents of Vraja; its object is the Krishna of Vrindavana. And Mahaprabhu did see in Jagannatha the Krishna of Vrindavana (CC, Madhya 13). 'Cast the service of others away' is a call to exclusive (ananya) bhakti; cf. *Bhagavad-gita* 18.66. Uncertain readings. ବୋଲି (6.77): printed ବୋ + ଲ with a tail below; taken as 'calling'. ଯେଝି (6.79): printed ଯେ + a sign ≈ ଝି; taken as 'whatever' (cf. ଯେଝା, 'whichever'). ଅନ୍ୟଙ୍କ (6.80): printed ଅନ୍ୟ + a dark sign ≈ ଙ୍କ/କ.
[^06-23]: 6.81–6.84. The nine kinds of bhakti are hearing, chanting and the rest (*Bhagavata*, 7.5.23–24). The four kinds of mukti are presumably salokya, samipya, sarupya and sayujya. The *Bhagavata* (3.29.13) names five kinds and says that a pure devotee does not accept them even when they are offered. Krishna lists the eighteen siddhis to Uddhava (*Bhagavata*, 11.15). The author sets the path of vidhi (it gives siddhis) against the path of raga (it leads to prema in Jagannatha). Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following the CC (Madhya 19), call those who desire bhukti, mukti and siddhi 'restless': pure bhakti is free from these desires (*Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu*, 1.1.11). 'Eighty-one kinds of bhakti' (6.83): ଏକାଶି = 81 = 9 × 9; the source of this count has not been found. Printed ଏକାଣି: ଣି looks like ଶି. 6.84 is understood uncertainly. *Duimata* means 'a twofold opinion, disagreement'; *racita* means 'arranged, composed'.
[^06-24]: 6.85–6.87: all doctrines lead to Jagannatha. His darshan gives bhakti, mukti and all siddhis. To those with desires He fulfils their desires, and to the desireless He gives all perfection; cf. *Bhagavata* 2.3.10 (*akāmaḥ sarva-kāmo vā mokṣa-kāma udāra-dhīḥ…*). Gaudiya Vaishnavas see darshan of the Deity as one of the limbs of bhakti (*Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu*, 1.2; CC, Madhya 22). They hold Krishna to be the final goal of all the Vedas (*Bhagavad-gita* 15.15), but they do not make all paths equal or set mukti and siddhis beside bhakti (CC, Madhya 19). Uncertain readings. ଗୁଡ଼ (6.85): taken as *sabu-guṛā*, 'all of them'; perhaps ଗୂଢ଼, 'the secret'. ମାନେ (6.86): the plural marker ('at the darshans'), or 'that is', or a misprint for ମାତ୍ରେ ('merely at the darshan'). ମତି (6.86): 'wish, intent' (?). ହିଦ୍ଧି (6.87): the same sign as in ପରହିଦ୍ଧି (6.9; see the note to 6.10); taken as ସିଦ୍ଧି.
[^06-25]: 6.88–6.94. Mahaprabhu's speech ends (6.88). Ati Bada Gosain (Jagannatha Dasa) hears it as Prabhu's command, writes it down and compiles a book. He reads this book in the jagamohana, the hall in front of the sanctum (see the note to 6.24). Thus Divakara makes Jagannatha Dasa the one who, by Mahaprabhu's command, recorded His teaching on Jagannatha; cf. 5.105–5.107 and the name 'Chaitanya-charitamrita' in 7.1. This is not in the CC or CB, and Jagannatha Dasa is not mentioned there. According to the CC (Adi 13.15–16), Mahaprabhu's pastimes were recorded by Murari Gupta (the early ones) and Svarupa Damodara (the later ones). Mahaprabhu's teaching was set out by Rupa and Sanatana Gosvamis (CC, Madhya 19–24). Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati includes the 'ativadi' in the list of apasampradayas (see the note to 1.1). Jagannatha Dasa is the author of the Odia *Bhagavata* (see the note to 1.92). Uncertain readings. ଶୋଳକ (6.91) is taken as a colloquial form of the word 'shloka'; perhaps ଷୋଳ, 'sixteen' (cf. the sixteen disciples of Ati Bada, 1.21–1.28). A chaupadi (*caupadī*) is an Odia song in four-line stanzas. 6.92 is unclear. ଅବର may be = ଆଉର, 'moreover'. ଫଳ is printed clearly ('fruit'), but the sense is unclear: 'the fruit [of the teaching]'? 'phala-shruti'?
[^06-26]: 'Fivefold' (*pañcātmika*) is the same word as in shloka 6.65 and in 6.67, where it is said of Subhadra and the kama-bija (see the note to 6.65). Here the whole of Mahaprabhu's speech on prema is so called. Printed ପ + a dark sign ≈ ଞ୍ଚ/ଶ୍ଚ + ା + ତ୍ମ + ି + କ.
[^06-27]: 'Svakiya' (*svakīyā*, 'one's own'). In Gaudiya rasa-shastra this is the love of a lawful wife, as distinct from parakiya, the love of the gopis outside marriage. According to Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching, the highest prema is the parakiya-bhava of the gopis of Vraja (CC, Adi 4; Rupa Gosvami's *Ujjvala-nilamani*). Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati upheld this teaching and rejected its worldly imitation by the sahajiyas. If Divakara means 'svakiya' in this sense, he departs from the Gaudiyas. But the word may also simply mean 'one's own', that is, 'the bhakti of our [line]' and 'the Vaishnavas of our [line]' (6.100), the followers of Ati Bada. We translate it as a term without choosing between these readings.
[^06-28]: The colophon is set in two lines, as at the end of Chapters 1–5 (see the note to the colophon of Ch. 5). Here it has neither 'Sri' nor the name 'Jagannatha': only 'Ati Bada Gosain'. Chapter 6 too is called 'Anukramani': printed ଅନୁକର୍ମଣି, taken as ଅନୁକ୍ରମଣି. Below the colophon is an ornament, followed by the heading of Chapter 7.

---

# Divakara Dasa. *Jagannatha-charitamrita*, Chapter 7
## Mahaprabhu with His Associates in the Jagamohana; the Gundicha Yatra and Bahuda


*Chapter Seven*

**7.1.** The 'Chaitanya-charitamrita': spoken from the mouth of Sri Chaitanya.[^07-1]  
**7.2.** This book the brahmin Divakara tells for the sake (?) of the saints.  
**7.3.** One day Lord Chaitanya graced the Sri jagamohana [with His presence].  
**7.4.** Gauridasa Pandita [was there] together with Matta Balarama.[^07-2]  
**7.5.** The disciple Rupa (?), who is a Gosain, [and] Sri Sanatana Prabhu are here;  
**7.6.** Sri Raghunatha Bhatta is present; Sri Jiva (?) Svami is here;  
**7.7.** Sri Gopala Dasa is here; Sri Raghunatha Dasa is here.  
**7.8.** These six Gosains are here; [here too is] the Odia Kashi Mishra.[^07-3]  
**7.9.** In front is Sarvabhauma; Gopinatha Acharya is [also] here.  
**7.10.** Jagadananda Chakravarti; and Mukunda Datta is here.  
**7.11.** Sri Damodara Chakravarti; Sri Ramananda, who is [also] here.  
**7.12.** Sri Nityananda [and] Sri Advaita: both Prabhus are together [with Him].[^07-4]  
**7.13.** Mahaprabhu Sri Chaitanya [takes] darshan of Sri Jagannatha:  
**7.14.** four circumambulations, twelve full prostrations.[^07-5]  
**7.15.** The Vaishnavas from the land of Gauda came before (??) the Gundicha.  
**7.16.** Having completed the nine-day yatra, they served (?) in the Gundicha procession.  
**7.17.** Bahuda, [the return] of the Sri Gundicha yatra: Jagannatha is [again] in Nila-kandara.  
**7.18.** On the Ekadashi of the bright fortnight of Ashadha all the Vaishnavas gathered,  
**7.19.** and all prostrated themselves near (?) Sri Prabhu Chaitanya.[^07-6]  

## Mahaprabhu Explains Harinama: the Names "Hari", "Rama" and "Krishna"


**7.20.** Sri Ati Bada Jagannatha [Dasa] too made his prostration.  
**7.21.** Nityananda and all the others gathered before Prabhu.  
**7.22.** They asked (?) about Harinama; Prabhu declared thus.[^07-7]  
**7.23.** First of all, then, in a great (??) shloka Prabhu established the virtues of the Name.  
**7.24.** The first is "Hari", two syllables: such is the command of Sri Chaitanya.  
**7.25.** O saints, listen: chant the name of Hari in bliss!  
**7.26.** Insects, birds and all other forms, all the unmoving and moving [beings] —  
**7.27.** in all of these, filling [them], abides the word "Hari".  
**7.28.** Whoever is by nature (??) a devotee becomes a Vaishnava by remembrance (?).[^07-8]  
**7.29.** Mere remembrance destroys the sin of the devotees;  
**7.30.** it destroys the ocean of sorrow: [one] easily attains the feet of Vishnu.  

*Shloka*  
**7.31.** *[Skt.]* "The essence of skill (??) in all (?), the embodied deity of beauty (?), who plays with Radha (?) in the rasa dance — He is called Rama (?)."[^07-9]  
**7.32.** *[Skt.]* "The yogis delight in the Infinite, in the Truth-bliss, in the Spirit; therefore by the word 'Rama' the Supreme Brahman is called."[^07-10]  
**7.33.** Inaccessible to Hara and Viranchi (?) — such is the name of Rama.  
**7.34.** It is very (?) lovely and tender, the best of all.  
**7.35.** It is the source of all bhavas, melting in the bhava of the bliss of prema.  
**7.36.** In all the Vedas and the Vedanta the name of Sri Rama is the essence of all.  
**7.37.** Since He [dances] the rasa with Radha, therefore Krishna is called Rama.  
**7.38.** Eternally in the hearts of the yogis: in meditation they eternally contemplate (?) Rama (?).  
**7.39.** He whom we call Rama is the form of sat-chit-ananda.  
**7.40.** Therefore this is the formless Brahman; the name of Sri Rama is the root support.[^07-11]  

*Shloka*  
**7.41.** *[Skt.]* "He whose sole happiness is bliss, possessed of Sri, dark, lotus-eyed, Gokulananda-nandana — He is called Krishna."[^07-12]  
**7.42.** Sri Lord Chaitanya gave the explanation of the word "Krishna".  
**7.43.** The supreme bliss and happiness of all living beings is the name of Krishna.  
**7.44.** In bliss He is Bliss; in the form of Brahman He is chid-ananda.  
**7.45.** From the body of Radha Lakshmi is born; therefore He is called Shriman (?).[^07-13]  
**7.46.** He is with Radha (?): the Couple abide together.  
**7.47.** His body is dark and beautiful, like a blue sapphire.  
**7.48.** His eyes are more beautiful than the lotus (?); the Name is the essence that delivers the earth.  
**7.49.** Dark as durva grass, He in the form of Brahman is the primeval root.  
**7.50.** All the hosts of rishis in the universe perform the sankirtana of [the name of] Krishna.  
**7.51.** The word "go" — … (??); the word "kula" — [refers] to the gods.[^07-14]  
**7.52.** The bliss of the devotees' minds — by this word [He is] "Gokulananda".  
**7.53.** Since this is born of bliss, therefore the name is "Ananda-ananda".  
**7.54.** So it is written (?) in the *Brahma-vaivarta Purana*.[^07-15]  
**7.55.** To all the devotees there are, [He is] bliss for the eyes (?).  
**7.56.** The word "Nandanandana" — when we take (?) it and write it in the book —[^07-16]  
**7.57.** [means]: He who appeared (lit. 'was born') to Nanda in the Dvapara-yuga; from this the name will become famous.  
**7.58.** Then this word in the Vedas and the Vedanta is uttered by the rishis, Mahesha and Brahma;  
**7.59.** in Satya and the other yugas, for millions upon millions [of years], they contemplate that word (?): "Krishna".  
**7.60.** Since He appears (lit. "is born") from bliss, He is called "Ananda-nandana".  
**7.61.** As many words as speech utters, as many more as are in the *Nirukta* (??),[^07-17]  
**7.62.** as many as you hear in the Vedas and the Vedanta — know all this to be the name of Krishna.  
**7.63.** As many forms as the eye sees — know this to be Harinama.  

## The Name "Hare Rama Krishna"; Prataparudra; Mahaprabhu Enters Jagannatha


**7.64.** From the Vedas to jest (?), all that is heard in this world,  
**7.65.** true or untrue (?) speech: know that this is Harinama.[^07-18]  

*Shloka*

**7.66.** *[Skt.]* Let people with joy in their hearts remember the One worthy of praise (?), the cause of the destruction of ignorance and its effects, the beloved of His own Radha (?), whose form is a dark youth; let the great remember the One worthy of praise with greatness (?).[^07-19]  
**7.67.** And Prabhu Chaitanya further spoke, [when] they asked (?) about the name (??):  
**7.68.** "Whoever is joyful (??), great [and] saintly (?), remember the Name in this way (lit. 'perform the bhava of the Name').[^07-20]  
**7.69.** Remember 'Hare Rama Krishna': [it] will destroy the season of ignorance.  
**7.70.** Avidya, births [and] karmas are destroyed by remembrance.  
**7.71.** In bliss, together with Radha, He eternally performs the rahasa (?):  
**7.72.** Sri Krishna enjoys with Radha; therefore He is called by the name 'Rama'.[^07-21]  
**7.73.** The name of Sri Krishna is the form of sat-cit-ananda:  
**7.74.** a youthful, dark form; in rasa, an ocean (?) of beauty [and] love.  
**7.75.** The rasika devotees (?), in the fullness of rasa, will float in this ocean of rasa.  
**7.76.** This should be shown to rasika disciples; it should not be told to the disciples of others.[^07-22]  
**7.77.** The Vaishnava who has conquered his senses and taken refuge in the mind-delighting (?) Name,  
**7.78.** such ones know this rasa; others are not capable of it (?)."  
**7.79.** Having said this, Gaurahari departed to [His] residence.  
**7.80.** Thus Lord Chaitanya spoke in the Sri jagamohana.  
**7.81.** This account of the five shlokas came forth from the mouth of Sri Chaitanya.[^07-23]  
**7.82.** Thus the glory of Chaitanya, the limit of [all] virtues, became manifest:  
**7.83.** like the rays of the sun, it lit up the earth.  
**7.84.** Prataparudra, the best of kings, the lord of Puri, [possessed of] a perfected body (siddha-anga),  
**7.85.** was born in the world of mortals for the sake of accomplishing bhakti.[^07-24]  
**7.86.** He asked many Vaishnavas, but did not thereby gain an understanding of bhakti.  
**7.87.** From [the time of] the darshan (?), giving [Him] his mind, he served Lord Chaitanya.  
**7.88.** He said: "Instruct me in Hari-bhakti"; [so] the king [asked] from his heart (?).  
**7.89.** Chaitanya Gosain said: "In the rasa of a vishayi (?) there is no bhakti."[^07-25]  
**7.90.** The best of kings reflected: "Right here I will attain bhakti."  
**7.91.** He served in many ways, [but] there was no permission from Mahaprabhu.  
**7.92.** One day, in disguise, the king went into the jagamohana;  
**7.93.** taking a broom in his hand, he swept, hidden (?) in [another's] garb.[^07-26]  
**7.94.** At the morning (?) interval [between the services] Mahaprabhu arrived.  
**7.95.** Bowing down full length to Jagannatha, He joined both hands  
**7.96.** and, saying "Hare Rama Krishna", sang kirtan loudly.  
**7.97.** The king, who was nearby, said "Hare Rama Krishna."  
**7.98.** Hearing this, Sri Chaitanya was displeased: "A vishayi is touching the Name!  
**7.99.** This is not proper: by this bhakti grows weak."  
**7.100.** Hearing this, the king hurried and fell at Prabhu's feet:  
**7.101.** "O Lord! I heard [the Name] from Your holy mouth and took refuge (?) in the nectar of the Name.  
**7.102.** What bhava is in this Name, that it leads (?) into the body of Jagannatha?  
**7.103.** … (??) joining with me, deign (?) to give [me] the command with a loving heart."[^07-27]  
**7.104.** Hearing this, Chaitanya was pleased [and] looked (?) at Jagannatha Dasa:  
**7.105.** "O Ati Bada Jagannatha! Tell the king the truth of prema."[^07-28]  
**7.106.** Having said this, Sri Chaitanya entered (lit. "merged into") the body of Sri Jagannatha:  
**7.107.** whence He had come before, there, returning, He entered.[^07-29]  
**7.108.** To establish bhakti in non-devotees, He showed bhakti to the devotees;  
**7.109.** leaving the virtue (?) of bhakti in the world, He departed to His own abode.[^07-30]
