## End of Chapter 7: Jagannatha Dasa's Bhakti; Divakara's Signature


**7.110.** In such (?) a bhava Jagannatha Dasa joyfully served that bhakti.  
**7.111.** At that time [people] did not know bhakti; they said, "I am Brahman."[^07-31]  
**7.112.** They did not know the name of Sri Hari; they lived in the mood of rajas and tamas.  
**7.113.** [Then] Krishna appeared (lit. "was born") in the form of Chaitanya: giving up everything, worship (?) [Him] with bhakti.[^07-32]  
**7.114.** Those feet of Krishna-Chaitanya are the refuge of the brahmana Divakara.  

*Thus [ends] in the Sri Jagannathamrita the seventh chapter, [the life] of Sri Ati Bada Jagannatha Dasa Gosain, called "Anukramani" (?).*[^07-33]

[^07-1]: 'Chaitanya-charitamrita' ('the nectar of Chaitanya's deeds') is what Divakara calls his narrative (or this part of it) here: 'spoken from the mouth of Sri Chaitanya'. Cf. 6.88–6.96, where Ati Bada records Mahaprabhu's teaching. This is not Krishnadasa Kaviraja's *Sri Chaitanya-charitamrita* (CC), to which we refer. In the colophons the poem is called *Sri Jagannathamrita*. ଅର୍ଥର (7.2) is taken as 'for the sake of' (gen. of *artha*, 'purpose, benefit'); perhaps 'the meaning [of the book]'.
[^07-2]: Gauridasa Pandita and Matta Balarama (Balarama Dasa) are links in Divakara's guru-pranali: Gauridasa, Hridayananda, Balarama Dasa, Jagannatha Dasa (see the notes to 1.42 and 2.139). According to the CC (Adi 11), Gauridasa Pandita is an associate of Nityananda from Ambika-Kalna. Balarama Dasa is not mentioned in the CC or CB.
[^07-3]: 7.5–7.8: the six Gosains are Rupa, Sanatana, Raghunatha Bhatta, Jiva, Gopala Dasa (presumably Gopala Bhatta) and Raghunatha Dasa. Here the list agrees with the Gaudiya one. In 3.170–3.172 Nityananda is named instead of Raghunatha Bhatta (see the note to 3.170). But according to the CC, the six were never all together with Mahaprabhu in Puri. Rupa and Sanatana came to Puri (Antya 1; Antya 4), Raghunatha Bhatta twice (Antya 13). Raghunatha Dasa lived in Puri for many years (Antya 6). Mahaprabhu met Gopala Bhatta in the south, according to Gaudiya tradition. Jiva Gosvami, by the same tradition, never saw Mahaprabhu (see the note to 3.170). ରୂପକ, ଜୀବକ are names ending in -ka. This may be a suffix on the name or a genitive ('Rupa's disciple'?); but the six Gosains add up only with Rupa. Kashi Mishra is called 'the Odia' (*oṛiā*); Mahaprabhu lived in his house in Puri (see the note to 2.7).
[^07-4]: Gopinatha Acharya is Sarvabhauma's brother-in-law (CC, Madhya 6). On Jagadananda Chakravarti see the note to 3.83. Mukunda Datta is a singer, an associate of Mahaprabhu since the Navadvipa days (CC, Adi 10). 'Damodara Chakravarti' has not been identified. He may be Svarupa Damodara or Damodara Pandita (CC, Adi 10); the title 'Chakravarti', as with Jagadananda, does not occur in the CC. Nityananda and Advaita, according to the CC, came to Puri with the Gaudiyas for the Ratha-yatra (Madhya 11; Madhya 16).
[^07-5]: Four circumambulations and twelve prostrations are the order of darshan in Divakara; we do not know his source. According to the CC (Antya 14), Mahaprabhu usually beheld Jagannatha from the Garuda-stambha in the natamandira, in front of the jagamohana.
[^07-6]: 7.15–7.19 follow the yearly cycle of the Ratha-yatra. The Gaudiyas came to Puri before the Ratha-yatra and stayed for the four months of chaturmasya (CC, Madhya 11; Madhya 15; cf. the notes to 3.167 and 3.175). The 'nine-day yatra' (*nabadina yātrā*) lasts from the Ratha-yatra (the second lunar day of the bright fortnight of Ashadha) to Bahuda, the return procession on the tenth day. Nila-kandara is Puri (see the note to 3.137). The Ekadashi after Bahuda is Hari-shayana Ekadashi, the beginning of chaturmasya; on this day in Puri the Deities on the chariots are adorned with gold (Suna Besha). Uncertain readings. ସମୂର୍ବେ (7.15, ??): printed ସ + ମୂ + ର୍ବେ, an unidentified word; by sense 'before' (ସପୂର୍ବେ? ପୂର୍ବେ?), since the Gaudiyas came before the Ratha-yatra. ଗୁଣ୍ଡିଚା is printed ≈ ଗୁଣ୍ଡିଚ୍ଚ. ଖଟିଲେ (7.16, ?) is taken as 'served, laboured' (*khaṭibā*). Who the subject of 7.16 is, the Gaudiyas or Jagannatha, is unclear. କୂଳେ (7.19, ?) is taken as 'near' (lit. 'on the bank'). The scene continues on p. 44.
[^07-7]: 7.22–7.63 explain the mahamantra word by word: "Hari", "Rama", "Krishna". The order is the Odia one, "Hare Rama … Hare Krishna" (see the notes to 2.135 and 3.147). Divakara takes the first word as the name "Hari", "two syllables" (7.24): Hari fills all forms, moving and unmoving (7.26–7.27). For the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, "Hare" in the mahamantra is the vocative of "Hara" (Harā), a name of Sri Radha; so in the Gaudiya explanations of the mahamantra attributed to Jiva Gosvami and Gopala Guru Gosvami. Mahaprabhu Himself gave the mahamantra to the residents of Navadvipa (CC, Adi 17). On the power of remembrance and of the shadow of the Name (7.28–7.30) see the note to 5.85. Unclear readings. ପଚାରିଲେ (7.22, ?): printed ପ + a sign like ସ୍ୱ/ଚ with a hook + ର + େଲ; possibly ପସାରିଲେ, 'spread'. ମହାଶ୍ଲୋକେ (7.23, ??): between ମ and ଶ୍ଲୋକେ there is a solid blot; possibly ମନ୍ତ୍ରଶ୍ଲୋକେ. Which shloka 7.23 means is unclear; the shlokas come below (7.31, 7.32, 7.41).
[^07-8]: ସ୍ୱରୂପଟି (7.28, ??): after ସ୍ୱରୂପ stands a sign like ଟ with a ring below and a loop above. It seems to be the same unidentified sign as in 6.61, 6.66 and 6.67 (see the note to 6.65). It closely resembles ଟି in the word କୋଟି (7.59): there ି is a hook over ଟ; here it seems fused into a loop. If so, Chapter 6 may read ଶଯ୍ୟାଟି, ପ୍ରବଟି etc., with the Odia particle ‑ଟି. This is our conjecture. Here understood as 'by nature' (cf. *svarūpataḥ*); possibly 'he who is the very form of a devotee'. ସ୍ମରଣେ (?): the first sign is like ଦୂ/ହୂ with a bold blot below; cf. ସ୍ମରଣ in 7.29.
[^07-9]: Shloka 7.31 is a variant of a verse from Gaudiya explanations of the mahamantra: *vaidagdhya-sāra-sarvasvaṁ mūrta-līlādhidaivatam / rādhikāṁ ramayan nityaṁ rāma ity abhidhīyate* ("The whole wealth and essence of skill, the embodied presiding deity of the lilas, He ever delights Radhika — therefore He is called Rama"). Its source is unknown to us. The print has other words (*sarveṣu*, *lāvaṇya-devatā*, *rādhayā rāsa-keliko*); we translate what is printed. The conclusion in 7.37: Rama is Krishna Himself, who dances the rasa with Radha. This agrees with the Gaudiya reading of "Rama" in the mahamantra as Radha-ramana. Unclear readings: ବଧେଗ୍ଧ (printed ≈ ବ + ଧ + େ + a sign like ଭ/ଷ with a tail) is taken as *vaidagdhya*; ସ·ର as *sāra*; ଲାବଣ୍ୟ (?); ରାଧୟା (?), printed ≈ ସ୍ୱ + ଧ + ୟା. The last word is printed ସ୍ୱସ (= ରାସ), like the word before it; by sense and by 7.37 it is ରାମ.
[^07-10]: Shloka 7.32 is from the *Padma Purana*; Mahaprabhu quotes it in CC (Madhya 9.29), followed by the explanation of the name "Krishna" (*kṛṣir bhū-vācakaḥ…*, Madhya 9.30). There Mahaprabhu also says that one name of Krishna equals three names of Rama (Madhya 9.32–9.33). In Divakara the name of Rama is "the essence of all the Vedas" (7.36), Brahman and "the root support" (7.40). 7.33: "inaccessible to Hara (Shiva) and Viranchi (Brahma)" — printed ≈ ବ/ର + ର + ଞ୍ଚି + ଙ୍କ, OCR ବରଞ୍ଚ…; ଅଗମ is printed with a sign like ଃ at the end (ଅଗମ୍ୟ?). 7.34 ଅତି (?): ତି is blotted. 7.38: ଭାବନ୍ତି (?) — after ଭାବ there is a small faint sign; ରାମ (?) is printed ≈ ଘମ.
[^07-11]: 7.39–7.40: Rama is the form of sat-chit-ananda; "the formless Brahman", whose "root support" is the name of Sri Rama. The syntax of 7.40 is unclear; possibly: "therefore this Brahman is formless, [and] the name of Rama is the root support". For the Gaudiya Vaishnavas the impersonal Brahman is the effulgence of Krishna's body (*Brahma-samhita* 5.40; CC, Adi 2), and the Name is non-different from the Lord Himself and has form.
[^07-12]: The source of shloka 7.41 is unknown to us. Divakara explains it word by word: "bliss" (7.43–7.44); "Shriman" (7.45–7.46); "dark" and "lotus-eyed" (7.47–7.49); "Gokulananda" (7.51–7.52); "Ananda-ananda" (7.53); "Nandanandana" (7.56–7.57); "Ananda-nandana" (7.60). The compound *gokulānanda-nandanaḥ* ("the joy of Gokula, the son [of Nanda]"? "the son of the bliss of Gokula"?) is left untranslated: Divakara derives several names from it. The usual Gaudiya explanation of the name "Krishna" is different: *kṛṣi*, "being", and *ṇa*, "bliss" (CC, Madhya 9.30). 7.44 is understood tentatively.
[^07-13]: 7.45: Lakshmi is born from Radha's body, therefore Krishna is "Shriman", "possessor of Sri". This is close to Gaudiya teaching: the Lakshmis are expansions of Sri Radha (CC, Adi 4). ଶ୍ରୀମତ (?): printed ଶ୍ରୀ + a sign like ମ/ଦ with a hook + ତ; taken as ଶ୍ରୀମନ୍ତ. 7.46 ସହିତ (?): printed ≈ ସଖୀତ; if read ସଖୀ, 'friend', the sense is perhaps: "She (Lakshmi), having become Radha's friend…". 7.48 ପଦ୍ମଠୁଁ (?): printed ପ + ଦ with a blot (୍ମ) + a sign like ଠୁ/ଷୁ + ଁ; taken as a comparison, "[more beautiful] than the lotus".
[^07-14]: 7.51–7.53 explain "Gokulananda". The word after "go" is unidentified: printed ମ + a sign like ସ୍ତା + a bold sign like ଙ୍କ/କ୍ଷ + ର; possibly ମନ୍ତ୍ରାକ୍ଷର, "mantra syllable". In Sanskrit *go* means "cow", also "the senses", "speech, the Veda"; which sense Divakara intends is unclear. "Ananda-ananda" (7.53) may be a misprint for "Ananda-nandana" (cf. 7.60).
[^07-15]: The *Brahma-vaivarta Purana* (Sri Krishna-janma-khanda) contains syllable-by-syllable explanations of the name "Krishna". We have not checked shloka 7.41 against it. ଲେଖିଛି (?): printed ଲେଖି + ଛ with a subscript sign; possibly ଲେଖିଛନ୍ତି.
[^07-16]: 7.56–7.57 are understood tentatively: apparently the name "Nandanandana" ("son of Nanda") became famous when Krishna appeared to Nanda in the Dvapara-yuga. Yet in 7.58–7.59 Divakara says that the rishis, Shiva and Brahma contemplate the name of Krishna already in the Satya-yuga. For the Gaudiya Vaishnavas the Name is eternal and non-different from Krishna (*nāma cintāmaṇiḥ kṛṣṇaḥ*, *Padma Purana*; CC, Madhya 17.133). ନେତ୍ରକୁ (7.55, ?): printed େନ + a triangular sign ≈ ତ୍ର + କୁ. ଆଣି (7.56, ?): printed ଅ + a bold sign ≈ ଣି/ଶି; rhymes with ବାଣୀ. ବଚନ (7.59, ?): the first sign is like ଢ/ଵ.
[^07-17]: 7.61–7.63: all words and all visible forms are the name of Krishna, Harinama. Cf. 7.27: the word "Hari" fills everything. For the Gaudiya Vaishnavas everything that exists is Krishna's energy, but the Name is not just any sound: it is transcendental (*aprakrita*) and non-different from Krishna (CC, Madhya 17.131–133). ନିରୁକ୍ତେ (7.61, ??): printed ନ + a sign like ରୁ/ବୃ + ତ୍ତ୍ରେ, OCR ଲଦ୍ଧନ୍ତେ; taken as "in the *Nirukta*" (Yaska's etymological work) or "in etymologies".
[^07-18]: 7.64–7.65 complete the thought of 7.62–7.63: all that the eye sees and the ear hears is Harinama. Gaudiya Vaishnavas teach otherwise: the name of Krishna is non-different from Krishna Himself (CC, Madhya 17.133). But not every sound in the world is Harinama. There is a distinction between the pure name, namabhasa and nama-aparadha (CC, Antya 3). Unclear readings. ହାସ୍ୟ (7.64, ?): the first letter resembles both ହ and ଦ; ଦାସ୍ୟ ('service') does not fit the sense. ଅସଦ୍ (7.65, ?): a stroke is printed over ଦ.
[^07-19]: The shloka is set in four lines (dandas after the 2nd and 4th). The metre is upajati (the eleven-syllable indravajra and upendravajra). The metre helps the reading. The second line is indravajra, so its 3rd syllable is short: *sva-rādhā-ramaṇaṁ* (printed ≈ ସ୍ୱସ୍ୱଧାରମଣଂ; ରା resembles ସ୍ୱ). The word *īḍyaṁ* (printed ≈ ଈ + ତ/ଡ୍ୟ + ଂ, twice) gives the required two long syllables. Corrections: ହେତୁଃ → *hetum* (acc., agreeing with *īḍyaṁ*); ପୁଂମାସଃ → *pumāṁsaḥ*. ମହତା is perhaps = *mahatāṁ* ('by the great'). "The cause of the destruction of ignorance" is probably an explanation of the name "Hari" (He who "takes away", *harati*, ignorance; cf. 7.24 and the note to 7.22). Cf. the explanations of "Rama" (7.31–7.32) and "Krishna" (7.41). We have not identified the source of the shloka.
[^07-20]: 7.67–7.68 are poorly read. ଜିନାମ (??): printed ଜି + ନାମ ('name'); perhaps a corruption of ନିଜ ନାମ, 'one's own name'. ପୁଛିଲ (?) is taken as 'asked'. ଯେହୁଷ୍ଟ (??): printed ଯେ + ହୁ/ଦୁ + ଷ୍ଟ. Variants: ଯେ ହୃଷ୍ଟ, 'who are joyful', or ଯେ ଦୁଷ୍ଟ, 'whoever [is] wicked'. The metre (9 syllables) allows both. ସାଧବ is taken as 'sadhus' (?). 'Will accomplish' (fut.) is also possible; the usual meaning of the Odia word is 'merchant'.
[^07-21]: "Rama" is "He who enjoys (*ramate*) with Radha". Cf. 7.37 and the note to 7.31: this agrees with the Gaudiya explanation of "Rama" in the mahamantra as Radha-ramana. But the order "Hare Rama Krishna" (7.69, 7.96) is an Odia custom; Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, begin the mahamantra with "Hare Krishna" (see the notes to 3.147 and 7.22). *Rāhāsa* (?) is taken as *rahasa*, 'the secret pastimes' (printed ≈ ସ୍ୱହାସ). Perhaps it is ରାସ, 'the rasa-lila', as in 7.37.
[^07-22]: An instruction to keep the teaching secret. It is shown only to "rasika disciples", not to "the disciples of others". This is how the Atibadi line speaks, setting itself apart from other Vaishnavas (cf. 6.100: the book is "the wealth of the svakiya Vaishnavas"). Gaudiya Vaishnavas also enjoin hearing of the pastimes of Radha and Krishna from a pure devotee and not from the unqualified (cf. CC, Madhya 8). But for them Mahaprabhu's teaching is open to all: He ordered the Name to be preached in every town and village (CC, Adi 9; CB, Antya 4). Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati counts the "ativadi" among the deviant communities (apasampradayas). *Pāraku nuhanti* (7.78, ?) means 'are not capable' (from ପାରକ).
[^07-23]: "The account of the five shlokas": so Divakara calls Mahaprabhu's teaching in the jagamohana (7.20–7.80). There are four shlokas set in the chapter: 7.31, 7.32, 7.41 and 7.66. How Divakara counts five is unclear to us; perhaps he also counts the shloka mentioned in 7.23 (see the note to 7.22). Cf. the title "Chaitanya-charitamrita, spoken from the mouth of Sri Chaitanya" (7.1).
[^07-24]: Prataparudra was the Gajapati of Orissa, a contemporary of Mahaprabhu. Divakara calls him "the lord of Puri" and "[possessed of] a perfected body", come into the world for the sake of bhakti. According to the CC, Prataparudra was a great devotee who received Mahaprabhu's mercy (Madhya 11–14). What follows (7.86–7.105) is an Odia version of the CC account, with divergences (see below). Cf. VL 2.121, 5.7, 9.119, 9.149. *Darśana hantu* (7.87, ?) is taken as 'from [the time of] the darshan'. ହନ୍ତୁ is perhaps a Middle Odia postposition 'from'. ନୃପ ଚିତ୍ତେ (7.88, ?): printed ≈ ନୃପ + ଚ + ତ୍ତେ; cf. ଚିତ୍ତେ in 7.103.
[^07-25]: According to the CC (Madhya 11.3–11), Mahaprabhu would not agree to see the king: for a renunciant, meeting a *viṣayī* ('a man engaged in worldly affairs') is worse than drinking poison (Madhya 11.8). Here Mahaprabhu says the same. The reading ବିଷୟୀ (?) is supported by ବିଷୟୀରେ in 7.98; in 7.89 the last sign is printed unclearly (ୀ or ୁ). If ବିଷୟୁ, then 'in the rasa of sense objects there is no bhakti'.
[^07-26]: *Pahãrā* is a broom, sweeping. Cf. the chhera-panhara, a rite of the Gajapati: at the Ratha-yatra he sweeps the chariots with a golden broom (VL 2.121, 5.7; CC, Madhya 13.15–16). According to the CC, Mahaprabhu was pleased by this service of the king. Later the king, in the simple dress of a Vaishnava, came to Mahaprabhu in a garden near Gundicha, began to recite the "Gopi-gita" and was embraced (Madhya 14). In Divakara it is different: the king in disguise sweeps the jagamohana, Mahaprabhu comes there and sings "Hare Rama Krishna", and the king repeats after Him. ଲୁଚିତ (?) is taken as 'hidden' (from ଲୁଚିବା, 'to hide'). ପ୍ରଭାତ (7.94, ?): printed ପ୍ରଭ + ତ, ା unclear.
[^07-27]: Unclear readings in 7.101–7.104. ଅଶ୍ରେ (7.101, ?) is taken as ଆଶ୍ରେ, 'refuge'. ଭେଦାଇ (7.102, ?) is taken as 'leads into' (from ଭେଦିବା, 'to penetrate'). The king's question foreshadows 7.106. ଜିରାଜ (7.103, ??): printed ଜି + a sign resembling ସ୍ୱ/ରା + ଜ; the word is not identified (ଜିହ୍ୱା, 'tongue'?). ଦେବାକ (?) is perhaps ଦେବା with the particle -କ (cf. ରୂପକ, ଜୀବକ in 7.5–7.6). ଚାହିଁଲେ (7.104, ?): printed ≈ ଲୁ/ଚୂ + ହିଁ + ଲେ.
[^07-28]: Mahaprabhu charges Ati Bada Jagannatha (Jagannatha Dasa) with teaching the king "the truth of prema". Thus Divakara makes Jagannatha Dasa the successor of Mahaprabhu's teaching; cf. 6.88–6.94 and the note to 6.90. According to the CC, the king was brought to Mahaprabhu by Sarvabhauma and Ramananda Raya (Madhya 11–14), while Mahaprabhu Himself heard the teaching on prema from Ramananda and revealed it to Svarupa Damodara and to Rupa and Sanatana. Jagannatha Dasa is not mentioned in the CC or CB. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas (Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati; the "ativadi" are in the list of apasampradayas), see the note to 6.90. ଅତେବଡ → ଅତିବଡ଼, as in 6.99.
[^07-29]: Mahaprabhu's departure in Divakara: having spoken His last words, He "merged (*līna*) into the body of Sri Jagannatha" and returned whence He had come. This is an Odia tradition; cf. VL 9.38, 9.80 and 9.120 (in 9.120 these are the words Rama Raya proposes to announce to the people; Madhava himself tells of the departure differently, VL 9.42–9.128). The position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas (Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati): the Lord's body is sat-cit-ananda, and His departure is a disappearance (antardhana) by His own will (cf. BG 4.9; the notes to VL 9.42 and 9.90). The CC does not describe the departure: its narrative ends with the "Shikshashtaka" (Antya 20). According to Lochana Dasa's *Chaitanya-mangala*, Mahaprabhu entered the Deity of Jagannatha; according to another tradition, Tota-Gopinatha. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas Sri Chaitanya is Krishna Himself (CC, Adi 1), non-different from Jagannatha. But they do not accept Divakara's teaching on the tattva of Jagannatha and Chaitanya (cf. Ch. 6).
[^07-30]: The end of the chapter is 7.110–7.114 and the colophon (printed p. 47). ଗୁଣ (7.109, ?): printed ≈ ଗୁଣ/ବୁଣ.
[^07-31]: "I am Brahman" (*muhi̐ brahma*, cf. Skt. *ahaṁ brahmāsmi*) is for Divakara a mark of the age before Sri Chaitanya's appearance: the impersonalist (Mayavada) self-identification with Brahman. Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, likewise see Mahaprabhu's preaching as a refutation of Mayavada (cf. the dialogue with Sarvabhauma, CC, Madhya 6, and with Prakashananda, CC, Adi 7); here Divakara agrees with them.
[^07-32]: *Bhajata* is formally an imperative plural ("worship"), but may be indicative ("they worship"); then: "all, giving up [the former ways], worship with bhakti." *Kṛṣṇa caitanya rūpe jāta* is taken as "Krishna appeared in the form of Chaitanya"; for Gaudiya Vaishnavas Sri Chaitanya is Krishna Himself in the mood of Radha (CC, Adi 1.5; 4).
[^07-33]: The colophon is set in two lines, as at the end of chapters 1–6 (cf. the notes on the colophons of chs. 5 and 6). Printed ଅତବଡ଼ (‑i not printed; cf. ଅତେବଡ in 6.99, 7.105) is taken as ଅତିବଡ଼; ଅନୁକର୍ମଣି as ଅନୁକ୍ରମଣି ("Anukramani", as in the previous chapters). The last couplet (7.114) is the author's signature ("the brahmana Divakara"), as at the ends of earlier chapters.

---

# Divakara Dasa. *Jagannatha-charitamrita*, Chapter 23
## The Glory of Beholding the Four Deities: the Snana-yatra, the Chariot and Gundicha


*Chapter Twenty-Three*

**23.1.** The brahmana Divakara says: O good people, listen [to this account], full of rasa.  
**23.2.** That Sri Ati Bada Gosain again told his disciples this:[^23-1]  
**23.3.** "Chaitanya Mahaprabhu was [here]. He too cherished this joy.  
**23.4.** The supreme, infinite form that (?) abides in the shape of wood (daru) —  
**23.5.** in Sri Baladeva, in Subhadra, in Jagannatha, in Sudarshana.  
**23.6.** Whoever takes darshan of the fourfold form, blessed is his life.[^23-2]  
**23.7.** [Their] darshan purifies the fallen, and also [their] wives and sons.  
**23.8.** The Lotus-eyed One who is Tripura (?) is eternally called by the name Lalita.[^23-3]  
**23.9.** If you serve her, you will be able to abide (??) in eternal service.  

*(In the Vishnu Purana.)*[^23-4]

**23.10.** Brahma told the rishis about Prabhu's Snana-yatra:[^23-5]  
**23.11.** whoever, saying 'Jaya, Rama, Krishna!', offers praise with his mouth,  
**23.12.** he dwells in Brahmaloka and sees not a trace (?) of sorrow.  
**23.13.** Whoever joyfully beholds the bathing of the three Deities,  
**23.14.** he, taking twenty-one generations of his family [with him], dwells happily in the eternal abode.  
**23.15.** If by fate a person takes darshan of the fourfold form,  
**23.16.** that being, taking a hundred generations [of his forefathers], [attains] what is desired in the eternal.  
**23.17.** When Bhagavan, having finished His bath, goes forth in the form [turned] to the south (?) —[^23-6]  
**23.18.** whatever beings see this attain sayujya liberation.[^23-7]  
**23.19.** As much [fruit] as a man has who has circumambulated the seven earths three times,  
**23.20.** that much fruit he attains who sees the form [turned] to the south.  
**23.21.** The darshan of Badari-kshetra, the plunge (?) at Ganga-sagara,  
**23.22.** as much merit as [comes] from giving gifts at the time (?) of a lunar and a solar eclipse,  
**23.23.** the fruit of austerity, homa and japa — [all this] that being obtains at once.  
**23.24.** (??) The beings who behold the great (?) Gundicha there (?),[^23-8]  
**23.25.** who see [it] even once in a month (?), are delivered from the fever of sufferings,  
**23.26.** obtain the happiness of supreme bliss and (?) attain ninefold bhakti.[^23-9]  
**23.27.** The man who serves seven days at the house of Gundicha —  
**23.28.** those, serving and serving, attain ninefold bhakti.  
**23.29.** Brahma told the rishis the glory of the Gundicha-yatra.  
**23.30.** [Even] the Primal One (?) does not know its virtues — how then can they be told in a composition?[^23-10]  
**23.31.** The being who utters (??) the three syllables 'Gun-di-cha' —[^23-11]  
**23.32.** however many sins there are in his family line, they are destroyed by saying [it] but once.  
**23.33.** Whoever beholds [the Lord] mounted on the chariot is blessed among the Vaishnavas.  
**23.34.** Following the chariot even for a moment, one attains bhakti of eighty-one kinds (?).[^23-12]  
**23.35.** On whose body the dust of the chariot flies up and falls — as many [specks] as fall, if one counts them,  
**23.36.** for that many yugas he serves in the company of Krishna.  
**23.37.** Whoever is crushed (?) beneath (?) the chariot, he, in [one] breath out and in,[^23-13]  
**23.38.** attains the fruit of the ashvamedha; who can tell what he gains (?)?  
**23.39.** If his forefathers are in heaven (?), seeing him they are delivered from hell;[^23-14]  
**23.40.** pushing aside Yama's messengers, they go to the world of Vishnu.  

*(In the Skanda Purana.)*[^23-15]

**23.41.** Bhagavan spoke these words to Indradyumna:  
**23.42.** 'Twenty-four (?) crores of tirthas [and] Pushkara are [here], O Indradyumna.[^23-16]  
**23.43.** Whoever beholds My Gundicha there for nine days,  
**23.44.** that being at once attains [the fruit of bathing in] the waters of twenty-four (?) crores of tirthas.  
**23.45.** [Whoever] sees Me in the house of Gundicha casts off (??) all sorrows.[^23-17]  
**23.46.** As much fruit as comes in a day — (??) one attains tenfold.  

## The Glory of Purushottama-kshetra; Jagannatha as Radha and Krishna in One Bhava; the End of the Poem


**23.47.** Whoever goes [there] in joy — who can tell of his bhakti?'  

*(In the Padma Purana.)*

**23.48.** One whom they make a forced porter (?) (*bethia*) is seized and taken to the best of kshetras (?);[^23-18]  
**23.49.** in his mind there is great sorrow; bound (?), he goes [there].  
**23.50.** On entering the kshetra (?) he at once (?) obtains the merit of crores [of good deeds].  
**23.51.** Though there is no [such] knowledge in his mind, I give [the fruit] to him by force.  
**23.52.** A load (?) on the back of a bullock, a horse, an elephant, a camel or a donkey,  
**23.53.** birds put in a cage — whoever one day brings [them] to My (?) kshetra, —  
**23.54.** whatever is the destination of those who perform the agnihotra, that fruit they obtain.  

*(In the Nrisimha Purana.)*

**23.55.** One day Nrisimha told Prahlada the news of the kshetra:  
**23.56.** 'Those who go to My Purushottama obtain your supreme (?) bhakti.[^23-19]  
**23.57.** Telling is no proof: go and see for yourself with your own eyes.  
**23.58.** First they obtain the ninefold [bhakti] (?), [then] they obtain the sixty-four angas [of bhakti],  
**23.59.** in time they obtain the bhakti of eighty-one [kinds] — see it with your own eyes, O steady-minded one!'  

*(In the Brahma-vaivarta.)*

**23.60.** Whoever reviles the Vedas and Puranas, whoever in the world reviles the Vedas,[^23-20]  
**23.61.** [whoever] makes dharma (??) …, reviles brahmanas and Vaishnavas,  
**23.62.** reviles renunciants, chaste women and ascetics, saying, 'I am great', —  
**23.63.** if such people go to the best of kshetras, in half a danda (?) they destroy that sin.  

*(In the Sri Bhagavata.)*

**23.64.** Bhagavan said to Prahlada: 'Listen, best of devotees![^23-21]  
**23.65.** However many murtis of Mine there are in the universe [manifest] for the sake of lila, —  
**23.66.** [if] all beings worship them for as long as a hundred years,  
**23.67.** however much merit comes of that, — [that much one obtains] by going to the best of kshetras for one danda.'  
**23.68.** Listen attentively, good people: [we] have told the account of the kshetra.  
**23.69.** Say it thus: Radha and Krishna are one bhava (?).[^23-22]  
**23.70.** They see no duality (?) and no other; they speak no other (?) speech.  
**23.71.** Neither [their] gaze nor [their] mind is [turned] to another: both are absorbed in one bhava.  
**23.72.** They abide (?) in the bhava of madhurya; there is no desire for aishvarya.  
**23.73.** [But] whoever holds a desire in the heart has aishvarya accordingly (?).  
**23.74.** To kill Ravana, Sri Rama conceived a desire in His heart.  
**23.75.** The Son of Nanda conceived the desire to arrange the rasa with the cowherd women (?).  
**23.76.** Nrisimha tore apart Hiranyakashipu; Vamana deceived Bali.  
**23.77.** Kurma — at the churning of the nectar; the earth — Varaha in [His] form.  
**23.78.** These and other avataras — all [are] bodies (?) of desire.  
**23.79.** Jagannatha has no desire: He abides happily in the bhava of Radha.  
**23.80.** Therefore [His lot] is the tasting of madhurya, the sadhana of rasa [lasting] crores of yugas.  
**23.81.** Perhaps you will say thus: 'Then from where was this [vast] world born?  
**23.82.** Who here kills the asuras? Who at the time of pralaya merges (?) [all into Himself]?  
**23.83.** Who hears the sorrow of the devotees and, taking the discus (?) in hand, hastens [to help]?  
**23.84.** Who makes the endless world, [if] This One (?) is ever absorbed [in bhava]?'  
**23.85.** To break (??) the doubt about this, we shall tell — listen, O sadhus.[^23-23]  
**23.86.** The Tortoise, Varaha and the Fish, Nrisimha, Hayagriva, know,  
**23.87.** Kapila, Vyasa and others, which (?) [are] the avataras of the Lord, —  
**23.88.** they lighten (?) the burden of the earth and, returning, merge here [into Him].  
**23.89.** Above all is this form; [it] is called the formless (*nirakara*) Reality.  
**23.90.** He is called Bhagavan; in the world (?) He has no birth.  
**23.91.** In the fourteen worlds there is the earth (?); there are [also] fourteen Vaikunthas.  
**23.92.** That Reality is the master of all; together with Lakshmi He performs lilas:  
**23.93.** He is called Lakshmi-Narayana, He bears the name Lakshmi-Nrisimha.  
**23.94.** So says the brahmana Divakara, having placed his mind on Nila-kandara (?).[^23-24]  

*Thus [ends], in the Sri Jagannathamrita, [the life of] Sri Ati Bada Jagannatha Dasa Gosain — the twenty-third chapter, called 'Anukramani' (?).*[^23-25]

[^23-1]: Ati Bada Gosain is Jagannatha Dasa (see the note to 1.20). From 23.3 he is the speaker; where his speech ends is not visible on these pages. In 23.3 Divakara links the worship of Jagannatha in Puri with Mahaprabhu. This agrees with the CC: Mahaprabhu beheld Jagannatha daily and took part in the Snana-yatra, the Gundicha-marjana and the Ratha-yatra (Madhya 11–14).
[^23-2]: On the Chaturdha-murti, the four Deities of the altar (Balabhadra, Subhadra, Jagannatha, Sudarshana), see the note to 4.25.
[^23-3]: Judging by her place in the list, the "Lotus-eyed One" is apparently Subhadra. Divakara identifies her with Tripura and Lalita: Lalita-Tripurasundari is a name of the Goddess in the Shakta tradition. This is a Shakta feature of the Odia temple worship of Subhadra. For the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, Subhadra is Krishna's sister, a manifestation of His internal energy; Lalita is the foremost of Sri Radha's principal sakhis, not Tripurasundari. The reading ତ୍ରିପୁରା: printed ତ with a subscript ୍ର and the arc ି, then ପୁ and a sign like ସ୍ୱ (= ରା). In 23.9 the word before ପାର is unread. It is taken as ତିଷ୍ଠି, 'to abide'; perhaps ଚିହ୍ନି ('to recognize') or ତରି ('to cross over').
[^23-4]: The references "In the *Vishnu Purana*" and "In the *Skanda Purana*" (before 23.41) are the author's; we have not checked them. The *mahatmyas* of the Snana-yatra, the Ratha-yatra and Gundicha are told at length in the *Purushottama-kshetra-mahatmya* of the *Skanda Purana*. We know of no such accounts in the *Vishnu Purana*.
[^23-5]: The Snana-yatra is the bathing of the Deities on the full moon of the month of Jyeshtha (cf. VL 8.83 and its note). ଋଷିଙ୍କି (*ṛṣiṅki*, also 23.29) is an archaic dative form, 'to the rishis'. "Jaya, Rama, Krishna" (23.11) is an exclamation on beholding the bathing.
[^23-6]: "The form [turned] to the south" (*dakṣiṇa mūrati*, also 23.20) is a tentative interpretation. By the sense, it is some procession of the Lord after the Snana-yatra. Perhaps it is the turning of the chariots to the south on the way back from Gundicha ("Dakshina-moda" before the Bahuda-yatra). Or perhaps something else, such as the Lord in anavasara. To be checked with a specialist.
[^23-7]: Divakara promises sayujya, liberation by merging with the Lord, as the fruit of darshan. The Gaudiya Vaishnavas reject it: see the note to 5.26 and the *Bhagavata* 3.29.13. In 23.26 and 23.28, by contrast, the fruit of beholding is said to be bhakti.
[^23-8]: Gundicha is the temple north-east of the main temple of Puri, to which the Deities ride on their chariots; the chariot festival is also called the "Gundicha-yatra" (see the note to 7.15; cf. VL 5.75 and its note). According to the CC (Madhya 12–14), Mahaprabhu and the devotees cleansed the Gundicha temple before the Ratha-yatra, and He danced before the chariot. The first two words of 23.24 are blotted. Printed ସ + a blot + ା and ମ + a blot + ା; tentatively taken as ସଦା (?) ମହା, 'always (?) the great'. The last word (ତହିଁ, 'there') is also doubtful. In 23.25 ମାସକ (?) is taken as 'in a month'.
[^23-9]: Ninefold bhakti means hearing, chanting and the other forms (*Bhagavata* 7.5.23–24); see the note to 6.81. In 23.28 ଭକତ (*bhakata*) is taken as *bhakti*: 'ninefold bhakti'. Before ଲଭନ୍ତି in 23.26 stands a bold sign, taken as ସେ ('they').
[^23-10]: ଅଦ୍ୟେ (ଅ = ଆ, *ādye*) is taken as '[even] the Primal One': Brahma (or the Lord) does not know all the virtues of Gundicha. 'First of all' is also possible; cf. ଅଦ୍ୟେ in 7.23–7.24.
[^23-11]: The word before ଉଚ୍ଚାର is unread. Printed କ + ଲ with a subscript (≈ ୍ୟ) + a sign like '8' with a hook. The same sign is in ଲେଶଟି (23.12, 'not a trace'). It is apparently the unidentified sign of 6.61–6.67 and 7.28 (note to 7.28); here it probably reads ଟି. The sense of the half-line is clear: 'whoever utters [the word] "Gundicha"'.
[^23-12]: ଏକାଶୀ (*ekāśī*) means 'eighty-one'. Perhaps the nine kinds of bhakti are meant, each in nine varieties (9 × 9). ମତ (*mata*) is taken as 'kind, sort'. In Gaudiya teaching, the *Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu* (1.2) lists 64 angas of bhakti.
[^23-13]: The reading is uncertain. ତଳରେ ('beneath') is printed as a bold blot + ଳ + ରେ. ଚମ୍ପଇ ('is crushed, pressed down'?) is printed ଚ + ମ with a subscript + ଇ. If the reading is right, Divakara praises one who perishes under the chariot. Cf. VL 5.76, where Sanatana throws himself under the chariot. According to the CC (Antya 4), Mahaprabhu forbade Sanatana to give up his body in this way: it is an act in the mode of ignorance, and Krishna is not attained so, but only by bhajana. ଉଃଶ୍ୱାସ (23.37) is a spelling of *ucchwāsa*, 'breathing in'.
[^23-14]: Literally: "if his forefathers abide in heaven (*swarge*), seeing him they are delivered from hell". The logic is unclear: one expects "if his forefathers are in hell". Perhaps ସ୍ୱର୍ଗେ is used broadly here for 'in the other world'. The reading ସ୍ୱର୍ଗେ is certain. ଯମଲୋକଙ୍କୁ (23.40) means 'the people of Yama', that is, his messengers (yamadutas).
[^23-15]: Indradyumna is the king who, according to the Puranic legend, established the worship of Jagannatha (see the note to 4.91). According to the tradition of the *Skanda Purana*, the Lord promised Indradyumna to come every year to the site of his sacrifices, at Gundicha. "Nine days" (23.43) is the time the Deities stay in the Gundicha temple, from the Ratha-yatra to the Bahuda-yatra (the return procession).
[^23-16]: Printed ଚୌବଶୀ (*caubaśī*; in 23.44 the sign after ଚୌ is blotted). Taken as ଚଉବିଶି, 'twenty-four', with an invisible ି. It may be ଚଉରାଶୀ, 'eighty-four', but the sign clearly resembles ବ, not ସ୍ୱ (= ରା). Pushkara is a famous tirtha (a lake near Ajmer). According to Divakara, all the tirthas and Pushkara abide in Puri (cf. VL 1.2: "You made all the tirthas one").
[^23-17]: The last words of 23.45 and 23.46 are unread. In 23.45 it is printed ଉ + a sign like ତ୍ + ପ + ଖେ (rhyming with ଦେଖେ). It is taken as ଉପେଖେ, 'disregards, casts off'; perhaps ଉତ୍ପାଟେ ('uproots'). In 23.46, before ଦଶଗୁଣ stands ସ + େ + a sign like ହ (ସହେ?). By the sense one expects 'there' or 'at night' (ରାତ୍ରେ?): "as much fruit as comes in a day [elsewhere], here one attains tenfold". Continued on p. 141 (PDF 142).
[^23-18]: 23.48–23.54 (with a reference to the *Padma Purana*): Purushottama-kshetra acts even on those who come unwillingly. The fruit comes to a forced porter taken to Puri by force, though he himself grieves. It also comes to pack animals and to caged birds brought into the kshetra. It comes 'by force', without knowledge or wish (23.51). The fruit is 'the destination of those who perform the agnihotra', that is, the heaven reached by the Vedic sacrificer. Who 'they' are in 23.54 is unclear: the animals or those who bring them. By the sense of 23.51, more likely the unwilling ones themselves. The idea is close to the *Purushottama-kshetra-mahatmya* of the *Skanda Purana*: in Puri all beings are liberated. Gaudiya Vaishnavas acknowledge the power of a holy place (CC, Madhya 17: the animals of Jharikhanda chant the Name). Cf. also the power of namabhasa uttered unintentionally (CC, Antya 3). But their goal is neither heaven nor liberation; it is pure bhakti (cf. the note to 23.18). Readings. ବେଠିଆ (?): the first signs are blotted; *beṭhi* is corvée, a porter without pay. One sees ≈ େ + ବ + ଠି + ଆ. କର is taken as 'levy' or = କରି. କ୍ଷେତ୍ରବରେ (23.48) and କ୍ଷେତ୍ରରେ (23.50): printed ≈ ଷେଶବରେ, ≈ ୈଷଷରେ (see the introductory note). ତୁରିତେ (?) ('at once') rhymes with ହୁଅନ୍ତେ. ଲଦଣ (?) ('load', from ଲଦିବା, 'to load'): the second sign resembles the ଦ of ବଳଦ, not ବ; ଲବଣ ('salt') is also possible. ମୋର (?) (23.53): after ମୋ there is a sign ≈ ର with a stroke.
[^23-19]: Nrisimha speaks to Prahlada (23.55–23.59). The pilgrim to Puri receives bhakti in stages: the ninefold, then the 64 angas, then bhakti 'of eighty-one [kinds]'. On this number see the note to 23.34: perhaps 9 × 9. 'Sixty-four angas' is the number of the angas of bhakti in the *Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu* (1.2). Here, however, they follow the ninefold bhakti as a higher stage, and above them stand the '81 kinds'. Rupa Gosvami has no such ladder. ତୁମ୍ଭର ପର (23.56): ପର is taken as 'supreme'; if it is ପରି with an invisible ି, it means 'bhakti like yours'. ନବଧାକୁ (?) (23.58): after ଧା stands କ with a hook; it may also be କ୍ତ, i.e. 'ନବଧା [ଭ]କ୍ତି' with ଭ omitted. ଏକାଶୀ (23.59): the third sign is ଶୀ with a filled base; it looks the same in ପ୍ରବେଶ (23.50).
[^23-20]: 23.60–23.63 (with a reference to the *Brahma-vaivarta*). One who reviles the Vedas, the Puranas, brahmanas, Vaishnavas, renunciants, chaste women and ascetics destroys this sin by staying in Puri for half a danda (≈ 12 minutes). Gaudiya Vaishnavas teach otherwise. Offence against Vaishnavas is the gravest offence, the 'mad elephant' that ruins the creeper of bhakti (CC, Madhya 19.156). Reviling sadhus and reviling the shastras are the first and third of the ten nama-aparadhas. What frees one from it is not a place but the forgiveness of the offended Vaishnava. Thus Gopala Chapala, who offended Shrivasa, was forgiven only by Shrivasa himself (CC, Adi 17.37–58). 23.61 is not understood. ଅନଭବ (??): printed ଅ + ନ + a sign ≈ ଭ/ଢ + ବ. Options: *an-ābhaba*, '[they make dharma] non-existent'; *parābhaba*, 'insult'; *anubhaba*. କ୍ଷେତ୍ରେତମେ (23.63) is corrected to କ୍ଷେତ୍ରୋତ୍ତମେ, as in 23.67 (ଦଣ୍ଡେ କ୍ଷେତ୍ରୋତ୍ତମେ ଯାଇ). ଦଣ୍ଡାର୍ଦ୍ଧେ (?): printed ଦ + ଣ୍ଡା + େ + a sign with repha.
[^23-21]: The reference 'In the *Sri Bhagavata*': we do not know such words of the Lord to Prahlada in the *Srimad-Bhagavatam*. Perhaps the Odia *Bhagavata* of Jagannatha Dasa or another text is meant; not checked. The thought of 23.65–23.67: one danda (≈ 24 minutes) in Puri equals a hundred years of worship of all the Lord's murtis in the universe. ପ୍ରହଲ୍ଲଦଙ୍କୁ — so Prahlada's name is printed (cf. ପ୍ରହଲାଦେ in 23.55).
[^23-22]: 23.69–23.80 give the Atibadi teaching on Jagannatha. In Him Radha and Krishna are 'one bhava': they see no duality and abide in pure madhurya, without desire for aishvarya (23.69–23.72). The avataras, by contrast, are 'bodies of desire': Rama wished to kill Ravana, Krishna to arrange the rasa with the cowherd women, and so on (23.73–23.78). Jagannatha has no desire: in Radha's bhava He tastes madhurya (23.79–23.80). The Gaudiya Vaishnava position (Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati). Radha and Krishna are one in essence and eternally two in lila; the united form of Radha and Krishna is Sri Chaitanya (CC, Adi 1.5; 4.55–56), while Jagannatha is Krishna Himself (CC, Madhya 13). The avataras descend by Their own will, not by desire as conditioned souls do. Their bodies are sat-cit-ananda (BG 4.6, 4.9). Gaudiyas regard Krishna's rasa-lila as the summit of madhurya, not a 'body of desire' below Jagannatha (CC, Adi 4). Gaudiyas too place madhurya above aishvarya (CC, Adi 4.17–25), but they assign it to Krishna in Vrindavana. Cf. the notes to 4.52, 4.55, 6.7 and 6.44. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati counts the 'ativadi' among the apasampradayas (see the note to 6.90). Unclear readings. ଭାବତ (?) (23.69): printed ଭ + ବ + ତ. ଦ୍ୱୈତ (?) and ଅନ୍ୟସେ (?) (23.70): printed ≈ େ + ଓ + ଦୂ + ତ and ଅନ୍ୟ + େ + ସ. ବସି (?) (23.72). ସଙ୍ଗତି (?) (23.73): the syntax is unclear. ଗୋପଙ୍ଗନା (?) (23.75): taken as ଗୋପାଙ୍ଗନା, 'cowherd women'. ବପୁର (?) (23.78): perhaps ବସ୍ତୁର.
[^23-23]: 23.81–23.93 answer an objection. If Jagannatha, free of desire, is absorbed in bhava, who creates the world, kills the asuras and saves the devotees (Gajendra — 'taking the discus in hand')? The answer: all the avataras, their work done, return and merge 'here', in Jagannatha (23.88). His form is above all; He is called 'the formless Reality' and Bhagavan without birth (23.89–23.90). He is Lakshmi-Narayana and Lakshmi-Nrisimha (23.92–23.93). Gaudiya Vaishnavas also teach that when Krishna descends, all the avataras are within Him (CC, Adi 4.8–12). But for them the source of all avataras is Krishna, Svayam Bhagavan (*Bhagavata* 1.3.28), and Jagannatha is Krishna Himself. They regard the formless Brahman as the effulgence of Krishna's body (*Brahma-samhita* 5.40; CC, Adi 2). The Lord's 'formlessness' can mean only the absence of a material body (cf. the note to 7.40). Cf. VL 1.13: the name of Jagannatha is 'full of Shunya'. Unclear readings. ମିଶଇ (?) (23.82): ମ with the arc of ି. ଚକ୍ରକୁ (?) (23.83): the second sign resembles ଷ/କ୍ଷ. ଏ କେ (?) (23.84). ସଂଶୟୁ ଭଗ୍ନମାନ (??) (23.85): taken as 'breaking the doubt'. ଯେନ (?) (23.87): the rhyme with ପୁଣି suggests ଯେଣି. ହ୍ରାସନ୍ତି (?) (23.88). ଭବରେ (?) (23.90): perhaps ଭାବରେ; after ନାହିଁ stands a separate sign ≈ ଟି (a particle?). 23.91 is poorly understood: ମହୀ (?) — printed ମ + a sign ≈ ହୀ/ଷୀ. How the 'fourteen worlds' and the 'fourteen Vaikunthas' relate to Jagannatha is unclear.
[^23-24]: The author's signature (*bhaṇitā*), as in 23.1. This is the last couplet of the poem. Nila-kandara (the 'Blue Cave') is Puri; see the note to 3.137. The first sign is printed ≈ ଗୀ/ନୀ.
[^23-25]: The colophon of Chapter 23 is set in two lines, like the colophons of Chapters 1–6. Like them, the chapter is called 'Anukramani' (printed ଅନୁକର୍ମଣି; cf. the notes to the colophon of Ch. 1 and to 2.171). Here it reads 'Ati Bada Jagannatha Dasa Gosain' (Ch. 1 has 'Jagannatha Dasa', Ch. 2 'Jagannatha Gosain'). Over the ସ of ଦାସ there is a dot, probably accidental. The chapter number is ତ୍ରୟୋବିଂଶୋଧ୍ୟାୟଃ (no avagraha). There is no colophon of the whole poem, no date, no signature of the author or a scribe: the colophon of Chapter 23 is the last text of the poem. Below it, an ornamental frame holds '(ସମାପ୍ତ)' ('The End'), and at the foot of the page are the printer's details. These are publisher's matter and are not translated.
